# Modifier-coverage report

Generated by `dev/lint_modifier_coverage.py`. Do not edit by hand.

For each base RatePlan filing, cross-references `[modeling].applicable_modifiers` against the actual RatePlanModifier filings in the corpus.

Status glyphs:

- `✓` present and `[modeling].complete = true`
- `~` present but `[modeling].complete = false` (has deferrals)
- `✗` not modeled — no matching filing in the corpus
- `?` filing exists but has no `[modeling]` block (lint cannot judge completeness)

## Per-base detail

### CPAU

#### `e-1` @ 2025-07-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Tier 1 quantity is denominated per day and prorated: Sheet E-1-1 Section D.2, "a level of 15 kWh per day, prorated by Meter reading days of Service... for a 30-day bill, the Tier 1 level would be 450 kWh." cpau:tier-1-bracket carries upperBound 15 against cpau:ms-energy (unitOfMeasure urpx:kWh, no intervalDuration), and no tier name or description states the denomination, so the bound resolves as 15 kWh rather than 15 kWh/day. The per-day denomination IS expressible under v0.5.1 and was validated 2026-09-21: a daily-energy MetricInput whose MetricSpecification carries intervalDuration "P1D" and intervalType urpx:dailyInterval, pointed at by ServiceTiers.referencesMetricInput, conforms against the v0.5.1 shapes. Work, not a blocker.
- The proration arithmetic itself is not expressible: a Tier 1 bound of 15 kWh times the number of meter reading days (Sheet E-1-1 Section D.2, which refers the calculation to Rule and Regulation 11, a document not held in this corpus). ServiceBracketShape is sh:closed in v0.5.1 and lowerBound/upperBound are xsd:decimal literals with no formula or metric reference, so a bound that scales with billing-period length cannot be stated. Same class as urpx-org/urpx#32 (additive baseline allowance), open. Corpus-wide: pge/e-1, pge/e-tou-c, sce/d, sdge/dr and ladwp/r-1a all encode a daily allowance as a bare decimal on an undenominated kWh metric.

#### `e-1-tou` @ 2026-01-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Sheet E-1-TOU-2 Section D.2 (SEASONAL RATE CHANGES) provides that when a billing period includes use in both the Summer and Winter periods, 'usage will be prorated based on the number of days in each seasonal period, and the Charges based on the applicable rates therein' (further discussion at CPAU Rule and Regulation 11, which is not in this corpus). The model carries both Seasons and all six seasonal commodity rates but nothing that allocates a straddling period's usage between them, so a consumer must supply the day-count split itself, and a consumer that instead assigns each metered interval to the season of its timestamp gets a different bill. urpx:prorationMethod (xsd:string, 0..1 on BillingModelShape) can carry the provision declaratively; it is used by no filing in this corpus, and the allocation arithmetic itself has no computable form in URPX v0.5.1.
- Section A excludes Net Energy Metered customers ("This Rate Schedule is not available to Net Energy Metered (NEM) customers and is provided at the sole discretion of CPAU"). The model quotes the sentence in a description; cpau:er-residential-tou carries four ComparisonNodes (residential kind, single-family, separately metered, AMI) and no NEM predicate. Expressible: cpau:e-nse-1-2025-09-01-cn-nem-successor is a notEqualsOperator NEM test in this same corpus.

| Modifier | Status | Filing |
|---|---|---|
| `e-hra` | ✓ present-complete | `us/ca/cpau/electricity/e-hra/versions/2023-01-01` |
| `e-nse-1` | ~ present-incomplete | `us/ca/cpau/electricity/e-nse-1/versions/2026-07-01` |

#### `e-14` @ 2025-07-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Section D.2 (Sheet E-14-2) gates the $2.50 per month switching surcharge twice, and the model prices it unconditionally. CPAU switches at no charge where each separately switched circuit carries at least 10 kW of lamp load, counting all lamps on the circuit whether or not served under E-14, and the charge is waived again where the switching installation is made for CPAU's convenience. The $2.50 is modeled (cpau:e14-price-switching-surcharge) but neither gate is: the 10 kW threshold survives only in the urpx:name of cpau:e14-mi-switching-circuit-count and the convenience waiver only in the urpx:description of cpau:e14-pd-switching-surcharge, so the Price carries no applicability condition and the $2.50 falls on every separately switched circuit the metric counts. Expressible under v0.5.1 as urpx:hasApplicabilityRule on the Price or the PriceDefinition with CustomOperandNode predicates; a candidate carrying both gates conforms against the v0.5.1 shapes, and CPAU E-3 already hangs hasApplicabilityGroup off a PriceDefinition. This is work, not a blocker.
- Section D.5 interim rate for lamps not represented on this Rate Schedule: where a customer installs an unlisted lamp rating, CPAU prepares an interim rate from its own estimated costs and bills at it until the rating is added, so the published price list is not exhaustive. Prose only in the model.

#### `e-16` @ 2025-09-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Section C.2 energy charge, Sheet E-16-1: the schedule prices all kWh by reference rather than by amount, "using Electric Rate Schedule E2 plus all applicable riders". This is the schedule's principal charge and the model carries no energy Price at all, so it resolves nothing per kWh. Unbuilt work, not a blocker: a urpx:Price carrying urpx:multiplier with urpx:basedOnPrice naming the cpau:e-2 energy Prices expresses it. Schedule E-2 Sheet E-2-1 states its energy charge as three unbundled components per season (0.15075 + 0.10806 + 0.00604 = 0.26485/kWh summer, 0.09334 + 0.07352 + 0.00604 = 0.17290/kWh winter as of 2025-07-01), so this needs either three E-16 Prices each naming one component or a per-season urpx:PriceSummary added to cpau:e-2. urpx:PriceShape constrains basedOnPrice with sh:or of sh:class urpx:Price and sh:class urpx:PriceSummary, so the referent must be typed in the validated graph; validate E-16 in union with cpau:e-2, which is what dev/shacl.sh already does (its gate unit is a merged graph per utility). The "plus all applicable riders" half is already carried: cpau:e-hra and cpau:e-eec-1 both name cpau:e-16 in urpx:applicableToRatePlan.
- Section E miscellaneous rates, Sheet E-16-3, six stated amounts and none in the model: electric conduit usage, exclusive use, at $1.94 per foot per year (E.1.A); utility pole attachments at $47.60, $95.20 and $142.80 per pole per year for one, two and three feet of usable space (E.3.A through E.3.C); and mounting of communication equipment including distributed antenna systems at $329.44 per pole per year (E.5). Unbuilt work, not a blocker, and never a vocabulary limit: chargeType urpx:rentalFee, chargeCategory urpx:infrastructure, a MetricSpecification with unitOfMeasure urpx:count for the per-pole fees and urpx:unitOther for linear feet (the 55 UnitOfMeasureValue members contain no linear-foot unit), and a CalculationMethod with intervalDuration "P1Y" all validate. The reason recorded earlier, that these are annual rather than monthly, does not hold: urpx:intervalDuration is xsd:duration. Construction note: urpx-shacl:PriceDefinitionShape requires urpx:referencesLedger at minCount 1 and maxCount 1 with sh:class urpx:Ledger, so each new PriceDefinition needs a Ledger; LedgerTypeValue has eight members and none is a natural fit for an infrastructure license fee, though urpx:calculatedLedger validates.
- Two Section E line items state no amount and cannot be carried as Prices. Sheet E-16-3 prices the conduit processing fee at "Actual Cost" (E.2) and the pole-attachment processing fee at "C-1 rate - 1 hr" (E.4). urpx-shacl:PriceShape requires exactly one of amount, basePrice, basedOnPrice, currentPrice or unitPrice, so "Actual Cost" has no value to carry. E.4 is a source gap rather than a vocabulary gap: the one-hour quantity is stated, but no CPAU Schedule C-1 is committed to this corpus to price it against, so there is nothing for urpx:basedOnPrice to name.
- Section F CPI escalation, Sheet E-16-3: the Section E.1 through E.5 fees are adjusted annually by the Consumer Price Index for All Urban Consumers for the San Francisco-Oakland-San Jose MSA, measured from December of the base year in which the master license agreement is signed to December of a subsequent base year. Nothing in URPX computes an escalation. The clause is recordable declaratively the way cpau:e-hra records its annual Council reset, with urpx:DynamicPricing at dynamicPricingType urpx:indexBasedPricing and a urpx:PriceUpdateRule carrying the formula as free text; UpdateFrequencyValue has no annual member and no consumer reads any of it. Record it as a datum, not as a calculation.
- The Customer Charge carries the wrong charge type. Sheet E-16-1 C.1 prices a Customer Charge of $10.96 per month and C.3 separately sets the minimum monthly charge equal to it. The single PriceDefinition is typed chargeType urpx:minimumBill alone, so a consumer grouping this filing by charge type sees a minimum bill and no customer charge. urpx:customerCharge is one of the six ChargeTypeValue members added since v0.2.1 (62 members there, 68 at v0.5.1, and the term returns zero hits in the v0.2.1 ontology and shapes), so this is the one item on this filing the migration genuinely unblocked. PriceDefinitionShape puts no maxCount on urpx:chargeType and urpx:primaryChargeType exists, so carrying both urpx:customerCharge and urpx:minimumBill with primaryChargeType urpx:customerCharge validates. The C.3 floor itself needs no further structure: the customer charge is billed unconditionally every month, so the bill cannot fall below it.

| Modifier | Status | Filing |
|---|---|---|
| `e-eec-1` | ~ present-incomplete | `us/ca/cpau/electricity/e-eec-1/versions/2026-07-01` |
| `e-hra` | ✓ present-complete | `us/ca/cpau/electricity/e-hra/versions/2023-01-01` |

#### `e-2` @ 2025-07-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Section D.3 Maximum Demand Meter is unmodeled: a demand meter is installed whenever monthly use exceeds 8,000 kWh for three consecutive months, and stays in service until monthly use falls below 6,000 kWh for twelve consecutive months. Neither threshold nor the consecutive-month counts appear anywhere in the JSON-LD; the only "Demand" strings are the Non-Demand-metered eligibility values.

#### `e-3` @ 2007-07-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Section C.3 (Sheet E-3-2) prices a market-based buyback for facilities rated above 1 MW on customer request, and nothing of it is in the model: Firm $2.00 to $10.00 per kW-month and $0.03 to $0.20 per kWh, Non-Firm $0.03 to $0.20 per kWh with no capacity component, the rate itself being the City's average cost of electricity at the PG&E City Gate for energy and capacity purchased at the first of the month together with daily market prices for that month. Expressible under v0.5.1 and therefore unbuilt work rather than a blocker: a Price carrying urpx:hasDynamicPricing (dynamicPricingType urpx:indexBasedPricing, priceSource urpx:wholesaleMarketSource) with urpx:priceFloor and urpx:priceCeiling, plus a scalar such as urpx:currentPrice to satisfy PriceShape's sh:xone; that candidate was built into this filing's own document and conforms under the v0.5.1 shapes. Two caveats to record with it: the floor and ceiling would carry the band declaratively, with the scalar carrying the price; and the ontology comments define these two as a clamp preventing a computed price from crossing a threshold, which is not obviously the same thing as a published tariff band.
- Special Note 8 (Sheet E-3-3) requires that when a billing period falls partly in the summer period and partly in the winter period, total kWh usage, kW demand and the applicable rates be prorated between the two seasons by the ratio of the number of days in each. Not modeled. The only v0.5.1 vocabulary for this is urpx:prorationMethod, an xsd:string on BillingModel whose stated purpose is service starting or ending mid-period; it would carry the provision as prose, is read by no consumer, and is used by no filing in the corpus. Per-instant appliesInSeason already gives an interval consumer the right rate, so the unmodeled case is a C.1 monthly total that straddles April 30 or October 31.
- Special Note 2 (Sheet E-3-2) requires the quantity of power delivered to the utility to be adjusted for the effect of losses and for administrative costs associated with the facility, and states no factor. A source gap rather than a vocabulary gap: no number is published in this schedule, and Special Note 4 places all interconnection and sales terms in the customer's service contract. Section D likewise sets transmission rates site by site on a case by case basis with no published number, and Special Note 5 reserves a standby customer charge to that same service contract; none of the three is missing from the model so much as unpriced by the schedule.

#### `e-4` @ 2025-07-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Section D.5, Sheets E-4-2 and E-4-3: a 2.5 percent Primary Voltage Discount where delivery is made at the same voltage as the supply line and that line voltage is above 2 kV. Not modeled; the filing carries no urpx:multiplier, no urpx:basedOnPrice and no urpx:PriceSummary. Expressible at v0.5.1: a PriceSummary under the existing PriceSet naming the component Prices, plus a Price carrying multiplier -0.025 and basedOnPrice naming that summary, conforms. Two cautions before building. The PDF never states which charges the 2.5 percent applies to (D.5 gives only the rate; D.1's "adjusted for any applicable discounts, surcharges, and/or taxes" is the closest thing to a base and is not a statement), so the base is a source ambiguity; CPAU Rule and Regulation 11, which might settle it, is not committed to this corpus. And a PriceSummary sums the Prices it references, while the charges here are in three units ($/kWh, $/kW and $/month), so each unit needs its own summary and its own discount Price. The above-2-kV condition can only be a urpx:ApplicabilityRule, which turns on a customer attribute this filing carries no value for, so the discount Price would state the condition without distinguishing which customers it reaches.
- Section D.6.b, Sheet E-4-3: Standby Charge per kW of Reserved Capacity for customers with a non-utility generation source interconnected behind the City's revenue meter. Summer $8.50 commodity plus $38.08 distribution, total $46.58; winter $0.00 plus $23.49, total $23.49. Not modeled at all: no reserved-capacity MetricInput, no Ledger, no PriceDefinition, no Price. Expressible at v0.5.1: urpx:standbyCharge is a ChargeTypeValue member and is one of the terms the migration supplied (zero hits at v0.2.1, present at v0.5.1), and four seasonal Prices carrying it on a kW MetricInput conform. Two things to settle first. Reserved Capacity is a contracted quantity rather than a measured one and v0.5.1 has no standard name for it: MeasurementTypeValue has 14 members and none is capacity, while urpx:profileMetricProperty is an open IRI slot with no controlled vocabulary. And whether a customer has an interconnected non-utility generation source is a selection over the plan space rather than a measured quantity, so host the choice on urpx:hasProfileAlternatives, which offers standby and non-standby service as alternatives to select between, rather than on an ApplicabilityRule, which would attach a condition to the standby Prices and offer nothing in their place.
- Section D.3, Sheet E-4-2: for customers with Thermal Energy Storage the Billing Demand is the Maximum Demand between noon and 6 PM on weekdays rather than at all hours. Not modeled; the four demand PriceDefinitions carry only the all-hours basis. The condition cannot live inside the urpx:CalculationMethod, whose shape is sh:closed over nine properties (aggregationMethod, calculatedBasedOn, calculationFormula, description, intervalAlignment, intervalDuration, intervalSelection, intervalType, name) with no condition path. The restructuring does work and validates: a noon-to-18:00 urpx:TimeBracket with dayType urpx:weekdayDays inside a urpx:TOUPeriod on the BillingModel, plus a second set of demand PriceDefinitions at the same unit prices scoped to it. Build it on urpx:hasProfileAlternatives rather than an ApplicabilityRule gate: whether a customer has Thermal Energy Storage is a selection over the plan space, and the two demand bases are alternatives to choose between rather than one basis with a condition attached to it.
- Section D.6.d, Sheets E-4-3 and E-4-4: where the customer's Maximum Demand occurs while one or more non-utility generators are not operating, the Maximum Demand is reduced by the sum of those generators' Maximum Generation, "but in no event shall the Customer's Maximum Demand be reduced below zero"; and under D.6.d(2), if a generator does not operate for an entire billing cycle the standby charge does not apply and the credit is not given. Not expressible at v0.5.1, and the reason is not an absence of arithmetic vocabulary. urpx:OperationNode exists, documented as "Expression node for nested arithmetic or derived comparison preparation", and urpx:OperatorValue has 17 members including urpx:subtractOperator, urpx:addOperator, urpx:multiplyOperator and urpx:divideOperator. What is missing is a path from that arithmetic to a billed quantity: OperationNode is reachable only as leftOperand or rightOperand of a ComparisonNode or another OperationNode, a ConditionExpression reaches it only through rootNode, and the only four shapes hosting urpx:hasConditionExpression are ApplicabilityGroupShape, ApplicabilityRuleShape, EligibilityRuleShape and ProfileAlternativeShape, every one of them a boolean gate. Nothing floors or clamps a determinant either: urpx:priceFloor and urpx:priceCeiling both carry rdfs:domain urpx:DynamicPricing and bound a price, not a demand quantity. urpx:CalculationMethod offers only urpx:calculatedBasedOn, which names source MetricInputs and carries no operator, and free-text urpx:calculationFormula, which no consumer reads. Recordable today only as prose. urpx-org/urpx PR #76, open, proposes urpx:ValueExpression on CalculationMethod with urpx:maxOperator and urpx:minOperator, which would carry the subtraction and its floor at zero into a calculated quantity.
- Section D.6.e(1), Sheet E-4-4: the standby charge does not apply to backup generators designed to operate only during an interruption in utility service and not used to offset customer electricity purchases. The fact cannot be stated as a property of a modeled DER: urpx-shacl:DistributedEnergyResourceShape is sh:closed over exactly 12 properties (connectorType, derType, evChargingCapacity, interconnectionStatus, inverterType, panelOrientation, powerRating, roundTripEfficiency, smartChargingEnabled, solarCapacity, storageCapacity, windCapacity) and none states an operating mode. The exemption is gateable as an opaque urpx:CustomOperandNode predicate, the same route D.6.e(2)'s Public Utilities Code 2827(b)(4) test would take, but the predicate is a free-text property name: it records which attribute decides the exemption and carries neither a value for it nor a definition of it. D.6.e(3), Utilities Director discretion, is not modelable.
- Section D.2, Sheet E-4-2: "When the billing period includes use both in the Summer and the Winter Periods, the usage will be prorated based on the number of days in each seasonal period, and the charges based on the applicable rates therein." This is not the month-granular season model the filing already carries: D.2 prorates by day count, so an October 20 to November 20 bill splits usage by days in each season rather than by which season each metered interval fell in. v0.5.1 has a nominal home, urpx:prorationMethod on BillingModelShape, but it is a bare xsd:string with no controlled vocabulary (ProrationMethodValueShape is empty), its skos:note scopes it to service starting or ending mid-period rather than to a season boundary, and no filing in this corpus uses it. Recordable as prose only. CPAU Rule and Regulation 11, to which D.2 refers the reader, is not committed to this corpus.

#### `e-4-tou` @ 2025-07-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Section D.5 Primary Voltage Discount (Sheet E-4-TOU-3): the schedule prices a 2.5 percent discount where delivery is made at the same voltage as the supply line and that line voltage is above 2 kV. No Price carries it. Expressible under URPX v0.5.1 and verified by SHACL validation: a Price with multiplier -0.025 and basedOnPrice naming a PriceSummary, gated by an ApplicabilityRule on a custom serviceLineVoltage_kV predicate. Two things to settle before building it. The PDF never says what the 2.5 percent applies to, and a PriceSummary sums member unitPrice values with no unit awareness, so this needs one discount Price per unit-homogeneous subtotal ($/kWh, $/kW, $/month) rather than one over the whole bill.
- Section D.7 Standby Charge (Sheet E-4-TOU-4): the schedule prices a charge per kW of Reserved Capacity, Summer $8.50 Commodity plus $38.08 Distribution equals $46.58, Winter $0.00 plus $23.49 equals $23.49, for customers with a non-utility generation source interconnected behind the revenue meter (D.7.a). No Price carries it. Expressible under URPX v0.5.1 and verified by SHACL validation: chargeType urpx:standbyCharge on a per-kW PriceDefinition with its own Reserved Capacity MetricInput and ledger, seasonal via appliesInSeason, gated by an ApplicabilityRule.
- Section D.7.d Maximum Demand Credit (Sheet E-4-TOU-4): where the customer's Maximum Demand occurs while one or more non-utility generators are not operating, the billing Maximum Demand is reduced by the sum of those generators' Maximum Generation but never below zero; and if a generator does not operate for an entire billing cycle, neither the standby charge nor this credit applies. Not expressible under URPX v0.5.1. CalculationMethodShape is sh:closed over nine properties with no condition path, so a conditional adjustment to a billing determinant has nowhere to live but the free-text calculationFormula, and DistributedEnergyResourceShape is sh:closed over twelve properties with no operating-mode property. urpx-org/urpx PR #76, open, proposes urpx:ValueExpression on CalculationMethod with urpx:maxOperator and urpx:minOperator, which would carry the subtraction and its floor at zero into a calculated quantity.
- Section D.7.e standby exemptions (Sheets E-4-TOU-4 and E-4-TOU-5): the standby charge does not apply to backup generators designed to operate only during an interruption of utility service and not used to offset customer purchases, nor to a customer meeting the definition of an Eligible Customer-generator under California Public Utilities Code Section 2827(b)(4). The backup-only exemption is not expressible under v0.5.1 for the same reason as D.7.d: DistributedEnergyResourceShape carries no operating-mode or offsetting-use property. The 2827(b)(4) exemption can ride as a CustomOperandNode predicate inside the standby ApplicabilityRule once the standby charge itself is built.

#### `e-7` @ 2025-07-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Section D.6 (sheet E-7-3) offers a 2.8 percent discount where delivery is made at the same voltage as the supplying line and that line voltage is above 2 kV. Not modeled; the file carries no multiplier, basedOnPrice or PriceSummary. Expressible under v0.5.1: one urpx:PriceSummary per unit-homogeneous group under urpx:hasPriceSummary on the PriceSet, plus a Price carrying urpx:multiplier -0.028 and urpx:basedOnPrice naming that summary, gated by an ApplicabilityRule whose ObjectOperandNode reads urpx:voltageLevel = urpx:primaryVoltage. A candidate of that shape SHACL-conforms against v0.5.1 (verified 2026-09-21). Unbuilt because the primary-service restriction would live entirely in that ApplicabilityRule: the multiplier Price itself carries no restriction, so a consumer that does not evaluate the rule discounts every E-7 bill rather than only service taken at primary voltage. This is work, not a blocker.
- Section D.7.b (sheet E-7-3) prices a Standby Charge per kW of Reserved Capacity for customers with a non-utility generation source interconnected behind the City's revenue meter (D.7.a): summer $9.25 commodity + $31.54 distribution = $40.79/kW, winter $0.00 + $27.75 = $27.75/kW. Not modeled. Expressible under v0.5.1: urpx:standbyCharge is a native ChargeTypeValue defined as a charge for standby or backup service to customers with on-site generation, and a reserved-capacity MetricInput takes the same kW shape as the existing demand input. A candidate SHACL-conforms against v0.5.1 (verified 2026-09-21). Unbuilt for the same reason as D.6: the D.7.a eligibility would live entirely in an applicability condition on the charge, so a consumer that does not evaluate it adds $40.79/kW to every E-7 bill. This is work, not a blocker.
- Section D.7.d.(1) (sheet E-7-4) reduces the Maximum Demand by the sum of the Maximum Generation of any non-utility generators that were not operating when the peak occurred, floored at zero, which changes the determinant the four demand Prices multiply. Not modeled, and not expressible in computable form under v0.5.1. Be precise about why, because the neighboring claim is false: urpx:OperationNode exists and urpx:subtractOperator is one of the 17 urpx:OperatorValue individuals, so URPX does carry subtraction. What is missing is a path from that arithmetic to a billing determinant. OperationNode is reachable only as an operand inside a ConditionExpression, which yields a boolean; CalculationMethodShape is sh:closed over exactly nine properties (aggregationMethod, calculatedBasedOn, calculationFormula, description, intervalAlignment, intervalDuration, intervalSelection, intervalType, name) with no offset or netting path; AggregationMethodValue has exactly five members, average, maximum, median, minimum and sum, none of which nets one quantity against another; and nothing anywhere floors a result at zero. All verified against the v0.5.1 ontology and SHACL at urpx-org/urpx abdfb521. Same standard-level gap as greater-of minimum-charge arithmetic. urpx-org/urpx PR #76, open, proposes urpx:ValueExpression on CalculationMethod with urpx:maxOperator and urpx:minOperator, which would carry the subtraction and its floor at zero into a calculated quantity.
- Section D.4 (sheet E-7-2) sets the Billing Demand for Thermal Energy Storage customers to the Maximum Demand between noon and 6 pm on weekdays rather than the month's actual Maximum Demand. Not modeled; the model prices the unrestricted monthly maximum for every customer, so a TES customer resolves too high. Expressible under v0.5.1 as a urpx:TOUPeriod carrying a TimeBracket with startTime 12:00:00, endTime 18:00:00 and dayType urpx:weekdayDays on the demand PriceDefinitions, the shape sibling cpau:e-7-tou already ships. The TES customer gate has the same inert-at-0.25.0 problem as D.6 and D.7.b.
- Section D.3 (sheet E-7-2, 'Request for Service') lets a qualifying customer take service under this schedule for more than one Account or Meter where the Accounts cover contiguous parcels with no intervening public right-of-way and share a common billing address, which changes the denominator of both the 1,000 kW eligibility test and the monthly demand maximum. Not modeled: SPARQL over the v0.5.1 ontology returns no site, account or parcel class a demand aggregation could be scoped to, and urpx:AccountServiceAgreement's own skos:note states that service account and agreement data lives in customer data, not URPX. The model prices a single account. (Previously recorded here as 'C.3'; Section C has no subsection 3.)

Not modeled. Expressible under v0.5.1: urpx:standbyCharge is a native ChargeTypeValue defined as a charge for standby or backup service to customers with on-site generation, and a reserved-capacity MetricInput takes the same kW shape as the existing demand input. A candidate SHACL-conforms against v0.5.1 (verified 2026-09-21). Unbuilt for the same reason as D.6: the D.7.a eligibility would live entirely in an applicability condition on the charge, so a consumer that does not evaluate it adds $42.61/kW to every E-7 bill. This is work, not a blocker.
- Section D.7.d.(1) (sheet E-7-4) reduces the Maximum Demand by the sum of the Maximum Generation of any non-utility generators that were not operating when the peak occurred, floored at zero, which changes the determinant the four demand Prices multiply. Not modeled, and not expressible in computable form under v0.5.1. Be precise about why, because the neighboring claim is false: urpx:OperationNode exists and urpx:subtractOperator is one of the 17 urpx:OperatorValue individuals, so URPX does carry subtraction. What is missing is a path from that arithmetic to a billing determinant. OperationNode is reachable only as an operand inside a ConditionExpression, which yields a boolean; CalculationMethodShape is sh:closed over exactly nine properties (aggregationMethod, calculatedBasedOn, calculationFormula, description, intervalAlignment, intervalDuration, intervalSelection, intervalType, name) with no offset or netting path; AggregationMethodValue has exactly five members, average, maximum, median, minimum and sum, none of which nets one quantity against another; and nothing anywhere floors a result at zero. All verified against the v0.5.1 ontology and SHACL at urpx-org/urpx abdfb521. Same standard-level gap as greater-of minimum-charge arithmetic. urpx-org/urpx PR #76, open, proposes urpx:ValueExpression on CalculationMethod with urpx:maxOperator and urpx:minOperator, which would carry the subtraction and its floor at zero into a calculated quantity.
- Section D.4 (sheet E-7-2) sets the Billing Demand for Thermal Energy Storage customers to the Maximum Demand between noon and 6 pm on weekdays rather than the month's actual Maximum Demand. Not modeled; the model prices the unrestricted monthly maximum for every customer, so a TES customer resolves too high. Expressible under v0.5.1 as a urpx:TOUPeriod carrying a TimeBracket with startTime 12:00:00, endTime 18:00:00 and dayType urpx:weekdayDays on the demand PriceDefinitions, the shape sibling cpau:e-7-tou already ships. The TES customer gate has the same inert-at-0.25.0 problem as D.6 and D.7.b.
- Section D.3 (sheet E-7-2, 'Request for Service') lets a qualifying customer take service under this schedule for more than one Account or Meter where the Accounts cover contiguous parcels with no intervening public right-of-way and share a common billing address, which changes the denominator of both the 1,000 kW eligibility test and the monthly demand maximum. Not modeled: SPARQL over the v0.5.1 ontology returns no site, account or parcel class a demand aggregation could be scoped to, and urpx:AccountServiceAgreement's own skos:note states that service account and agreement data lives in customer data, not URPX. The model prices a single account. (Previously recorded here as 'C.3'; Section C has no subsection 3.)

#### `e-7-tou` @ 2025-07-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Section D.6, Sheet E-7-TOU-3: Primary Voltage Discount of 2.8 percent where delivery is made at the same voltage as the supplying line and that line is above 2 kV. Not modeled; no Price in this filing carries urpx:multiplier. Expressible at v0.5.1 as a Price with multiplier -0.028 and basedOnPrice naming a urpx:PriceSummary, gated by an ObjectOperandNode with conditionProperty urpx:voltageLevel and referencesValue urpx:primaryVoltage; urpx:voltageLevel's own skos:note sanctions that route. Two things to settle first. The schedule never states the base: D.1 implies the whole Section C bill, and Section C prices in three units, so the base needs three unit-homogeneous PriceSummaries ($/kWh, $/kW, $/month) rather than one summary summing across units. And the discount's applicability would rest entirely on that ConditionExpression gate: nothing on the Price itself distinguishes a primary-voltage customer, so a consumer that does not evaluate the gate discounts every customer's bill.
- Section D.7.b, Sheet E-7-TOU-4: Standby Charge per kW of Reserved Capacity, summer $9.25 commodity plus $31.54 distribution equals $40.79, winter $0.00 plus $27.75 equals $27.75, applicable under D.7.a to customers with a non-utility generation source behind the revenue meter. Not modeled; urpx:standbyCharge has zero uses anywhere in this corpus. Expressible at v0.5.1, where urpx:standbyCharge is one of 68 ChargeTypeValue members and is new since v0.2.1. Before building, settle the denomination: the assessor's candidate used urpx:virtualMetricInput, whose v0.5.1 skos:definition is "Metric input for virtual net metering" and is the wrong term here, and urpx:standbyCharge's own skos:note reads "Distinct from urpx:capacityCharge (reserved supply capacity)", which is directly on point for a charge priced per kW of Reserved Capacity. MeasurementTypeValue has 14 members and none is capacity. Same consumer caveat as D.6: the applicability gate is inert at the pin, so the charge would bill to customers with no generator.
- Section D.7.d(1), Sheet E-7-TOU-4: where Maximum Demand occurs while one or more non-utility generators are not operating, Maximum Demand is reduced by the sum of those generators' Maximum Generation, floored at zero (D.7.d(2) waives both charge and credit if the generator does not operate for an entire billing cycle). Not expressible at v0.5.1. Arithmetic vocabulary does exist (urpx:OperationNode, and 17 OperatorValue members including urpx:subtractOperator), but it is reachable only inside a ConditionExpression, whose only four hosts are ApplicabilityGroupShape, ApplicabilityRuleShape, EligibilityRuleShape and ProfileAlternativeShape, all boolean gates; nothing floors or clamps a billing determinant, since urpx:priceFloor and urpx:priceCeiling are rdfs:domain urpx:DynamicPricing. urpx-shacl:CalculationMethodShape is sh:closed over nine properties with no condition path, and its free-text calculationFormula is read by no consumer. urpx-org/urpx PR #76, open, proposes urpx:ValueExpression on CalculationMethod with urpx:maxOperator and urpx:minOperator, which would carry the subtraction and its floor at zero into a calculated quantity.
- Section D.7.e(1), Sheet E-7-TOU-5: the standby charge does not apply to backup generators designed to operate only during a service interruption and not used to offset customer purchases. What is not expressible is the backup-only fact as a property of a modeled DER: urpx-shacl:DistributedEnergyResourceShape is sh:closed over exactly 12 properties and none states an operating mode. The exemption itself is gateable as a urpx:CustomOperandNode predicate on the standby charge, the same route D.7.e(2)'s Public Utilities Code 2827(b)(4) test would take and the route this filing already uses for six CustomOperandNodes in its EligibilityRule, so it is half buildable rather than blocked outright. Such a predicate names its test in a free-text urpx:customConditionProperty, as those six do, so it carries the exemption declaratively and no ontology term states it. D.7.e(3), Utilities Director discretion, is not modelable.
- Section D.3, Sheet E-7-TOU-3: a qualifying customer may take service for more than one Account or Meter where the Accounts cover contiguous parcels with no intervening public right-of-way and share a common billing address. This makes both the Section A 1,000 kW threshold (stated per site) and the demand determinants site-level rather than account-level, so it bears on pricing and not only on enrollment. Not expressible at v0.5.1: the ontology's own skos:note on urpx:AccountServiceAgreement says "Service account and agreement data lives in customer data, not URPX." A MetricInput with metricInputType urpx:aggregatedMetricInput plus a CalculationMethod carrying aggregationMethod urpx:sum and a prose description validates and declares the intent, but it names no accounts. The filing prices a single account.

| Modifier | Status | Filing |
|---|---|---|
| `e-7-g` | ~ present-incomplete | `us/ca/cpau/electricity/e-7-g/versions/2026-07-01` |
| `e-hra` | ✓ present-complete | `us/ca/cpau/electricity/e-hra/versions/2023-01-01` |

### GPC

#### `flat` @ 2024-05-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- FlatBill Monthly Amount. The sheet bills each premises a fixed monthly amount computed from that premises' expected usage, Schedule R's charges with ECCR, DSM-R, FCR and the Municipal Franchise Fee, and a Risk Adder of up to 10 percent, none of which it publishes for any premises. The JSON-LD defines the amount as a usage-independent monthly PriceDefinition and carries no Price for it, so a bill on this filing alone has no charge; the formula is in the version description only.
- Offer conditions. The sheet limits FlatBill to premises with at least 12 months of metered usage history and customers in good financial standing, and makes no offer where the monthly calculated amount is under $50. The EligibilityRule carries residential customer kind; these conditions are in its description only.
- Early withdrawal. A customer who does not complete the 12-month contract may be required to pay the excess of the Residential Service bill for the electricity actually used over the FlatBill amounts billed, with no refund where it is less. No Price carries it.
- Return to Residential Service. Where a customer's monthly usage is 50 percent greater than the Expected Monthly kWh adjusted for the current year's actual weather, the Company may return the customer to Residential Service for the rest of the contract, under the same withdrawal terms. Nothing here carries it.

| Modifier | Status | Filing |
|---|---|---|
| `iqd` | ~ present-incomplete | `us/ga/gpc/electricity/iqd/versions/2024-05-01` |

#### `pbd` @ 2024-05-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Pay by Day Daily Price. The sheet bills each premises a fixed daily price computed from the customer's expected usage, Schedule PPS's charges with ECCR, DSM-R, FCR, the Municipal Franchise Fee and applicable taxes, and a Risk Adder of up to 10 percent, none of which it publishes for any premises. The JSON-LD defines the price as a usage-independent daily PriceDefinition and carries no Price for it, so a bill on this filing alone has no charge; the formula is in the version description only.
- Offer conditions. The sheet limits Pay by Day to customers who have been in their current residence and had their electricity priced on a residential tariff over the previous 12 months, and requires AMI with reconnect/disconnect functionality and acceptable network coverage. The EligibilityRule carries residential customer kind; these conditions are in its description only.
- Pre-payment terms. The sheet requires a $40.00 minimum positive balance to connect, disconnects service when the balance reaches $0.00, requires a minimum payment covering usage up to disconnection, any reconnect fees under Rule F3 and a $5.00 minimum pre-payment to reconnect, and adds any Account Establishment Charge under Rule E2 before connection. None of these is a price per unit of service and none is carried.
- Early withdrawal. A customer who leaves before the end of the 12-month contract may be required to pay the excess of the Pre-Pay Service bill for the electricity actually used over the Pay by Day amounts billed, with no refund where it is less. No Price carries it.
- Return to the traditional tariff. Where a customer's monthly usage, excluding the effects of weather, is 50 percent greater than in the same month of the previous year, the Company may return the customer to the traditional tariff for the rest of the contract, under the same withdrawal terms. Nothing here carries it.

#### `pps` @ 2026-06-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Fuel seasons by billing month. FCR-27 prices June through September and October through May, which Schedule R's bills show is read by the billing month a bill is rendered in. The JSON-LD gates the fuel PriceDefinitions on calendar months, as PPS-11's own energy seasons are, so usage in the weeks a billing period runs ahead of its billing month takes the other season's fuel price. No PPS bill was available to show how a pre-paid account's billing month is set.
- Municipal Franchise Fee base. MFF-11 applies to the total revenues of each bill, which include the ECCR-15 and DSM-R-16 amounts. Its multiplier Prices here are based on the energy-and-fuel subtotal and on the Basic Service Charge Price, not on the ECCR and DSM-R multiplier Prices, so the franchise fee on those two amounts, about 0.44 percent of the base bill inside city limits, is not carried.
- Minimum Bill. The sheet sets it at the Basic Service Charge, while service is connected and for up to ten days after disconnection, plus ECCR, DSM-R and the Municipal Franchise Fee, and no Price carries it. Every energy and fuel price here is positive, so a bill on these prices alone is never below it while service is connected; the ten days of Basic Service Charge after a disconnection are not carried.
- Pre-payment terms and Term of Contract. The sheet requires a $40.00 minimum positive balance to connect, disconnects service when the balance reaches $0.00, requires a minimum payment covering usage up to disconnection, any reconnect fees under Rule F3 and a $5.00 minimum pre-payment to reconnect, adds any Account Establishment Charge under Rule E2 before connection, and sets a term that "varies based on pre-paid amount and customer electric consumption". None of these is a price per unit of service and none is carried.
- Fuel Cost Recovery voltage levels. FCR-27 also prices Primary Distribution and Transmission customers. Only the Secondary Distribution rates are carried, as Prices with no condition, so they apply to every customer on the schedule.

#### `r` @ 2026-06-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Seasons by billing month. R-31 and FCR-27 choose summer or winter by the billing month a bill is rendered in, June through September summer and October through May winter. The JSON-LD gates the energy and fuel PriceDefinitions on calendar months instead, so usage in the weeks a billing period runs ahead of its billing month takes the other season's prices: a bill read in early June bills its May usage at summer prices, and one read in early October bills its September usage at winter prices, where these prices give the opposite.
- Municipal Franchise Fee base. MFF-11 applies to the total revenues of each bill, which include the ECCR-15 and DSM-R-16 amounts. Its multiplier Prices here are based on the energy-and-fuel subtotal and on the Basic Service Charge Price, not on the ECCR and DSM-R multiplier Prices, so the franchise fee on those two amounts, about 0.44 percent of the base bill inside city limits, is not carried.
- Minimum Monthly Bill. The sheet sets it at the Basic Service Charge plus ECCR, DSM-R and the Municipal Franchise Fee, and no Price carries it. Every energy and fuel price here is positive, so a bill on these prices alone is never below it.
- Multiple Service (R-31-M). Where two or more dwelling units share one meter, the sheet multiplies the Basic Service Charge and each kWh block by the number of units. The JSON-LD carries the single-unit charge and blocks.
- Budget Billing Option and Term of Contract. The sheet offers a levelized budget bill whose details are on file with the Commission, and sets a one-year term. Neither is priced on the sheet and neither is carried.
- Fuel Cost Recovery voltage levels. FCR-27 also prices Primary Distribution and Transmission customers. Only the Secondary Distribution rates are carried, as Prices with no condition, so they apply to every customer on the schedule.

- Minimum Monthly Bill. The sheet sets it at the Basic Service Charge plus ECCR, DSM-R and the Municipal Franchise Fee, and no Price carries it. Every energy and fuel price here is positive, so a bill on these prices alone is never below it.

### JEMC

#### `a` @ 2026-01-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Wholesale Power Cost Adjustment factor. Schedule P-22 increases or decreases every bill by a per-kWh factor the Cooperative computes each month from its estimated purchased-power costs and sales, and publishes no factor. The JSON-LD defines the adjustment as a per-kWh PriceDefinition with no Price, so a modeled bill carries the base energy and Service Charges only, high or low by the month's factor times its kWh.
- Seasons by usage month or billing month. The rate table names "Usage Months" and a later heading "Billing Months" for the same seasons, Summer May through September and Winter October through April. The JSON-LD gates the energy PriceDefinitions on the calendar month of usage; if the Cooperative prices a bill by its billing month, usage in the weeks a billing period runs ahead of its billing month takes the other season's blocks.
- Church facilities. The sheet also makes the schedule available to existing church facilities and to church facilities with a transformer under 100 kVA connected after January 1, 2016. The EligibilityRule carries residential customer kind; the church provision is in its description only.
- Budget Billing, Terms of Payment and Contract Period. The sheet offers a levelized budget bill with the Cooperative's approval, adds a late fee and disconnection charges under its Service Rules and Regulations, makes all charges subject to applicable taxes, and sets a one-year contract. None is priced on the sheet and none is carried.

#### `apev` @ 2026-01-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- On-Peak through September 15. The sheet's On-Peak hours, 3:00 to 8:00 p.m. on weekdays, run from June 1 through September 15. The JSON-LD's Summer Season is the calendar months June through September, so weekday afternoons from September 16 through 30 take the On-Peak price, 27.00 cents per kWh above the Off-Peak price the sheet charges for them.
- Wholesale Power Cost Adjustment factor. Schedule P-22 increases or decreases every bill by a per-kWh factor the Cooperative computes each month from its estimated purchased-power costs and sales, and publishes no factor. The JSON-LD defines the adjustment as a per-kWh PriceDefinition with no Price, so a modeled bill carries the base energy and Service Charges only, high or low by the month's factor times its kWh.
- Qualified plug-in electric vehicle and charging station. The sheet limits the schedule to owner-occupied single-family dwellings whose customer documents possession of a qualified plug-in electric vehicle, with any charging station single-phase, at most 7.8 kW and on a dedicated circuit breaker. The EligibilityRule carries residential customer kind; these conditions are in its description only.
- Budget Billing, Terms of Payment and Contract Period. The sheet requires twelve consecutive months of service, offers a levelized budget bill with the Cooperative's approval, adds a late fee and disconnection charges under its Service Rules and Regulations, makes all charges subject to applicable taxes, and sets a one-year contract. None is priced on the sheet and none is carried.
- Contribution in aid to construction and experimental status. The sheet makes the customer responsible for an upfront non-refundable contribution in aid to construction where the Cooperative must furnish additional facilities, which does not count toward any minimum charge, and lets the Cooperative modify or discontinue the experimental schedule on sixty days' written notice. Neither is a price per unit of service and neither is carried.

- Owner-occupied single-family dwellings. The sheet limits the schedule to single-family dwelling units owned and occupied by the customer for permanent year-round service. The EligibilityRule carries residential customer kind; this condition is in its description only.
- Budget Billing, Terms of Payment and Contract Period. The sheet requires twelve consecutive months of service beginning June 1st of each year, offers a levelized budget bill with the Cooperative's approval, adds a late fee and disconnection charges under its Service Rules and Regulations, makes all charges subject to applicable taxes, and sets a one-year contract. None is priced on the sheet and none is carried.

### LADWP

#### `r-1a` @ 2026-04-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Zone 1 / Zone 2 selector absent. Page 1 of the source PDF prices two tier ladders at identical per-tier rates but different breakpoints (Zone 1: first 350 / next 700 / above 1050 kWh; Zone 2: first 500 / next 1000 / above 1500 kWh). The model folds zone into serviceTierNumber (tiers 1-3 Zone 1, tiers 4-6 Zone 2) inside one ServiceTiers container over one kWh MetricInput, so the brackets overlap and only the tier urpx:name strings say which ladder to walk. URPX v0.5.1 expresses this directly: PlanElements.hasProfileAlternatives with an AlternativeDimension.customDimensionProperty of 'serviceZone', plus ServiceBracket.referencesProfileAlternative on each bracket. The corpus already ships that shape for a geographic dimension in us/ca/pge/electricity/e-1/versions/2026-03-01/rate-plan_pge_e-1_2026-03-01.jsonld (baseline territory P through Z). This is work, not a blocker: the target shape is 3 tiers carrying 2 zone-keyed brackets each, in place of the 6 flattened tiers this filing carries.
- Minimum Charge composition not modeled. Page 1 prices a $10.00 per month Minimum Charge and footnotes it '(1) Plus ECA, ESA and RCA', so the floor is $10.00 plus the three capped-ordinance per-kWh adjustments on actual usage. The $10.00 is carried as ladwp:price-minimum-charge with chargeType urpx:minimumBill and intervalDuration P1M; the 'plus ECA, ESA and RCA' composition is not carried anywhere. Separately, no URPX construct enforces a 'greater of' floor, which is a standard-level gap recorded corpus-wide rather than per filing.
- Adjustment-factor provenance not cited. Page 1 prices VEA, CRPSEA, VRPSEA (computed quarterly) and IRCA (computed annually) by reference only: 'Refer to www.LADWP.com >About Us >Power Rates >Variable Energy Factors and Reliability Cost Adjustment Factor for current Quarterly Electric Adjustment Factors'. The model supplies Apr-Jun 2026 values for all four (VEA 0.00503, CRPSEA 0.01779, VRPSEA 0.03296, IRCA 0.05505) from LADWP's residential-adjustment-billing-factors page, but the document's only DataSourceReference is on the RatePlanVersion and cites the PDF at pageStart/pageEnd 1. v0.5.1 can cite the HTML page: urpx:textHtml is in the mediaType sh:in, DataSourceReference carries accessURL, dateRetrieved, dataSourceType and hasSelector, and Price.hasDataSourceReference is 0..N. No corpus filing uses textHtml yet, so the pattern is unattested but validates.
- IRCA grouped as a quarterly value. ladwp:r-1a-2026-04-01-price-irca-2026-q2 is named and grouped with the Apr-Jun 2026 quarterly factors in ladwp:r-1a-2026-04-01-ps-adjustment-factors-2026-q2, but the sheet footnotes IRCA as computed annually and VEA, CRPSEA and VRPSEA as computed quarterly. The version's urpx:expirationDateTime of 2026-07-01 bounds all seven values. The 2026-07-01 and later versions carry IRCA in a PriceSet of its own, starting on the July 1 its fiscal-year value takes effect.

#### `r-1b` @ 2026-04-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Electric Vehicle Discount, -$0.02500 per kWh, listed on the R-1(B) rate sheet (Electric Rate Summary effective 2019-07-01, page 2) in both the High Season and Low Season columns, and counted as part (3) of the Rate B bill by Ordinance 180127 Schedule R-1 Section 3 ("Rate B shall be the sum of parts (1) through (6)"). Not modeled. It belongs in this filing, not in a separate modifier: Section 4.b sits inside Schedule R-1. v0.5.1 supplies the vocabulary. PriceShape admits hasApplicabilityRule, and a Price at unitPrice -0.02500 gated by an ApplicabilityRule whose ConditionExpression tests a CustomOperandNode customConditionProperty for EV ownership validates against the v0.5.1 shapes (SHACL, conforms, 2026-09-21). It is still unbuilt because the quantity is a source gap. Section 4.b entitles "Owners of licensed passenger electric vehicles" to the discount "on the block of energy designated by the Department as necessary for basic vehicle charging", and neither the rate sheet nor the ordinance publishes that block, so the discounted kWh cannot be derived from any document in this corpus. Any Price this corpus could write for the discount would have to spread -$0.025/kWh across a customer's entire consumption, which is a confident wrong number for everyone who does not own an EV, and is still wrong for those who do, because the discount reaches only the designated block. Revisit when the block size is published.

### PGE

#### `e-1` @ 2026-03-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Base Services Charge is carried by a sibling modifier rather than by the base plan, and URPX has no way to say that composing it is mandatory. Every E-1 customer pays a Base Services Charge; only the amount varies, by income tier (0.19713 CARE, 0.39688 FERA, 0.79343 standard per customer per day, Sheet 1, 61247-E). urpx:PlanElements allows one urpx:hasProfileAlternatives container per version (sh:maxCount 1) and the base plan spends its container on the 20-cell baseline Territory x Code matrix that sets the energy tier boundaries, so the income tier moved to rate-plan-modifier_pge_e-1-income-tier_2026-03-01.jsonld with a container of its own. The only link back is urpx:hasModifierOptions, which says the modifier is available rather than required, so a consumer resolving the base plan alone gets the energy charges and no daily charge at all. Measured 2026-09-23: the base plan alone resolves 4 ledgers and composed with the modifier 5, and the marginal rate 0.32561 is the same either way because the charge is per P1D and not per kWh. Three shapes that would have kept it in the base plan were measured first and each fails differently. Gating three Prices on program enrollment with urpx:hasApplicabilityGroup validates under v0.5.1, and a consumer that does not evaluate the enrollment predicate applies all three daily charges at once and bills every customer 1.38744 per day. Pricing it in the base plan at the Income Tier 3 amount and re-pricing it in the modifier has no precedent here to measure against, since no modifier filing in this corpus prices a urpx:PriceDefinition that its own base defines. And carrying the matrix in a modifier instead, the shape sibling e-tou-c uses, would leave the base plan's brackets as a second source of tier bounds.
- Season is absent from the machine-readable data. Sheet 4 (61099-E) gives separate summer and winter bounds and Special Condition 6 (Sheet 5, 61100-E) defines summer as June 1 through September 30. The two urpx:Season nodes in this file are declared and referenced by nothing: of the 120 urpx:ServiceBracket nodes on the six tiers, 20 per tier, not one carries urpx:appliesInSeason, and no Price carries it either, so season survives only inside urpx:name strings and the tier identities. Each tier's 20 brackets are discriminated by baseline cell alone, which leaves the six tiers overlapping over one kWh metric: at Territory P Code B, 12 kWh/day matches both summer tier 1 (0 to 13.5) and winter tier 2 (11.0 to 44.0) with no discriminator. Measured 2026-09-23 with a cell nominated, at 2026-07-15T17:00-07:00 and at 2026-12-15T17:00-08:00: all six tiers resolve with identical bounds at both instants. Writing appliesInSeason on all 120 is a change of its own, and because this filing declares its own two Season nodes, nothing would depend on another plan's definitions. appliesInSeason is 0..n on both ServiceBracketShape and PriceShape at v0.5.1.
- Schedule E-1 Sheet 6 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 61101-E): Direct Access and Transitional Bundled Service customers 'shall pay all charges shown in the Unbundling of Total Rates except for the Bundled Power Charge Indifference Adjustment and the generation charge', paying their Vintaged PCIA instead. pge:e-1-vintage-pcia is gated on DA and TBS and adds the vintaged rate, but nothing removes this version's generation Prices or its Bundled PCIA Price, so base plus pge:e-1-vintage-pcia still charges a DA or TBS customer PG&E generation and both the bundled and the vintaged PCIA. The DA and TBS offset waits on Schedule DA-CRS. Community Choice Aggregation customers are priced by base plus pge:cca-crs, whose multiplier -1 Price offsets pge:e-1-2026-03-01-summary-generation.
- Income Tier 2 is under-specified. Sheet 1 (61247-E) defines it as either (a) FERA-enrolled or (b) a resident of an affordable rental unit listed in the California Housing Partnership database and restricted to incomes at or below 80 percent of Area Median Income. The model gates Income Tier 2 only on enrollment in pge:program-fera and defines Income Tier 3 as not-CARE and not-FERA, so a branch (b) customer resolves to Tier 3 and is billed 0.79343 per day instead of 0.39688, roughly double. The gate lives in the sibling rate-plan-modifier_pge_e-1-income-tier_2026-03-01.jsonld. Expressible and precedented in this filing: the vintage-PCIA modifier and the base plan's 20 baseline cells both use CustomOperandNode with customConditionProperty for a predicate URPX does not name.
- Base Services Charge is modeled only as the three income-tier totals (0.19713, 0.39688, 0.79343), in the sibling rate-plan-modifier_pge_e-1-income-tier_2026-03-01.jsonld. Sheet 3 (61098-E) also prices it by component, 4 components x 3 income tiers = 12 values in dollars per customer per day: Distribution (-0.10751 / -0.02710 / 0.36945), Public Purpose Program (0.19131 / 0.31065 / 0.31065), Nuclear Decommissioning (0.00000 x3) and New System Generation Charge (0.11333 x3). None of the 12 appears in the model. The totals are right and the components sum to them, so no bill is wrong, but the published unbundling is absent while the energy rates are fully unbundled.
- Rule 19 Part F: "If PG&E finds that you need more than the standard 16.438 kWh per day, it will increase your daily Medical Baseline Quantity. In determining how many Additional Quantities you will be assigned, PG&E will round its calculation of your total daily kWh medical needs up to the nearest increment of 16.438 kWh." pge:e-1-medical-baseline-2026-03-01 carries one Standard Medical Baseline Quantity. An additional quantity is a per-customer multiple PG&E assigns, so it is a customer attribute rather than a tariff figure; it is expressible as further adjusting brackets once a multiple is chosen, and nothing in this corpus chooses one.
- Seasonal changeover proration not modeled. Special Condition 6 (Sheet 5, 61100-E) requires bills that include the June 1 or October 1 changeover to multiply the applicable daily baseline quantity for each season by the number of days in each season. Nothing in the model expresses this. The only slot is urpx:prorationMethod, and it is settled rather than unconfirmed: BillingModelShape gives it sh:datatype xsd:string with sh:maxCount 1 and no sh:in, so it is free text with no allowed-value list to establish (verified against abdfb521). It is used by zero filings in this corpus, and its own skos:note scopes it to service starting or ending mid-period rather than to a season boundary, so it would carry this provision as prose at best. The allocation arithmetic itself has no computable form in v0.5.1.
- New System Generation Charge, energy component, 0.00000 per kWh (Sheet 2, 61097-E) has no PriceDefinition and no Price. Zero-valued today so no bill is affected, but the schedule's line item is unrepresented and a future nonzero value would be silently missing. Minor; listed so the Sheet 2 enumeration is complete.

|---|---|---|

#### `e-elec` @ 2026-01-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Sheet 4 Special Condition 4 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 60703-E) prices the Vintaged Power Charge Indifference Adjustment as 18 per-kWh rates, 2009 Vintage $0.02973 through 2026 Vintage $(0.01011), payable by DA, CCA and TBS customers. For CCA customers the same 18 rates are carried by pge:cca-crs, from Schedule CCA-CRS Sheets 3 to 5. For DA and TBS customers they are not modeled; this version's only PCIA Price is the Bundled PCIA at $(0.01011). Expressible as an inline modifier gated on DA and TBS, as pge:e-tou-c-vintage-pcia is.
- Schedule E-ELEC Sheet 4 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 60703-E) Special Condition 4: Direct Access and Transitional Bundled Service customers 'shall pay all charges shown in the Unbundling of Total Rates except for the Bundled Power Charge Indifference Adjustment and the generation charge', paying their Vintaged PCIA instead. Neither half is modeled for them: no modifier carries this schedule's vintage table for DA or TBS, and nothing removes this version's generation Prices or its Bundled PCIA Price, so this base charges a DA or TBS customer PG&E generation and the bundled PCIA. The DA and TBS treatment waits on Schedule DA-CRS. Community Choice Aggregation customers are priced by base plus pge:cca-crs, which carries the vintage rates from Schedule CCA-CRS Sheets 3 to 5 and whose multiplier -1 Price offsets pge:e-elec-2026-01-01-summary-generation.
- Wildfire Fund Charge exclusion, Sheet 5 Special Condition 7 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 54740-E): the charge 'applies to all retail sales, excluding CARE and Medical Baseline sales.' pd-wildfire-fund prices $0.00591 per kWh unconditionally. Expressible under v0.5.1: PriceDefinitionShape permits hasApplicabilityRule and ApplicabilityRuleShape permits hasConditionExpression, and pd-climate-credit in this same file already uses hasApplicabilityRule. pge:d-care's exemption names the current E-ELEC version's Price, not this one's, and no PG&E Medical Baseline schedule is modeled.

#### `e-elec` @ 2026-03-27  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Sheet 5 (AL 7846-E, leaf 61120-E) prices the Vintaged Power Charge Indifference Adjustment as 18 per-kWh rates, 2009 Vintage $0.02973 through 2026 Vintage $(0.01011), payable by DA, CCA and TBS customers. For CCA customers the same 18 rates are carried by pge:cca-crs, from Schedule CCA-CRS Sheets 3 to 5. For DA and TBS customers they are not modeled; this version's only PCIA Price is the Bundled PCIA at $(0.01011). Expressible as an inline modifier gated on DA and TBS, as pge:e-tou-c-vintage-pcia is.
- Sheet 2 (AL 7869-E, leaf 61248-E) defines Income Tier 2 as customers who either (a) take service on FERA or (b) live in an affordable rental unit within a property listed in the California Housing Partnership's database restricted to residents at or below 80 percent of Area Median Income. The model gates Tier 2 on hasProgramEnrollment equals pge:program-fera alone, so a qualifying branch-(b) customer not on FERA falls through to the Standard alternative and is billed $0.79343 per meter per day instead of $0.39688, exactly double. Expressible: urpx:conditionProperty carries no sh:in in the v0.5.1 shapes (any IRI is accepted) and CustomOperandNode.customConditionProperty is available, so branch (b) can be OR'd into the Tier 2 expression with a matching third negation on the Standard branch. Every PG&E residential base schedule shares this gap, so fix them together.
- Schedule E-ELEC Sheets 4 and 5 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheets 61119-E and 61120-E) Special Condition 4: Direct Access and Transitional Bundled Service customers 'shall pay all charges shown in the Unbundling of Total Rates except for the Bundled Power Charge Indifference Adjustment and the generation charge', paying their Vintaged PCIA instead. Neither half is modeled for them: no modifier carries this schedule's vintage table for DA or TBS, and nothing removes this version's generation Prices or its Bundled PCIA Price, so this base charges a DA or TBS customer PG&E generation and the bundled PCIA. The DA and TBS treatment waits on Schedule DA-CRS. Community Choice Aggregation customers are priced by base plus pge:cca-crs, which carries the vintage rates from Schedule CCA-CRS Sheets 3 to 5 and whose multiplier -1 Price offsets pge:e-elec-2026-03-27-summary-generation.
- Special Condition 7 (Sheet 6) states the Wildfire Fund Charge 'applies to all retail sales, excluding CARE and Medical Baseline sales'; the model carries it as an unconditional $0.00591/kWh Price, so it prices the CARE and Medical Baseline sales the tariff excludes. The CARE half is carried by pge:d-care, whose exemption Price is urpx:multiplier -1 over this Price. The Medical Baseline half is not modeled, and no PG&E Medical Baseline schedule is modeled.
- Sheet 3 also prices the Base Services Charge by component (Distribution, Public Purpose Program, Nuclear Decommissioning, New System Generation Charge, each at three income tiers, 12 figures). Only the three bundled totals are modeled. The components do sum to the modeled totals, so nothing resolves wrong, but the filing is asymmetric: energy is modeled unbundled and the base charge bundled. This is the convention across the PG&E residential filings, not an e-elec slip; it matters for a DA or CCA consumer needing the delivery-only components. Lowest-weight item in this list.

#### `e-tou-c` @ 2026-03-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Schedule E-TOU-C Sheet 8 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 61131-E): Direct Access and Transitional Bundled Service customers 'shall pay all charges shown in the Unbundling of Total Rates except for the Bundled Power Charge Indifference Adjustment and the generation charge', paying their Vintaged PCIA instead. pge:e-tou-c-vintage-pcia is gated on DA and TBS and adds the vintaged rate, but nothing removes this version's generation Prices or its Bundled PCIA Price, so base plus pge:e-tou-c-vintage-pcia still charges a DA or TBS customer PG&E generation and both the bundled and the vintaged PCIA. The DA and TBS offset waits on Schedule DA-CRS. Community Choice Aggregation customers are priced by base plus pge:cca-crs, whose multiplier -1 Price offsets pge:e-tou-c-2026-03-01-summary-generation.
- Sheet 1 and Special Condition 11 (Sheet 10, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 61133-E) exempt CARE and Medical Baseline sales from the Wildfire Fund Charge. The Medical Baseline half is pge:e-tou-c-medical-baseline-2026-03-01, whose exemption Price is urpx:multiplier -1 over this version's Wildfire Fund Charge Price. The CARE half is not carried for this version: pge:d-care's exemption names the 2026-03-27 version's Price, not this one's, so a CARE customer under this version is priced $0.00591 per kWh.
- Special Condition 1 (Sheet 5, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 61128-E) publishes the baseline quantities as kWh PER DAY. pge:e-tou-c-2026-03-01-mi-energy-baseline declares unitOfMeasure urpx:kWh with no intervalDuration, so a bracket bound of 13.5 reads as 13.5 kWh rather than 13.5 kWh per day; on a 30-day summer bill in territory P the real Tier 1 ceiling is 405 kWh. v0.5.1 MetricSpecificationShape carries urpx:intervalDuration (xsd:duration, maxCount 1), so P1D is expressible. Put the interval there and not on a PriceDefinition's CalculationMethod, where an interval states the window a charge is calculated over rather than the period the baseline quantity is published in.
- The schedule draws one usage boundary, baseline versus over baseline: Sheet 2's 'Applied to Baseline Usage Only' and the two Sheet 3 Conservation Incentive Adjustment lines. This version asserts a third tier, pge:e-tou-c-2026-03-01-tier-baseline-t2c 'above four times the Special Condition 1 quantity', and caps tier 2 at exactly 4x the Special Condition 1 quantity in every cell. Nothing in the ten sheets states 400 percent, four times, or a second over-baseline tier. Both tiers price at $0.05354 so the split is economically inert, but it publishes a boundary this filing does not. Either cite the source for the 4x figure or collapse Tier 2 and Tier 2 Continued.
- Rule 19 Part F: "If PG&E finds that you need more than the standard 16.438 kWh per day, it will increase your daily Medical Baseline Quantity. In determining how many Additional Quantities you will be assigned, PG&E will round its calculation of your total daily kWh medical needs up to the nearest increment of 16.438 kWh." pge:e-tou-c-medical-baseline-2026-03-01 carries one Standard Medical Baseline Quantity. An additional quantity is a per-customer multiple PG&E assigns, so it is a customer attribute rather than a tariff figure; it is expressible as further adjusting brackets once a multiple is chosen, and nothing in this corpus chooses one.
- Sheet 4 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 61127-E) publishes the Base Services Charge by component for each income tier, twelve values: Distribution -0.10751 / -0.02710 / 0.36945, Public Purpose Program 0.19131 / 0.31065 / 0.31065, Nuclear Decommissioning zero across all three, New System Generation Charge 0.11333 across all three. The filing models only the three daily totals of $0.19713, $0.39688 and $0.79343. The components sum exactly to the totals, so there is no bill effect, but the energy side is modeled at component granularity and this is not. Expressible today and at v0.2.1; simply unbuilt.
- Sheet 1 gives Income Tier 2 two qualification paths: FERA enrollment, or an affordable rental unit within a property listed in the California Housing Partnership's database restricted to residents at or below 80 percent of Area Median Income. Only the FERA branch is modeled, and the Tier 3 alternative is an AND of notEquals against CARE and FERA, so a customer qualifying through affordable housing alone resolves to $0.79343 per day instead of $0.39688. Expressible: urpx:orOperator is in v0.5.1, and this filing already uses CustomOperandNode for departureVintage and baselineTerritory.
- Sheet 3's New System Generation Charge on all usage, $0.00000 per kWh, is the only one of the 23 published Sheet 3 line items with no Price in the model. Zero-valued, so there is no bill effect, but every sibling line is modeled.
- Neither proration rule is carried. Sheet 1 states that time-of-use period usage is assigned to baseline tiers pro rata, so that a customer with twenty percent of its usage in the peak period has twenty percent of the usage in each tier treated as on-peak. Special Condition 4 (Sheet 6) prorates the daily baseline quantity and rates by the number of days of each season in a bill spanning the June 1 or October 1 changeover. This version carries both the time-of-use dimension and the baseline tiers, but nothing states how the two combine or how a daily quantity scales to a billing period. Not expressible at v0.5.1: CalculationMethodShape is sh:closed with no condition or allocation path and only the free-text calculationFormula, which no consumer reads; urpx:prorationMethod is an xsd:string on BillingModel scoped to mid-period service start and stop.
- Special Condition 8 (Sheet 7, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 61130-E) and its CCA and DA counterpart on Sheet 9 credit a customer, for twelve months from enrollment, the difference between what it paid on this schedule and what it would have paid on the tiered non-time-of-use Schedule E-1, subject to their own exclusions. Not modeled. Recordable but not computable under v0.5.1: urpx:creditPriceProtection is a ChargeTypeValue member (verified in the ChargeTypeValue oneOf at abdfb521), so the line item can be declared and cited, but its amount is a whole bill computed under a different schedule and settles as a cumulative true-up on a later bill rather than as a rate, and URPX carries no cross-schedule bill comparison. If the corpus decides a 12-month retrospective true-up is rebilling rather than pricing, drop this entry rather than leaving it to depress coverage.

Sheet 61131-E): Direct Access and Transitional Bundled Service customers 'shall pay all charges shown in the Unbundling of Total Rates except for the Bundled Power Charge Indifference Adjustment and the generation charge', paying their Vintaged PCIA instead. pge:e-tou-c-vintage-pcia is gated on DA and TBS and adds the vintaged rate, but nothing removes this version's generation Prices or its Bundled PCIA Price, so base plus pge:e-tou-c-vintage-pcia still charges a DA or TBS customer PG&E generation and both the bundled and the vintaged PCIA. - Sheet 1, RATES: 'TOU period usage is assigned to tiers on a pro-rated basis. For example, if twenty percent of a customer's usage is in the peak period, then twenty percent of the total usage in each tier will be treated as on-peak usage.' This is the rule composing this version's TOU dimension with its baseline tiers, and neither is stated to depend on the other. Under v0.5.1, CalculationMethodShape is sh:closed and the only slot is the free-text calculationFormula, so the rule can be recorded as prose but not as a computable composition. Standard-level, not a modeling oversight.
- Sheet 3's line item New System Generation Charge (all usage), $0.00000 per kWh, has no PriceDefinition and no Price. Zero-valued, so no bill is wrong and the 20 modeled components still reconstruct the Sheet 2 totals, but the unbundled component list is incomplete against the sheet and the omission becomes a wrong number the first time PG&E sets the charge nonzero.
- Special Condition 1 (Sheet 5, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 61128-E) publishes the baseline quantities as kWh PER DAY. pge:e-tou-c-2026-03-27-mi-energy-baseline declares unitOfMeasure urpx:kWh with no intervalDuration, so a bracket bound of 13.5 reads as 13.5 kWh rather than 13.5 kWh per day; on a 30-day summer bill in territory P the real Tier 1 ceiling is 405 kWh. v0.5.1 MetricSpecificationShape carries urpx:intervalDuration (xsd:duration, maxCount 1), so P1D is expressible. - The schedule draws one usage boundary, baseline versus over baseline: Sheet 2's 'Applied to Baseline Usage Only' and the two Sheet 3 Conservation Incentive Adjustment lines. This version asserts a third tier, pge:e-tou-c-2026-03-27-tier-baseline-t2c 'above four times the Special Condition 1 quantity', and caps tier 2 at exactly 4x the Special Condition 1 quantity in every cell. #### `e-tou-c` @ 2026-06-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Schedule E-TOU-C Sheet 8 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 61131-E): Direct Access and Transitional Bundled Service customers 'shall pay all charges shown in the Unbundling of Total Rates except for the Bundled Power Charge Indifference Adjustment and the generation charge', paying their Vintaged PCIA instead. pge:e-tou-c-vintage-pcia is gated on DA and TBS and adds the vintaged rate, but nothing removes this version's generation Prices or its Bundled PCIA Price, so base plus pge:e-tou-c-vintage-pcia still charges a DA or TBS customer PG&E generation and both the bundled and the vintaged PCIA. The DA and TBS offset waits on Schedule DA-CRS. Community Choice Aggregation customers are priced by base plus pge:cca-crs, whose multiplier -1 Price offsets pge:e-tou-c-2026-06-01-summary-generation.
- Sheet 4 prices the Base Services Charge by component, per income tier, $ per customer per day: Distribution (-$0.10751 / -$0.02710 / $0.36945), Public Purpose Program ($0.19131 / $0.31065 / $0.31065), Nuclear Decommissioning ($0.00000 all tiers), New System Generation Charge ($0.11333 all tiers). The model carries only the three Sheet 2 bundled totals ($0.19713 / $0.39688 / $0.79343), which the components sum to exactly, so no number is wrong today. What is lost is the delivery-versus-supply split of the fixed charge, which is what Sheet 8's CCA/DA treatment needs, and the model already unbundles the energy side this way. Expressible now: three more PriceDefinitions, each with a three-alternative PriceSetAlternatives mirroring the existing Base Services Charge structure.
- Rule 19 Part F: "If PG&E finds that you need more than the standard 16.438 kWh per day, it will increase your daily Medical Baseline Quantity. In determining how many Additional Quantities you will be assigned, PG&E will round its calculation of your total daily kWh medical needs up to the nearest increment of 16.438 kWh." pge:e-tou-c-medical-baseline-2026-06-01 carries one Standard Medical Baseline Quantity. An additional quantity is a per-customer multiple PG&E assigns, so it is a customer attribute rather than a tariff figure; it is expressible as further adjusting brackets once a multiple is chosen, and nothing in this corpus chooses one.
- Sheet 1 defines Income Tier 2 by two disjuncts: (a) FERA-eligible households, or (b) residents of an affordable rental unit in a property listed in the California Housing Partnership's database and restricted to incomes at or below 80 percent of Area Median Income. The tier-2 ProfileAlternative gates only on hasProgramEnrollment = pge:program-fera, so a qualifying (b) customer resolves to Income Tier 3 and is billed $0.79343 per customer per day where the schedule prices $0.39688. Expressible now as an orOperator disjunct over a CustomOperandNode, the construct this file's vintage-PCIA alternatives already use for customConditionProperty 'departureVintage'.
- Sheet 1, RATES: 'TOU period usage is assigned to tiers on a pro-rated basis. For example, if twenty percent of a customer's usage is in the peak period, then twenty percent of the total usage in each tier will be treated as on-peak usage.' This is the rule composing this version's TOU dimension with its baseline tiers, and neither is stated to depend on the other. Under v0.5.1, CalculationMethodShape is sh:closed and the only slot is the free-text calculationFormula, so the rule can be recorded as prose but not as a computable composition. Standard-level, not a modeling oversight.
- Sheet 3's line item New System Generation Charge (all usage), $0.00000 per kWh, has no PriceDefinition and no Price. Zero-valued, so no bill is wrong and the 20 modeled components still reconstruct the Sheet 2 totals, but the unbundled component list is incomplete against the sheet and the omission becomes a wrong number the first time PG&E sets the charge nonzero.
- Special Condition 1 (Sheet 5, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 61128-E) publishes the baseline quantities as kWh PER DAY. pge:e-tou-c-2026-06-01-mi-energy-baseline declares unitOfMeasure urpx:kWh with no intervalDuration, so a bracket bound of 13.5 reads as 13.5 kWh rather than 13.5 kWh per day; on a 30-day summer bill in territory P the real Tier 1 ceiling is 405 kWh. v0.5.1 MetricSpecificationShape carries urpx:intervalDuration (xsd:duration, maxCount 1), so P1D is expressible. Put the interval there and not on a PriceDefinition's CalculationMethod, where an interval states the window a charge is calculated over rather than the period the baseline quantity is published in.
- The schedule draws one usage boundary, baseline versus over baseline: Sheet 2's 'Applied to Baseline Usage Only' and the two Sheet 3 Conservation Incentive Adjustment lines. This version asserts a third tier, pge:e-tou-c-2026-06-01-tier-baseline-t2c 'above four times the Special Condition 1 quantity', and caps tier 2 at exactly 4x the Special Condition 1 quantity in every cell. Nothing in the ten sheets states 400 percent, four times, or a second over-baseline tier. Both tiers price at $0.05354 so the split is economically inert, but it publishes a boundary this filing does not. Either cite the source for the 4x figure or collapse Tier 2 and Tier 2 Continued.
