#### `e-tou-d` @ 2026-03-27  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- PG&E's TOU schedules take holiday dates as "those on which the holidays are legally observed", read here as California Government Code section 6701: "(a) If January 1, February 12, March 31, July 4, September 9, November 11, or December 25 falls upon a Sunday, the Monday following is a holiday. (b) If November 11 falls upon a Saturday, the preceding Friday is a holiday." In pge:holidays-e-tou-d a weekday holiday is Off-Peak from 5 to 8 p.m. Veterans Day carries urpx:nearestWeekdayObservance, which is exactly 6701 for November 11. New Year's Day, Independence Day and Christmas Day carry urpx:actualDateObservance, so a Saturday one stays on Saturday, and the Monday after a Sunday one is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26 and 2040-01-02. After 2040 each Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day or Christmas Day needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.
- Schedule E-TOU-D Sheet 5 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 61138-E) Special Condition 5: Direct Access and Transitional Bundled Service customers 'shall pay all charges shown in the Unbundling of Total Rates except for the Bundled Power Charge Indifference Adjustment and the generation charge', paying their Vintaged PCIA instead. pge:e-tou-d-vintage-pcia is gated on DA and TBS and adds the vintaged rate, but nothing removes this version's generation Prices or its Bundled PCIA Price, so base plus pge:e-tou-d-vintage-pcia still charges a DA or TBS customer PG&E generation and both the bundled and the vintaged PCIA. The DA and TBS offset waits on Schedule DA-CRS. Community Choice Aggregation customers are priced by base plus pge:cca-crs, whose multiplier -1 Price offsets pge:e-tou-d-2026-03-27-summary-generation.
- Special Condition 8 (Sheet 7, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 61140-E): the Wildfire Fund Charge of $0.00591/kWh (Sheet 3, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 61136-E) 'applies to all retail sales, excluding CARE and Medical Baseline sales.' The model carries that Price unconditionally. The CARE half is carried by pge:d-care, whose exemption Price is urpx:multiplier -1 over this Price. The Medical Baseline half is not modeled, and no PG&E Medical Baseline schedule is modeled.
- Sheet 3 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 61136-E) prices New System Generation Charge (all usage) at $0.00000 per kWh. No urpx:Price exists for it. Zero today, so no resolved number moves, but the 16-component sum that reconciles to the Sheet 2 bundled totals would go silently short the first time the charge is set nonzero. Add the Price at 0.00000.
- Sheet 3 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 61136-E) prices the Base Services Charge by component, 12 values in $ per customer per day: Distribution ($0.10751) / ($0.02710) / $0.36945, Public Purpose Program $0.19131 / $0.31065 / $0.31065, Nuclear Decommissioning $0.00000 x3, New System Generation Charge $0.11333 x3 for Income Tiers 1/2/3. The model carries only the three per-tier totals ($0.19713 / $0.39688 / $0.79343), while unbundling the energy side fully into 16 components. Expressible as per-tier PriceSets under the existing income-tier PriceSetAlternatives; not built. Note the PDF offsets the NSGC sub-table's label and value rows by one; $0.11333 for all three tiers is confirmed by each tier's four components summing exactly to its Sheet 2 total.
- Sheet 1 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 61134-E) defines Income Tier 2 as customers either (a) above 200 and below 250 percent of the Federal Poverty Guideline levels for FERA, or (b) living in an affordable rental unit in the California Housing Partnership's database restricted to residents at or below 80 percent of Area Median Income. The Tier 2 ProfileAlternative tests FERA enrollment only and the Tier 3 alternative is 'not CARE and not FERA', so a consumer evaluating these conditions places a branch (b) customer in Tier 3 at $0.79343 per day instead of $0.39688. Expressible under v0.5.1 as an orOperator BooleanOperationNode with a CustomOperandNode carrying urpx:customConditionProperty for the affordable-housing predicate; not built.

#### `ev2` @ 2026-03-27  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Schedule EV2 Sheets 4 and 5 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheets 61172-E and 61173-E) Special Condition 4: Direct Access and Transitional Bundled Service customers 'shall pay all charges shown in the Unbundling of Total Rates except for the Bundled Power Charge Indifference Adjustment and the generation charge', paying their Vintaged PCIA instead. pge:ev2-vintage-pcia is gated on DA and TBS and adds the vintaged rate, but nothing removes this version's generation Prices or its Bundled PCIA Price, so base plus pge:ev2-vintage-pcia still charges a DA or TBS customer PG&E generation and both the bundled and the vintaged PCIA. The DA and TBS offset waits on Schedule DA-CRS. Community Choice Aggregation customers are priced by base plus pge:cca-crs, whose multiplier -1 Price offsets pge:ev2-2026-03-27-summary-generation.
- Income Tier 2's second qualifying branch is not modeled. Sheet 2 places a customer in Tier 2 under either (a) FERA-level income or (b) residence in an affordable rental unit listed in the California Housing Partnership's database and restricted to residents at or below 80 percent of Area Median Income. The model gates Tier 2 on FERA program enrollment alone and defines Tier 3 as 'not CARE and not FERA', so a branch (b) customer who is not FERA-enrolled resolves the Tier 3 base services charge of $0.79343 per day instead of $0.39688, about $145 per year too high. Expressible as a second operand under an orOperator using a CustomOperandNode with customConditionProperty, the same device this file already uses for departure vintage. Work, not a blocker.
- The Wildfire Fund Charge is not excluded for CARE and Medical Baseline customers. Sheet 6, Special Condition 7, says the charge 'applies to all retail sales, excluding CARE and Medical Baseline sales'. The $0.00591 per kWh Wildfire Fund Price carries no applicability condition. The CARE half is carried by pge:d-care, whose exemption Price is urpx:multiplier -1 over this Price. The Medical Baseline half is not modeled, and no PG&E Medical Baseline schedule is modeled.
- The Base Services Charge is carried only as a total, not unbundled. Sheet 3 prices it by component for each income tier: Distribution $(0.10751) / $(0.02710) / $0.36945, Public Purpose Program $0.19131 / $0.31065 / $0.31065, Nuclear Decommissioning $0.00000 for all three, New System Generation Charge $0.11333 for all three. The model carries only the three Sheet 2 totals. The twelve components sum exactly to those totals so no bill is wrong, but the schedule prices these values and the energy side of this same filing is modeled component-wise.
- The New System Generation Charge per-kWh line has no Price. Sheet 3 lists it at $0.00000 as the thirteenth all-usage energy component, and it is the only unbundled energy component missing from the model. No dollar is misbilled; the line item is absent.
- The annual usage cap is now encoded as a percentage, not a kWh quantity. Sheet 1 caps a customer at 800 percent of their annual baseline allowance over a rolling 12 months. pge:ms-energy-annual-rolling previously declared urpx:measurementKind urpx:energy and urpx:unitOfMeasure urpx:kWh, so pge:er-annual-usage-cap read as an 800 kWh cap, which the schedule does not say. It now carries urpx:percent and no measurementKind, both of which v0.5.1 admits: urpx:percent is a UnitOfMeasureValue member and measurementKind is sh:minCount 0. What is missing upstream is a standard named metric for 'annual baseline allowance' to serve as the denominator, so the model says 800 percent without saying of what (urpx-org/urpx discussion #38).
- Charges priced by reference to schedules this corpus does not hold, recorded once so the exclusion is stated rather than silent. Sheet 1 sends a customer regularly supplied in part by a nonutility source to 'monthly reservation charges as specified under Section 1 of Schedule S', with Special Conditions 5 and 6 on Sheet 6 exempting non-exporting solar of one megawatt or less that has not elected NEM, and Electric Rule 1 distributed energy resources. Sheet 5 Special Condition 4 sends DA and CCA customers to Schedule E-FFS for the franchise fee surcharge, and Sheet 4 sends TBS customers to Schedule TBCC for short-term commodity prices. None of the three schedules is committed here. These are other schedules' pricing and are out of scope for this filing under the same ruling the sibling pge:e-elec@2026-03-27 applies to the identical cross-references. Recorded rather than dropped because Special Conditions 5 and 6 are this schedule's own text.

### PSE

#### `307` @ 2026-05-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Sheet 307-A prices the Basic Charge at "$7.49 single phase or $17.99 three phase". Only the single-phase $7.49 is carried, as a Price with no condition, so it applies to every customer and a three-phase customer's monthly charge is $10.50 low.
- Schedule 81 (Tax Adjustment) increases "the rates and charges named in this tariff" by the effect of a municipal tax that varies by jurisdiction, from 2.136 percent to 9.119 percent in the Sheet 81 tables. Not carried; the Summary Sheet also excludes it ("Rates in this summary include the effect of all supplemental rate schedules except Schedule 81"). A bill inside a taxing jurisdiction resolves low by that percentage of every charge.
- Sheet 307-B BILL PROTECTION: an annual bill credit, for low-income Customers selected for bill protection in the pilot that ended September 30, 2025, of Energy Charge costs more than 10 percent above what Schedule 7 would have charged. Not carried. It reaches only those pilot participants, and its value is a bill recomputed under Schedule 7, which no Price here can state.
- Exemptions in the supplemental schedules are not carried: neither Schedule 111 rate applies to a Customer taking optional Schedule 139 service or registered as a National Security Entity, and Schedule 141A does not apply to a Customer who took Schedule 139 service on or before that schedule's original effective date. Both rates are carried as Prices with no condition, so such a customer resolves $0.009637 per kWh low on Schedule 111 (whose net rate is a credit) and $0.002010 per kWh high on Schedule 141A.
- Native American Heritage Day is carried as dated urpx:Holiday entries for each year from this version's year through 2040. Sheet 307-B defines it as "The Friday immediately following the fourth Thursday in November", which a rule of the form "Fourth Friday in November" gets wrong in years when November 1 is a Friday, so no rule string is carried. #### `307` @ 2026-10-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Sheet 307-A prices the Basic Charge at "$7.49 single phase or $17.99 three phase". Only the single-phase $7.49 is carried, as a Price with no condition, so it applies to every customer and a three-phase customer's monthly charge is $10.50 low.
- Schedule 81 (Tax Adjustment) increases "the rates and charges named in this tariff" by the effect of a municipal tax that varies by jurisdiction, from 2.136 percent to 9.119 percent in the Sheet 81 tables. Not carried; the Summary Sheet also excludes it ("Rates in this summary include the effect of all supplemental rate schedules except Schedule 81"). A bill inside a taxing jurisdiction resolves low by that percentage of every charge.
- Sheet 307-B BILL PROTECTION: an annual bill credit, for low-income Customers selected for bill protection in the pilot that ended September 30, 2025, of Energy Charge costs more than 10 percent above what Schedule 7 would have charged. Not carried. It reaches only those pilot participants, and its value is a bill recomputed under Schedule 7, which no Price here can state.
- Exemptions in the supplemental schedules are not carried: neither Schedule 111 rate applies to a Customer taking optional Schedule 139 service or registered as a National Security Entity, and Schedule 141A does not apply to a Customer who took Schedule 139 service on or before that schedule's original effective date. Both rates are carried as Prices with no condition, so such a customer resolves $0.009637 per kWh low on Schedule 111 (whose net rate is a credit) and $0.002010 per kWh high on Schedule 141A.
- Native American Heritage Day is carried as dated urpx:Holiday entries for each year from this version's year through 2040. Sheet 307-B defines it as "The Friday immediately following the fourth Thursday in November", which a rule of the form "Fourth Friday in November" gets wrong in years when November 1 is a Friday, so no rule string is carried. Each year after 2040 needs a further dated entry.

### SCE

#### `d` @ 2025-11-15  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Baseline / Nonbaseline tier boundary is not carried. Sheet 3 (Cal. P.U.C. 90635-E) makes the Conservation Incentive Adjustment the only component that differs between the tiers, $(0.03845) per kWh on Baseline Service against $0.06604 on Nonbaseline, so the boundary decides which of the two Energy Charges a kWh receives. Sheet 2 (90634-E) footnote * puts the boundary outside this document: Nonbaseline Service is all kWh in excess of applicable Baseline allocations as described in Preliminary Statement, Part H, Baseline Service. Part H is in no PDF in this corpus, so this is a source gap for the absolute bounds, and the allocation varies by Baseline Region, by season, and by Basic against All-Electric service. What the model states instead is wrong rather than merely incomplete: sce:d-2025-11-15-tiers-baseline-nonbaseline points referencesMetricInput at sce:d-2025-11-15-mi-energy, whose MetricSpecification carries unitOfMeasure urpx:kWh, while the brackets carry lowerBound 0 with upperBound 1.0, and lowerBound 1.0. Read as written that says Tier 1 ends at one kilowatt-hour, and the ServiceBrackets carry no unit of their own to say otherwise. Two routes are open under v0.5.1 and neither is built: precompute absolute kWh bounds per region, season and service type, the way us/ca/pge/electricity/e-tou-c does at 13.5 and 11.0 kWh per day, which needs Part H; or point referencesMetricInput at a MetricInput whose MetricSpecification carries unitOfMeasure urpx:ratio, which SHACL validation accepts against the v0.5.1 shapes and which makes the 0 to 1.0 bounds honest without needing Part H.
- Base Services Charge is carried only as its $0.794 per meter per day total. Sheet 3 (90635-E) prices it by component: Distrbtn $0.423, NSGC $0.000, NDC $0.000, PPPC $0.371, and footnote ** splits the $0.423 into a BSC Distribution Charge of $0.360 per meter per day and a BSC Surcharge of $0.063 per meter per day. The model decomposes the Energy Charge into all eight Sheet 3 components but leaves the BSC as one number. The decomposition is load-bearing rather than cosmetic: Special Condition 4 on Sheet 7 (90639-E) exempts CARE and FERA customers from the Base Services Charge Surcharge of $0.063 per meter per day, and us/ca/sce/electricity/care and .../fera each carry a standalone $-0.063 credit with no base component for it to cancel. Expressible now with the same PriceDefinition and Price pattern the Energy Charge already uses; this is work, not a blocker.
- CARE Surcharge of $0.212 per meter per day is itemized nowhere. Special Condition 4 on Sheet 7 (90639-E) states that eligible CARE Customers are exempt from paying the CARE Surcharge of $0.212 per meter per day. No rate row on Sheet 2 or Sheet 3 names it. Sheet 3 footnote 5 says the PPPC includes the California Alternate Rates for Energy Surcharge where applicable, and the only per meter per day PPPC on the operative sheets is the BSC PPPC component of $0.371, but whether the $0.212 sits inside that $0.371 is not settled by this PDF and would need Schedule D-CARE or SCE's PPPC detail, neither of which is in this corpus. us/ca/sce/electricity/care records this exemption as the reason its own claim is partial and states that the gap closes when the base SCE filings itemize the surcharge, which makes it this filing's work. Note that the four superseded Sheet 7 revisions in this same PDF carry the surcharge as $0.01435, $0.01292, $0.01462 and $0.01443 per kWh, so the 2025-11-15 restructuring moved it from an energy charge to the Base Services Charge.
- California Climate Credit roll-over is not modeled. Special Condition 5 on Sheet 7 (90639-E): any California Climate Credit not consumed in the billing period in which the credit is applied will roll over to each subsequent billing period until the credit is fully consumed. URPX v0.5.1 has no term for a balance carried until consumed. Its only ledger-balance term is urpx:settlementInterval (domain Ledger, range xsd:duration, "Period over which ledger balances are settled"), a finite period whose own skos:note example is the P12M NBT true-up; LedgerShape and BillingCycleShape are sh:closed and none of their other properties names a balance, and a SPARQL scan of the ontology at abdfb521 for carry-forward, rollover, carryover, unconsumed and banked returns no other balance term. The $(56.00) amount and its April and October ApplicabilityRule are both carried correctly; only the carry-forward is missing. The same Special Condition also fixes the credit's position in the bill, after city and county utility users' taxes and before franchise fees, which is likewise unmodeled and has no v0.5.1 term: urpx:priority applies only to TOUPeriod, PriceUpdateRule and RateCase, and PriceShape, PriceDefinitionShape and LedgerShape carry no ordering property.
- Generation is applicable only to Bundled Service Customers per Sheet 2 (90634-E) footnote 2, and sce:d-2025-11-15-pd-generation carries no applicability gate, so as written its $0.13170 per kWh applies to every customer of this plan, Direct Access and CCA included. Expressible under v0.5.1 through hasApplicabilityRule and a ConditionExpression, and not built. Related and belonging to the CRS filings rather than this one: Sheet 3 footnote 9 says DA and CCA customers are not subject to this Schedule's WFC of $0.00595 per kWh but pay it under Schedule DA-CRS or CCA-CRS, and neither us/ca/sce/electricity/cca-crs nor .../da-crs cancels this plan's WFC, so composing base plus CRS double-counts it. Note also that Sheet 2 footnote 1 names the DWRBC where Sheet 3 footnote 9 names the WFC; the two footnotes on the same filing disagree.
- The filed source is three sheets of the schedule, so any completeness claim here is bounded to those three. The PDF at [source].sha256 78fc8c0060f8be7add8a73170a0a21bec1c66078068738c1df96c9e76bdd2c94 is fifteen pages carrying five revisions each of Sheets 2, 3 and 7; the operative ones at this date are 90634-E, 90635-E and 90639-E, all AL 5654-E. Sheet 7 opens with SPECIAL CONDITIONS (Continued) and with 4. Billing Calculation: (Continued), which is direct evidence that Sheets 1 and 4 through 6 exist and are absent: Applicability, Territory, Special Conditions 1 through 3, and the first part of the Billing Calculation condition that Sheet 2 footnote 3 points to for the DWR Energy Credit. What those sheets price cannot be enumerated from this filing. Consequence worth noting: the Summer and Winter season months the model declares (--06 through --09 and --10 through --05) appear on none of the three sheets, so they are asserted from outside the filed source. They are inert today because every rate on Sheet 3 is identical Summer and Winter.

#### `d` @ 2026-03-20  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- SJV Pilot Bill Protection Discount (Sheet 8, Special Condition 8, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 90640-E): 20 percent off the bill in participation years 1 through 5, and 10 or 20 percent in years 6 through 10. Not built. This is work, not a blocker: under URPX v0.5.1 the pilot-participation gate is a urpx:ApplicabilityRule on the Price whose ConditionExpression compares a urpx:CustomOperandNode carrying urpx:customConditionProperty, and the 20 percent is the multiplier plus basedOnPrice pattern sce:care already applies to sce:d-2026-03-20-summary-discountable. That shape was built against this filing and conforms under v0.5.1. Two parts stay unexpressed: the 10-year window runs from the participant's own first Bill Protection Discount and urpx:Price carries only absolute dates, and Sheet 8 leaves the years 6 through 10 rate to a future SCE evaluation rather than stating it.
- Transitional Community Solar Discount (Sheet 9, Special Condition 9, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 90641-E): 20 percent monthly bill credit for SJV Pilot participants eligible for CSGT or DAC-GT when neither is available, over the same 10-year bill protection period, excluded for customers with onsite solar under SASH, DAC-SASH or NEM. Not built, on the same footing as Special Condition 8 above: gate and percentage are both expressible under v0.5.1 and this is work rather than a blocker, while the participation-relative 10-year window and the three program exclusions remain unexpressed.
- California Climate Credit carry-forward (Sheet 7, Special Condition 5, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 91033-E): the $(36.00) credit is priced, but Special Condition 5 also says any portion not consumed in the billing period in which it is applied rolls over to each subsequent billing period until fully consumed, and that is not expressed. The nearest v0.5.1 vocabulary is urpx:settlementInterval on the Ledger, an xsd:duration naming a fixed accumulation period, which does not state an unbounded roll-forward.
- Baseline Allocation, the boundary between the Baseline and Over Baseline energy rates (Sheets 2 and 3, footnote *): the schedule prices $0.18482 per kWh at or below the allocation and $0.28590 above it, but says only that the allocations are 'as described in Preliminary Statement, Part H, Baseline Service'. Part H is in no PDF in this corpus, so the kWh boundary cannot be sourced from this filing. This is a source gap, not a URPX gap.
- CORRECTION, not an absence: sce:d-2026-03-20-tiers-baseline-nonbaseline records bounds of 0 to 1.0 and above 1.0 while declaring referencesMetricInput to sce:d-2026-03-20-mi-energy, whose MetricSpecification carries unitOfMeasure urpx:kWh. Read in its declared unit the model says the baseline tier ends at 1.0 kWh. The intended meaning is a fraction of the customer's Baseline Allocation. Fixable now: pointing the ServiceTiers at a metric denominated urpx:ratio conforms under v0.5.1, verified against this filing. Precomputing absolute kWh bounds instead is blocked by the Part H source gap above. Any consumer that selects a tier by usage reads the baseline boundary as 1.0 kWh rather than the customer's allocation.
- CORRECTION, not an absence: the California Climate Credit's urpx:ApplicabilityRule asserts urpx:month --04 and --10, which this filing's source does not state. Advice Letter 5771-E struck both 'April and October' and '(April 1 and October 1)' from Special Condition 5 on Sheet 7, leaving an orphan closing parenthesis and (T) change markers on three lines; Sheets 2 and 3 say only 'semi-annually'. The months are carried over from the superseded 2025-11-15 source, which does state them. Either re-source them from a current SCE document or drop them.
- Base Services Charge component breakdown (Sheet 3): the BSC is carried as a single $0.794 per meter per day Price, while Sheet 3 breaks that row across the same columns it uses for the energy row, Distrbtn $0.424 and PPPC $0.370, with footnote ** naming a BSC Distribution Charge of $0.360 and a BSC Surcharge of $0.064 inside the $0.424. The nine energy columns are itemized as separate PriceDefinitions and the BSC row's are not. The $0.064 surcharge in particular is the figure Sheets 6 and 7 exempt DRAH, CARE and FERA customers from, and the CARE and FERA modifiers already carry it as a -0.064 credit with nothing here to subtract it from. Expressible now, purely additional PriceDefinitions summing to the same $0.794.

#### `tou-d-4-9pm` @ 2025-11-15  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Preliminary Statement Part H, paragraph 3: "The total billable baseline quantity is determined based upon the total number of winter days and total number of summer days during the billing period. Total billable baseline is then proportionately billed to winter and summer periods based upon the percentage of customer-consumption registered during the winter and summer seasons." The Tier 1 and Tier 2 brackets carry the Sheet 2 and Sheet 3 quantities as kWh-per-day bounds on a kWh MetricInput, qualified by season, as pge:e-tou-c does. Nothing in the filing states the daily basis, the multiplication by the days of each season in a billing period, or the proration by consumption, so a consumer must scale the bounds to the billing period itself.
- Preliminary Statement Part H, paragraph 4: "The standard Medical Baseline Allocation will be 16.5 kWh per day", provided "in addition to the applicable Baseline Allocation set forth in Paragraph 3". Not modeled: the Tier 1 and Tier 2 bounds here carry the paragraph 3 allocation only, and sce:medical-baseline carries the Schedule MB-E Wildfire Fund exemption and no allocation. An adjusting-bracket modifier of the form this filing's HPWH document uses (urpx-org/urpx#80) is the available route.
- SCE's Sunday-to-Monday holiday observance (Sheet 13, leaf 89841-E: 'When any holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday will be recognized as a holiday. No change will be made for holidays falling on Saturday.'). New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas in sce:tou-d-4-9pm-2025-11-15-holiday-calendar carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date. This version is in effect from 2025-11-15 until 2026-01-01, when no listed holiday falls on a Sunday, so the rule creates no Monday inside it and no dated observed holiday is carried; the 2026-03-20 filing carries them through 2040.
- Option 4-9 PM-CPP prices printed on this schedule's own rate block (Sheet 2, leaf 90669-E, heading 'Option 4-9 PM / Option 4-9 PM-CPP'): CPP Event Energy Charge $0.80000/kWh, Summer CPP Non-Event On-Peak Energy Credit $(0.15170)/kWh, Summer Season Maximum Available Credit $(0.55677)/kWh. None is modeled here and no overlay filing exists for the 4-9 PM variant. Expressible now: us/ca/sce/electricity/tou-d-prime-cpp/ ships the urpx:RatePlanModifier pattern for the PRIME equivalent, with the same three price roles. The $(0.55677) cap is specific to 4-9 PM and differs from PRIME's $(0.58794).
- Special Condition 7, Change of Rate Schedule (Sheet 18), governs how long a customer stays on this Option and is not in the model. Verbatim: "At any time, a Customer on this Schedule may elect to leave his/her Option (e.g., Option 4-9 PM, Option 5-8 PM, Option PRIME, Option A, Option B) and elect to either: (1) switch to another Option of this Schedule for which they are eligible, or (2) transfer from this Schedule to another applicable residential rate schedule. The elected rate change will become effective on the next regularly scheduled meter read date following the Customer's notice to SCE. Unless the Customer was placed on an Option by SCE (i.e., defaulted), the Customer shall not be allowed to make an additional change in rate until 12 months of service has been provided under the elected rate, unless otherwise specified in that rate schedule." urpx:minimumTenure exists on RatePlanVersion and the model does not set it; the re-entry half has no v0.5.1 form.

#### `tou-d-4-9pm` @ 2026-03-20  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Preliminary Statement Part H, paragraph 3: "The total billable baseline quantity is determined based upon the total number of winter days and total number of summer days during the billing period. Total billable baseline is then proportionately billed to winter and summer periods based upon the percentage of customer-consumption registered during the winter and summer seasons." The Tier 1 and Tier 2 brackets carry the Sheet 2 and Sheet 3 quantities as kWh-per-day bounds on a kWh MetricInput, qualified by season, as pge:e-tou-c does. Nothing in the filing states the daily basis, the multiplication by the days of each season in a billing period, or the proration by consumption, so a consumer must scale the bounds to the billing period itself.
- Preliminary Statement Part H, paragraph 4: "The standard Medical Baseline Allocation will be 16.5 kWh per day", provided "in addition to the applicable Baseline Allocation set forth in Paragraph 3". Not modeled: the Tier 1 and Tier 2 bounds here carry the paragraph 3 allocation only, and sce:medical-baseline carries the Schedule MB-E Wildfire Fund exemption and no allocation. An adjusting-bracket modifier of the form this filing's HPWH document uses (urpx-org/urpx#80) is the available route.
- San Joaquin Valley Disadvantaged Communities Pilot Bill Protection Discount (Sheet 25, Special Condition 15): 20 percent of the monthly bill in pilot years 1 through 5, then 10 percent or 20 percent in years 6 through 10 depending on SCE's evaluation of participants' energy cost savings. Not modeled: the discount is gated on SJV Pilot participation, and a Price carrying it without that gate would discount every customer on the schedule. The percentage-of-subtotal shape itself is the one sce/care and sce/fera already ship against this filing's urpx:PriceSummary. Special Condition 15 begins mid-clause on Sheet 25, so the years 1 through 5 figure is cross-cited to Schedule D's Sheet 8 rather than to this PDF.
- Transitional Community Solar Discount (Sheet 25, Special Condition 16): 20 percent monthly bill credit over the same 10-year bill protection period, for SJV Pilot participants who cannot take the Community Solar-Green Tariff or the Disadvantaged Communities-Green Tariff. Same pilot-participation gate as Special Condition 15.
- California Climate Credit carry-forward (Sheet 21, Special Condition 12): the $(36.00) per household credit is priced, but the roll-over of any portion not consumed in a billing period to subsequent billing periods is not expressed, so a consumer whose bill is smaller than the credit drops the remainder. Whether URPX can express it is not established: urpx:BillingCycle and settlementInterval exist, and SMUD NEM1 uses settlementInterval for its 12-month settlement period.
- Schedule TOU-D Sheet 13: "When any holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday will be recognized as a holiday. No change will be made for holidays falling on Saturday." New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas in sce:tou-d-4-9pm-2026-03-20-holiday-calendar carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day or Christmas is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2029-11-12, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2035-11-12, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02 and 2040-11-12. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.
- Base Services Charge component split (Sheet 3): the $0.794 per meter per day total is carried as one Price, but the sheet also prints its Distribution part of $0.424, itself a BSC Distribution Charge of $0.360 and a BSC Surcharge of $0.064, and a Public Purpose Programs part of $0.370. The $0.064 surcharge is the quantity Special Conditions 8, 9 and 11 exempt CARE, FERA and deed-restricted customers from, and those exemptions are priced in the sce/care, sce/fera and sce/deed-restricted modifiers, so the bill math is right today; this filing simply does not carry the split.
- Special Condition 7, Change of Rate Schedule (Sheet 18), governs how long a customer stays on this Option and is not in the model. Verbatim: "At any time, a Customer on this Schedule may elect to leave his/her Option (e.g., Option 4-9 PM, Option 5-8 PM, Option PRIME, Option A, Option B) and elect to either: (1) switch to another Option of this Schedule for which they are eligible, or (2) transfer from this Schedule to another applicable residential rate schedule. The elected rate change will become effective on the next regularly scheduled meter read date following the Customer's notice to SCE. Unless the Customer was placed on an Option by SCE (i.e., defaulted), the Customer shall not be allowed to make an additional change in rate until 12 months of service has been provided under the elected rate, unless otherwise specified in that rate schedule." urpx:minimumTenure exists on RatePlanVersion and the model does not set it; the re-entry half has no v0.5.1 form.
- Special Condition 10, Arrearage Management Plan (Sheet 19), in this filing's own PDF, changes what a customer on this Option owes and is not in the model. Verbatim: "Once enrolled in the AMP, SCE shall forgive 1/12 of an eligible residential Customer's arrearage after each on-time payment. After 12 on-time payments, SCE shall forgive the remaining arrearage debt up to a maximum of $8,000 per instance of enrollment." Eligibility is six months of SCE service, CARE or FERA enrollment, at least one on-time payment, and an arrearage of $500 or greater at least 90 days old. The corpus's existing treatment, recorded in this filing's own CoverageClaim Evaluation as governing "enrollment and account administration rather than price", is the reading LAYOUT.md section 4.4 does not support.

New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas in sce:tou-d-5-8pm-2026-03-20-holiday-calendar carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day or Christmas is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2029-11-12, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2035-11-12, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02 and 2040-11-12. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.
- Option 5-8 PM-CPP, printed on this filing's own Sheet 4: CPP Event Energy Charge $0.80000/kWh, Summer CPP Non-Event On-Peak Energy Credit $(0.15170)/kWh, and Summer Season Maximum Available Credit $(0.58794)/kWh, with the CPP Event Period defined on Sheets 12 and 13 as 4 p.m. to 9 p.m. Work, not a blocker: sce/tou-d-prime-cpp already ships exactly this as a urpx:RatePlanModifier with the same three figures, so what is missing is a sibling tou-d-5-8pm-cpp filing listed in applicable_modifiers, not vocabulary. Note that the Maximum Available Credit caps the sum of credits across a dual-participation program; prime-cpp carries it as a plain Price that nothing enforces, and URPX has no way to bound one credit by the value of another.
- Special Condition 15 (Sheet 25) San Joaquin Valley Pilot Bill Protection Discount and Special Condition 16 (Sheet 25) Transitional Community Solar Discount: a 20 percent monthly bill discount each, the SJV discount dropping to 10 percent for years 6 through 10 if the pilot evaluation finds savings. Neither is modeled. A percentage of a computed subtotal is expressible (urpx:PriceSummary plus a multiplier Price with basedOnPrice, as sce/care and sce/fera already do against this filing's summary-discountable), but both are gated on pilot participation, and a discount carried here without an enforceable participation gate would reduce the bill of every customer on the schedule, which is worse than omitting it. Partial source gap as well: Sheet 25 carries only the continuation of Special Condition 15, so the first-five-year discount rate is never stated in this PDF.
- Special Condition 12 (Sheet 21): "Any California Climate Credit not consumed in the billing period in which the credit is applied will roll over to each subsequent billing period until the credit is fully consumed." The $(36.00) per-household credit is modeled, with a urpx:ApplicabilityRule for April and October, but the carry-forward of an unconsumed balance is not. Whether URPX can express it is unsettled rather than closed: urpx:BillingCycle and urpx:settlementInterval exist in v0.5.1, and SMUD NEM1 uses urpx:settlementInterval for its 12-month settlement period.
- Special Condition 7, Change of Rate Schedule (Sheet 18), governs how long a customer stays on this Option and is not in the model. Verbatim: "At any time, a Customer on this Schedule may elect to leave his/her Option (e.g., Option 4-9 PM, Option 5-8 PM, Option PRIME, Option A, Option B) and elect to either: (1) switch to another Option of this Schedule for which they are eligible, or (2) transfer from this Schedule to another applicable residential rate schedule. The elected rate change will become effective on the next regularly scheduled meter read date following the Customer's notice to SCE. Unless the Customer was placed on an Option by SCE (i.e., defaulted), the Customer shall not be allowed to make an additional change in rate until 12 months of service has been provided under the elected rate, unless otherwise specified in that rate schedule." urpx:minimumTenure exists on RatePlanVersion and the model does not set it; the re-entry half has no v0.5.1 form.
- Special Condition 10, Arrearage Management Plan (Sheet 19), in this filing's own PDF, changes what a customer on this Option owes and is not in the model. Verbatim: "Once enrolled in the AMP, SCE shall forgive 1/12 of an eligible residential Customer's arrearage after each on-time payment. After 12 on-time payments, SCE shall forgive the remaining arrearage debt up to a maximum of $8,000 per instance of enrollment." Eligibility is six months of SCE service, CARE or FERA enrollment, at least one on-time payment, and an arrearage of $500 or greater at least 90 days old. The corpus's existing treatment, recorded in this filing's own CoverageClaim Evaluation as governing "enrollment and account administration rather than price", is the reading LAYOUT.md section 4.4 does not support.

#### `tou-d-5-8pm` @ 2026-06-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Preliminary Statement Part H, paragraph 3: "The total billable baseline quantity is determined based upon the total number of winter days and total number of summer days during the billing period. Total billable baseline is then proportionately billed to winter and summer periods based upon the percentage of customer-consumption registered during the winter and summer seasons." The Tier 1 and Tier 2 brackets carry the Sheet 2 and Sheet 3 quantities as kWh-per-day bounds on a kWh MetricInput, qualified by season, as pge:e-tou-c does. Nothing in the filing states the daily basis, the multiplication by the days of each season in a billing period, or the proration by consumption, so a consumer must scale the bounds to the billing period itself.
- Preliminary Statement Part H, paragraph 4: "The standard Medical Baseline Allocation will be 16.5 kWh per day", provided "in addition to the applicable Baseline Allocation set forth in Paragraph 3". Not modeled: the Tier 1 and Tier 2 bounds here carry the paragraph 3 allocation only, and sce:medical-baseline carries the Schedule MB-E Wildfire Fund exemption and no allocation. An adjusting-bracket modifier of the form this filing's HPWH document uses (urpx-org/urpx#80) is the available route.
- Option 5-8 PM-CPP, printed on this filing's own Sheet 4: CPP Event Energy Charge $0.80000/kWh, Summer On-Peak Energy Credit $(0.15170)/kWh outside CPP Events, and Summer Season Maximum Available Credit $(0.58794)/kWh, with the CPP periods defined in Special Condition 1 (Sheet 9) and the program in Special Condition 3 (Sheets 10 and 11). Work, not a blocker: sce/tou-d-prime-cpp ships the same three figures as a urpx:RatePlanModifier, so what is missing is a sibling filing listed in applicable_modifiers, not vocabulary.
- San Joaquin Valley Disadvantaged Communities Pilot Bill Protection Discount (Special Condition 13, Sheets 17 and 18): 20 percent of the monthly bill in pilot years 1 through 5, then 10 percent or 20 percent in years 6 through 10 depending on SCE's evaluation of participants' energy cost savings. Not modeled: the discount is gated on SJV Pilot participation, and a Price carrying it without that gate would discount every customer on the schedule. The percentage-of-subtotal shape itself is the one sce/care and sce/fera already ship against this filing's urpx:PriceSummary.
- Transitional Community Solar Discount (Special Condition 14, Sheet 18): 20 percent monthly bill credit over the same 10-year bill protection period, for SJV Pilot participants who cannot take the Community Solar-Green Tariff or the Disadvantaged Communities-Green Tariff. Same pilot-participation gate as Special Condition 13.
- California Climate Credit carry-forward (Sheet 16, Special Condition 11): the $(36.00) per household credit is priced, but the roll-over of any portion not consumed in a billing period to subsequent billing periods is not expressed, so a consumer whose bill is smaller than the credit drops the remainder. Whether URPX can express it is not established: urpx:BillingCycle and settlementInterval exist, and SMUD NEM1 uses settlementInterval for its 12-month settlement period.
- Schedule TOU-D Sheet 9: "When any holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday will be recognized as a holiday. No change will be made for holidays falling on Saturday." New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas in sce:tou-d-5-8pm-2026-06-01-holiday-calendar carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day or Christmas is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2029-11-12, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2035-11-12, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02 and 2040-11-12. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.
- Base Services Charge component split (Sheet 5): the $0.794 per meter per day total is carried as one Price, but the sheet also prints its Distribution part of $0.429, itself a BSC Distribution Charge of $0.360 and a BSC Surcharge of $0.069, and a Public Purpose Programs part of $0.365. The $0.069 surcharge is the quantity CARE, FERA and DRAH customers are exempt from; sce/care and sce/fera price that exemption as a separate credit and sce/deed-restricted does not. Expressible now, purely additional PriceDefinitions summing to the same $0.794.
- Special Condition 6, Change of Rate Schedule (Sheet 13), governs how long a customer stays on this Option and is not in the model. Verbatim: "At any time, a Customer on this Schedule may elect to leave his/her Option (e.g., Option 4-9 PM, Option 5-8 PM, Option PRIME) and elect to either: (1) switch to another Option of this Schedule for which they are eligible, or (2) transfer from this Schedule to another applicable residential rate schedule. Unless the Customer was placed on an Option by SCE (i.e., defaulted), the Customer shall not be allowed to make an additional change in rate until 12 months of service has been provided under the elected rate, unless otherwise specified in that rate schedule." urpx:minimumTenure exists on RatePlanVersion and the model does not set it; the re-entry half has no v0.5.1 form.
- Special Condition 9, Arrearage Management Plan (Sheet 14), in this filing's own PDF, changes what a customer on this Option owes and is not in the model. Verbatim: "Once enrolled in the AMP, SCE shall forgive 1/12 of an eligible residential Customer's arrearage after each on-time payment. After 12 on-time payments, SCE shall forgive the remaining arrearage debt up to a maximum of $8,000 per instance of enrollment." #### `tou-d-prime` @ 2025-11-15  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Option PRIME-CPP is priced on this filing's own rate sheet and is modeled nowhere at this effective date. Page 24 (Sheet 6, leaf 90673-E, AL 5654-E) heads the block "Option PRIME / Option PRIME-CPP" and prices a CPP Event Energy Charge of $0.80000/kWh, a Summer CPP Non-Event On-Peak Energy Credit of $(0.15170)/kWh, and a Summer Season Maximum Available Credit of $(0.55677)/kWh. This corpus carries PRIME-CPP as a sibling filing, but us/ca/sce/electricity/tou-d-prime-cpp/ has only a 2026-03-20 version, so no filing holds these three numbers at 2025-11-15. Work, not a blocker: either add the 2025-11-15 sibling, or express the two options as PriceSetAlternatives, a pattern other filings in this corpus already ship.
- The EV Meter Credit of $(0.446)/meter/day and the EV Submeter Credit of $(0.152)/meter/day (page 24 and page 29) are carried as unconditional Prices, so a consumer applies both to every Option PRIME customer. Schedule TOU-D Special Condition 6 gates the first on "a separate meter provided by SCE exclusively for the charging of Electric Vehicles", and Special Condition 19 gates the second on use of an EV submeter under D.22-08-024. Neither sheet is in this filing's PDF, which holds only Sheets 2-11 and 18; both were read from the full tariff book committed at us/ca/sce/electricity/tou-d-prime/versions/2026-03-20/. urpx:hasApplicabilityRule with a ConditionExpression expresses both under v0.5.1 and is precedented in this corpus.
- SCE's Sunday-to-Monday holiday observance (Schedule TOU-D Sheet 13: 'When any holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday will be recognized as a holiday. No change will be made for holidays falling on Saturday.'). New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas in sce:tou-d-prime-2025-11-15-holiday-calendar carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date. This version is in effect from 2025-11-15 until 2026-01-01, when no listed holiday falls on a Sunday, so the rule creates no Monday inside it and no dated observed holiday is carried; the 2026-03-20 filing carries them through 2040.
- The California Climate Credit is gated to April and October on no source in this filing. The model carries urpx:ApplicabilityRule urpx:month --04 and --10; page 24 footnote 4 says only "Applied on an equal basis, per household, semi-annually. See the Special Conditions of this Schedule for more information," and the Special Condition it points at is on a sheet this PDF omits. As printed in the 2026-03-20 tariff book, that Special Condition names no months either: it applies the credit "at the time the California Climate Credit is disbursed." Either source the two months and cite where, or drop the month gate and keep the semi-annual denomination. The same Special Condition adds that an unconsumed credit "will roll over to each subsequent billing period until the credit is fully consumed", which is not modeled and for which no carry-forward pattern is established in this corpus.
- The discountable PriceSummary sce:tou-d-prime-2025-11-15-summary-discountable mixes units. Its 22 members are twenty $/kWh prices plus the two $/meter/day EV credits, while the $/meter/day Base Services Charge is left out. A subtotal over its includesPrice members' unitPrice values adds dollars-per-day to dollars-per-kWh, so the CARE 32.5% and FERA 18.0% modifiers that compose against this summary take their percentage of a mixed-unit total. Splitting it into one unit-homogeneous summary per denominator fixes the arithmetic. Which components belong in the CARE and FERA base is a separate and unsettled question: Sheet 18 Special Conditions 8 and 9 say the discount applies to the bill before the PUCRF and separately grant BSC adjustments, without saying whether the Base Services Charge is inside or outside the percentage.
- Special Condition 5, PRIME Eligibility Requirements, carries a carve-out the model does not encode and the assessment dropped as governing "who may enroll, not what anything costs". Verbatim: "Beginning May 1, 2019, all Customers newly requesting to take service under Option PRIME or Option PRIME-CPP, except Customers served on Option A, Option A-CPP, Option B, Option B-CPP, or Schedule TOU-D-T or TOU-D-T-CPP effective March 20, 2020, must attest to having one or more of the following technologies to be eligible for service on Option PRIME or Option PRIME-CPP: (1) Electric Vehicle; (2) On-site behind-the-meter (BTM) energy storage system; (3) Electric heat pump system for water; and/or (4) Electric heat pump system for space heating". The model's second EligibilityRule is a four-leaf OR over storageDER, evChargingDER, airSourceHeatPump and a CustomOperandNode "Program Enrollment" = "Net Billing Tariff (NBT)": the two heat-pump technologies collapse to one leaf, the NBT leaf is in no Special Condition, and the grandfather carve-out is absent. This entry is already shipped on this filing and should be kept, not dropped. Sourcing caveat: Sheet 17 is not in this filing's own PDF, which holds Sheets 2 through 11 and Sheet 18; the text is read from the full book committed with the 2026-03-20 filing.
- Special Condition 7, Change of Rate Schedule (Sheet 18, in this filing's own PDF), bars an additional rate change "until 12 months of service has been provided under the elected rate" unless SCE defaulted the customer onto the Option. urpx:minimumTenure exists on RatePlanVersion and this filing does not set it.

#### `tou-d-prime` @ 2026-03-20  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Schedule TOU-D Sheet 13: "When any holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday will be recognized as a holiday. No change will be made for holidays falling on Saturday." New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas in sce:tou-d-prime-2026-03-20-holiday-calendar carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day or Christmas is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2029-11-12, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2035-11-12, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02 and 2040-11-12. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.
- Sheet 17 Special Condition 6 limits the EV Meter Credit of $(0.451) per meter per day to "customers with a separate meter provided by SCE exclusively for the charging of Electric Vehicles", and Sheet 26 Special Condition 19 limits the EV Submeter Credit of $(0.152) per meter per day to a customer whose EV submeter meets the Plug-In Electric Vehicle Submetering Protocol of D.22-08-024. Both are modeled as unconditional PriceDefinitions, so a consumer gives every Option PRIME customer both credits. v0.5.1 supplies the vocabulary: PriceDefinition and Price both carry urpx:hasApplicabilityRule and urpx:hasApplicabilityGroup, ApplicabilityRule carries urpx:hasConditionExpression, and a gated copy of this document conforms under SHACL validation with a CustomOperandNode for the metering arrangement. Not built. Note when building it that the gate would be carried declaratively, as a ConditionExpression in the document, so it binds only for a consumer that evaluates conditions.
- Sheet 25 Special Condition 15 prices the San Joaquin Valley Disadvantaged Communities Pilot Bill Protection Discount at "a 10 percent discount" or "a 20 percent discount" off the monthly electricity bill for years 6 through 10, and Special Condition 16 prices "a 20 percent Transitional Community Solar monthly bill credit for the 10-year bill protection period". Neither is modeled. Both are percentages of a bill subtotal, which v0.5.1 expresses as a urpx:PriceSummary plus a modifier Price carrying urpx:multiplier and urpx:basedOnPrice, gated on pilot participation. Two cautions before building it. The opening of Special Condition 15, which grants the discount and states its first-five-year rate, is not in this 26-sheet PDF: Sheet 24 ends at 14.e and Sheet 25 opens "15. ... (Continued)", so only the years 6 through 10 percentages are sourceable here. And the pilot-participation gate would be carried declaratively, so a document that asserts the discount before a consumer evaluates conditions would apply a 20 percent discount to every Option PRIME customer, which is worse than the current omission.
- urpx:PriceSummary sce:tou-d-prime-2026-03-20-summary-discountable, the subtotal that sce/care and sce/fera discount against, names 24 Prices in two different denominations: 22 in $/kWh, and two in $/Meter/Day (sce:price-ev-meter-credit-2026-03-20 at $(0.451) and sce:price-ev-submeter-credit-2026-03-20 at $(0.152), both per Sheets 6 and 7). A sum over the urpx:unitPrice values of its members adds dollars per day to dollars per kWh, so the CARE 32.5 percent of Sheet 18 Special Condition 8 and the FERA 18.0 percent of Special Condition 9 are percentages of a mixed-unit figure. Split it into two unit-homogeneous summaries with one modifier Price over each.
- Sheet 21 Special Condition 12: "Any California Climate Credit not consumed in the billing period in which the credit is applied will roll over to each subsequent billing period until the credit is fully consumed." The $(36.00) credit is modeled; the carry-forward is not. URPX v0.5.1 has no term for a balance carried until consumed. Its only ledger-balance term is urpx:settlementInterval (domain Ledger, range xsd:duration, "Period over which ledger balances are settled"), a finite period whose own skos:note example is the P12M NBT true-up; LedgerShape and BillingCycleShape are sh:closed and none of their other properties names a balance, and a scan of every ontology literal for carry, rollover, balance, banked and unconsumed finds no other balance term.
- The California Climate Credit's urpx:ApplicabilityRule asserts urpx:month "--04" and "--10". This filing's PDF names neither month for the credit: Sheets 6 and 7 footnote it only as "Applied on an equal basis, per household, semi-annually" and Sheet 21 Special Condition 12 names no month. The model is asserting more than the source supports. Either cite an external SCE source for April and October, or reduce the claim to the semi-annual statement the sheet actually makes.
- Special Condition 5, PRIME Eligibility Requirements (Sheet 17, in this filing's own PDF), is modeled inaccurately and its carve-out is unmodeled. Verbatim: "Beginning May 1, 2019, all Customers newly requesting to take service under Option PRIME or Option PRIME-CPP, except Customers served on Option A, Option A-CPP, Option B, Option B-CPP, or Schedule TOU-D-T or TOU-D-T-CPP effective March 20, 2020, must attest to having one or more of the following technologies ... (1) Electric Vehicle; (2) On-site behind-the-meter (BTM) energy storage system; (3) Electric heat pump system for water; and/or (4) Electric heat pump system for space heating". The model's four-leaf OR is storageDER, evChargingDER, airSourceHeatPump and a CustomOperandNode "Program Enrollment" = "Net Billing Tariff (NBT)": the heat-pump-water-heater path is absent, the NBT leaf is in no Special Condition, and the Option A / Option B / TOU-D-T carve-out is absent.
- Special Condition 7, Change of Rate Schedule (Sheet 18), bars an additional rate change "until 12 months of service has been provided under the elected rate" unless the Customer was defaulted onto the Option by SCE. urpx:minimumTenure exists on RatePlanVersion and this filing does not set it.
- Special Condition 10, Arrearage Management Plan (Sheet 19), in this filing's own PDF, changes what a customer on this Option owes and is not in the model. Verbatim: "Once enrolled in the AMP, SCE shall forgive 1/12 of an eligible residential Customer's arrearage after each on-time payment. After 12 on-time payments, SCE shall forgive the remaining arrearage debt up to a maximum of $8,000 per instance of enrollment." Eligibility is six months of SCE service, CARE or FERA enrollment, at least one on-time payment, and an arrearage of $500 or greater at least 90 days old. The corpus's existing treatment, recorded in this filing's own CoverageClaim Evaluation as governing "enrollment and account administration rather than price", is the reading LAYOUT.md section 4.4 does not support.
- Special Condition 6 and Special Condition 19 gate two Prices this filing carries unconditionally. Special Condition 6: "the PRIME Separately-Metered EV Option is applicable to customers with a separate meter provided by SCE exclusively for the charging of Electric Vehicles". Special Condition 19 gates the EV Submeter Credit on use of an EV submeter under D.22-08-024. The shipped deferral "Conditional applicability of EV Meter Credit / EV Submeter Credit" records this and should be kept.

#### `tou-d-prime` @ 2026-06-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Schedule TOU-D Sheet 9: "When any holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday will be recognized as a holiday. No change will be made for holidays falling on Saturday." New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas in sce:tou-d-prime-2026-06-01-holiday-calendar carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day or Christmas is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2029-11-12, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2035-11-12, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02 and 2040-11-12. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.
- Special Condition 5 (Sheet 12) limits the EV Meter Credit of $(0.461) per meter per day to "customers with a separate meter provided by SCE exclusively for the charging of Electric Vehicles, as defined in Rule 1", and Special Condition 17 (Sheet 19) limits the EV Submeter Credit of $(0.152) per meter per day to a customer whose EV submeter meets the Plug-In Electric Vehicle Submetering Protocol of Decision 22-08-024. Both are modeled as unconditional PriceDefinitions, so a consumer gives every Option PRIME customer both credits. v0.5.1 supplies the vocabulary: PriceDefinition and Price both carry urpx:hasApplicabilityRule, and ApplicabilityRule carries urpx:hasConditionExpression with a CustomOperandNode for the metering arrangement. Not built.
- San Joaquin Valley Disadvantaged Communities Pilot Bill Protection Discount (Special Condition 13, Sheets 17 and 18): 20 percent of the monthly bill in pilot years 1 through 5, then 10 percent or 20 percent in years 6 through 10 depending on SCE's evaluation of participants' energy cost savings. Not modeled: the discount is gated on SJV Pilot participation, and a Price carrying it without that gate would discount every customer on the schedule. The percentage-of-subtotal shape itself is the one sce/care and sce/fera already ship against this filing's urpx:PriceSummary.
- Transitional Community Solar Discount (Special Condition 14, Sheet 18): 20 percent monthly bill credit over the same 10-year bill protection period, for SJV Pilot participants who cannot take the Community Solar-Green Tariff or the Disadvantaged Communities-Green Tariff. Same pilot-participation gate as Special Condition 13.
- urpx:PriceSummary sce:tou-d-prime-2026-06-01-summary-discountable, the subtotal that sce/care and sce/fera discount against, names 24 Prices in two different denominations: 22 in $/kWh, and two in $/Meter/Day (sce:price-ev-meter-credit-2026-06-01 at $(0.461) and sce:price-ev-submeter-credit-2026-06-01 at $(0.152), both per Sheets 6 and 7). A sum over the urpx:unitPrice values of its members adds dollars per day to dollars per kWh, so the CARE 32.5 percent of Special Condition 7 and the FERA 18.0 percent of Special Condition 8 are percentages of a mixed-unit figure. Split it into two unit-homogeneous summaries with one modifier Price over each.
- California Climate Credit carry-forward (Sheet 16, Special Condition 11): the $(36.00) per household credit is priced, but the roll-over of any portion not consumed in a billing period to subsequent billing periods is not expressed, so a consumer whose bill is smaller than the credit drops the remainder. Whether URPX can express it is not established: urpx:BillingCycle and settlementInterval exist, and SMUD NEM1 uses settlementInterval for its 12-month settlement period.
- The California Climate Credit's urpx:ApplicabilityRule asserts urpx:month "--04" and "--10". This filing's PDF names neither month: the rate tables footnote the credit only as "Applied on an equal basis, per household, semi-annually" and Special Condition 11 (Sheet 16) names no month. Either cite an external SCE source for April and October, or reduce the claim to the semi-annual statement the sheets make.
- Special Condition 4, PRIME Eligibility Requirements (Sheet 12), is modeled inaccurately. Verbatim: "Beginning May 1, 2019, all Customers newly requesting to take service under Option PRIME or Option PRIME-CPP effective March 20, 2020, must attest to having one or more of the following technologies to be eligible for service on Option PRIME or Option PRIME-CPP: (1) Electric Vehicle; (2) On-site behind-the-meter (BTM) energy storage system; (3) Electric heat pump system for water; and/or (4) Electric heat pump system for space heating". The model's four-leaf OR is storageDER, evChargingDER, airSourceHeatPump and a CustomOperandNode "Program Enrollment" = "Net Billing Tariff (NBT)": the heat-pump-water-heater path is absent and the NBT leaf is in no Special Condition.
- Special Condition 6, Change of Rate Schedule (Sheet 13), governs how long a customer stays on this Option and is not in the model. Verbatim: "At any time, a Customer on this Schedule may elect to leave his/her Option (e.g., Option 4-9 PM, Option 5-8 PM, Option PRIME) and elect to either: (1) switch to another Option of this Schedule for which they are eligible, or (2) transfer from this Schedule to another applicable residential rate schedule. The elected rate change will become effective on the next regularly scheduled meter read date following the Customer's notice to SCE. Unless the Customer was placed on an Option by SCE (i.e., defaulted), the Customer shall not be allowed to make an additional change in rate until 12 months of service has been provided under the elected rate, unless otherwise specified in that rate schedule." urpx:minimumTenure exists on RatePlanVersion and the model does not set it; the re-entry half has no v0.5.1 form.
- Special Condition 9, Arrearage Management Plan (Sheet 14), in this filing's own PDF, changes what a customer on this Option owes and is not in the model. Verbatim: "Once enrolled in the AMP, SCE shall forgive 1/12 of an eligible residential Customer's arrearage after each on-time payment. After 12 on-time payments, SCE shall forgive the remaining arrearage debt up to a maximum of $8,000 per instance of enrollment." Eligibility is six months of SCE service, CARE or FERA enrollment, at least one on-time payment, and an arrearage of $500 or greater at least 90 days old.

#### `rtc` @ 2026-01-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- City Light's residential Time of Use page: a NERC holiday that falls on a Sunday "is observed on the following Monday"; one on a Saturday "remains on that Saturday". New Year's Day, Independence Day and Christmas Day in scl:rtc-2026-01-01-holidays carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day or Christmas Day is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.

_No applicable modifiers declared._

### SDGE

#### `dr` @ 2026-04-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Sheet 2 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 64654-E) publishes a DR-MB rate table for Medical Baseline customers: Base Services Charge $0.79343/day (note 6: the MB program provides no BSC discount), UDC $0.23169/kWh up to 130% of baseline and $0.34061/kWh above it, DWR-BC + WF-NBC $0.00000 (note 3 exempts MB customers), EECC $0.18274/kWh, and Total Effective MB Rates of $0.32475/kWh and $0.41010/kWh. No SDG&E Medical Baseline overlay exists anywhere in this corpus, so an MB customer on Schedule DR cannot be billed from this data. Expressible under v0.5.1 in the CARE and FERA shape already shipping against this filing: a RatePlanModifierVersion carrying the DWR-BC and WF-NBC exemption credits plus a percentage Price using multiplier and basedOnPrice over an MB-discountable PriceSummary. Work, not a blocker.
- Sheet 3 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 64338-E): "A Franchise Fee Differential of 5.78% will be applied to the monthly billings calculated under this schedule for all customers within the corporate limits of the City of San Diego." Not modeled. Expressible under v0.5.1 as a Price with multiplier 0.0578 and basedOnPrice over a PriceSummary of the monthly billing, chargeType urpx:taxMunicipal, gated on hasMunicipality in a ConditionExpression; the gate carries the whole restriction, since an ungated Price would charge the 5.78% to every DR customer, inside the city limits or not.
- Special Condition 3 on Sheet 4 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 64339-E) publishes the Baseline Allowance table in kWh/day for Coastal, Inland, Mountain and Desert zones: Basic Allowance summer 9.0, 10.4, 13.6, 15.9 and winter 9.2, 9.6, 12.9, 10.9; All Electric summer 6.0, 8.7, 15.2, 17.0 and winter 8.8, 12.2, 22.1, 17.1. None of the 16 values is in the model, and sdge:tiers-baseline-130pct instead bounds Tier 1 at a dimensionless 1.3 while referencing sdge:dr-2026-04-01-mi-energy, whose unitOfMeasure is urpx:kWh, so the document as serialized says Tier 1 ends at 1.3 kWh. Both fixes validate against the v0.5.1 shapes: point ServiceTiers.referencesMetricInput at a MetricSpecification with unitOfMeasure urpx:ratio, keeping 1.3 and making it mean a fraction of the customer's allowance; or carry the absolute allowances as per-season ServiceBrackets using ServiceBracketShape appliesInSeason. Prefer the ratio route, because the allowance is denominated per day against a billing-period quantity.
- Special Condition 9.b, the Direct Access and Community Choice Aggregation bill path, is a computation rule on this schedule's own charges and is in no part of the model. The assessment read urpx:serviceRole urpx:bundledServiceRole as scoping the version out of it, but that is a statement the model makes, not one the source makes: Sheet 1 note (2) says "Differences in total rates paid by Direct Access (DA) and Community Choice Aggregation (CCA) customers are identified in Schedule DA-CRS and CCA-CRS, respectively" and Special Condition 9.b routes those customers through the Cost Responsibility Surcharge. The crediting half operates on this filing's own EECC Prices; the CRS half is another schedule's and no SDG&E DA-CRS or CCA-CRS filing exists in this corpus. The assessment itself asked for a corpus-wide ruling here.
- Special Condition 1 (Sheet 3) states load-check and rebilling terms on this schedule, including an 11-month rebilling window, and nothing in the model corresponds to them.

Base deferrals:
- Sheet 2 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 64800-E) publishes a DR-MB rate table for Medical Baseline customers: Base Services Charge $0.79343/day (note 6: the MB program provides no BSC discount), UDC $0.22285/kWh up to 130% of baseline and $0.32948/kWh above it, DWR-BC + WF-NBC $0.00000 (note 3 exempts MB customers), EECC $0.18274/kWh, and Total Effective MB Rates of $0.31782/kWh and $0.40138/kWh. No SDG&E Medical Baseline overlay exists anywhere in this corpus, so an MB customer on Schedule DR cannot be billed from this data. Expressible under v0.5.1 in the CARE and FERA shape already shipping against this filing: a RatePlanModifierVersion carrying the DWR-BC and WF-NBC exemption credits plus a percentage Price using multiplier and basedOnPrice over an MB-discountable PriceSummary. Work, not a blocker.
- Sheet 3 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 64338-E): "A Franchise Fee Differential of 5.78% will be applied to the monthly billings calculated under this schedule for all customers within the corporate limits of the City of San Diego." Not modeled. Expressible under v0.5.1 as a Price with multiplier 0.0578 and basedOnPrice over a PriceSummary of the monthly billing, chargeType urpx:taxMunicipal, gated on hasMunicipality in a ConditionExpression; the gate carries the whole restriction, since an ungated Price would charge the 5.78% to every DR customer, inside the city limits or not.
- Special Condition 3 on Sheet 4 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 64339-E) publishes the Baseline Allowance table in kWh/day for Coastal, Inland, Mountain and Desert zones: Basic Allowance summer 9.0, 10.4, 13.6, 15.9 and winter 9.2, 9.6, 12.9, 10.9; All Electric summer 6.0, 8.7, 15.2, 17.0 and winter 8.8, 12.2, 22.1, 17.1. None of the 16 values is in the model, and sdge:dr-2026-06-01-tiers-baseline-130pct instead bounds Tier 1 at a dimensionless 1.3 while referencing sdge:dr-2026-06-01-mi-energy, whose unitOfMeasure is urpx:kWh, so the document as serialized says Tier 1 ends at 1.3 kWh. Both fixes validate against the v0.5.1 shapes: point ServiceTiers.referencesMetricInput at a MetricSpecification with unitOfMeasure urpx:ratio, keeping 1.3 and making it mean a fraction of the customer's allowance; or carry the absolute allowances as per-season ServiceBrackets using ServiceBracketShape appliesInSeason. Prefer the ratio route, because the allowance is denominated per day against a billing-period quantity.
- Special Condition 9.b, the Direct Access and Community Choice Aggregation bill path, is a computation rule on this schedule's own charges and is in no part of the model. The assessment read urpx:serviceRole urpx:bundledServiceRole as scoping the version out of it, but that is a statement the model makes, not one the source makes: Sheet 2 note (2) says "Differences in total rates paid by Direct Access (DA) and Community Choice Aggregation (CCA) customers are identified in Schedule DA-CRS and CCA-CRS, respectively" and Special Condition 9.b routes those customers through the Cost Responsibility Surcharge. The crediting half operates on this filing's own EECC Prices; the CRS half is another schedule's and no SDG&E DA-CRS or CCA-CRS filing exists in this corpus. The assessment itself asked for a corpus-wide ruling here.
- Special Condition 1 (Sheet 3) states load-check and rebilling terms on this schedule, including an 11-month rebilling window, and nothing in the model corresponds to them.

| `drah` | ~ present-incomplete | `us/ca/sdge/electricity/drah/versions/2026-09-30` |

#### `dr-ses` @ 2026-04-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Franchise Fee Differential of 5.78 percent on the monthly billings calculated under this schedule, for customers inside the corporate limits of the City of San Diego. Source: Sheet 2, Revised Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 64348-E, AL 4791-E, under the heading Franchise Fee Differential. Not a vocabulary gap under v0.5.1: express it as a Price carrying urpx:multiplier 0.0578 and urpx:basedOnPrice naming a urpx:PriceSummary over this filing's own charges, chargeType urpx:taxMunicipal, gated on urpx:hasMunicipality in a ConditionExpression. This filing already carries two working PriceSummary nodes for the CARE and FERA discounts, so the subtotal machinery exists. Unbuilt: no SDG&E filing in this corpus prices it. Before building it, note that the ConditionExpression carries the whole of the restriction: a Price that does not carry it, or a consumer that does not evaluate it, applies the 5.78 percent to every DR-SES customer, most of whom are outside the city, trading a known omission for a confident wrong number.
- Medical Baseline customers are exempt from the California Wildfire Fund Non-Bypassable Charge, so the $0.00591 per kWh WF-NBC this filing carries is not billed to them. Source: Sheet 1, Revised Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 64657-E, footnote (3), 'DWR-BC and WF-NBC charges do not apply to CARE or Medical Baseline customers'; the DWR-BC half is moot because its rate is 0.00000. Expressible now and already precedented in this corpus: the SDG&E CARE overlay at us/ca/sdge/electricity/care/versions/2025-10-01/ carries sdge:care-price-wf-nbc-exemption-2025-10-01 at -0.00591 and lists sdge:dr-ses in urpx:applicableToRatePlan, so the identical shape works for Medical Baseline. Unbuilt because this corpus holds no SDG&E Medical Baseline overlay at all (SCE has one at us/ca/sce/electricity/medical-baseline/; SDG&E has none) and Schedule E-MEDICAL, which Special Condition 16 cites, is not in this repo. Note that DR-SES publishes no reduced Medical Baseline energy or BSC rates: Sheet 1 footnote (5) says the Medical Baseline program provides no BSC discount, so this exemption is the whole of the Medical Baseline gap on this schedule.
- SDG&E Electric Rule 1 (DEFINITIONS), Sheet 6, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 61672-E, HOLIDAYS: "When a Holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday shall be defined as a Holiday. No change will be made for Holidays falling on Saturday." New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas Day in sdge:dr-ses-2026-04-01-holidays carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day or Christmas Day is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2029-11-12, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2035-11-12, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02 and 2040-11-12. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.
- Sheet 1's second rate table decomposes the Base Services Charge into UDC sub-components (Distr 0.63391 + PPP 0.02913 + LGC 0.13039 = 0.79343 standard; 0.06674 + 0.00000 + 0.13039 = 0.19713 CARE; 0.23736 + 0.02913 + 0.13039 = 0.39688 DRAH and FERA). The model carries only the 0.79343 total, while decomposing the energy UDC into all 7 published sub-components on the same table. The billed amount is unaffected, so this costs no accuracy today; it costs the ability to answer what share of the fixed charge is distribution. Trivially expressible (three more PriceDefinitions and Prices on the existing BSC ledger). Recorded here because it is real, but it is a corpus-wide convention rather than a DR-SES decision: every SDG&E base plan with a BSC ledger carries a single PriceDefinition on it, so settle it once for SDG&E rather than plan by plan.
- Special Condition 5 bars re-entry after leaving and the model records the wrong thing. Verbatim: "If a customer elects to discontinue service on Schedule DR-SES, the customer will not be permitted to return to Schedule DR-SES for a period of 12 consecutive months." The RatePlanVersion carries urpx:minimumTenure P12M, which the v0.5.1 ontology defines as "The minimum duration a customer must remain enrolled in a rate plan, program, or service offering". DR-SES imposes no such commitment, so the re-entry bar is unmodeled and the property that is set asserts something the schedule does not say. The sibling ev-tou and ev-tou-2 filings both record this provision; this one records nothing, and its deferrals array is empty.

Sheet No. 65155-E, which shows the Wildfire Non-Bypassable Charge and the DWR Bond Charge as N/A in the DR-SES-MB column; the DWR-BC half is moot because its rate is 0.00000. Sheet 2 (Original Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 65153-E) also prints a DR-SES-MB Total Effective Energy Rates column, which its note 2 calls illustrative and attributes to discounts under Schedule E-MEDICAL: each cell is 80 percent of the DR-SES-MB Total Energy Rate beside it, for example $0.59482 against $0.74353 summer on-peak for bundled customers. The DR-SES-MB Base Services Charge is the standard $0.79343 per day. The WF-NBC exemption is expressible now and already precedented in this corpus: the SDG&E CARE overlay at us/ca/sdge/electricity/care/versions/2025-10-01/ carries sdge:care-price-wf-nbc-exemption-2025-10-01 at -0.00591 and lists sdge:dr-ses in urpx:applicableToRatePlan, so the identical shape works for Medical Baseline. Unbuilt because this corpus holds no SDG&E Medical Baseline overlay at all (SCE has one at us/ca/sce/electricity/medical-baseline/; SDG&E has none) and Schedule E-MEDICAL, which General Special Condition 15 cites, is not in this repo.
- SDG&E Electric Rule 1 (DEFINITIONS), Sheet 6, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 61672-E, HOLIDAYS: "When a Holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday shall be defined as a Holiday. No change will be made for Holidays falling on Saturday." New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas Day in sdge:dr-ses-2026-09-30-holidays carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day or Christmas Day is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2029-11-12, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2035-11-12, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02 and 2040-11-12. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.
- Sheet 3's Detailed UDC Rates table decomposes the Base Services Charge into UDC sub-components (Distr 0.63391 + PPP 0.02913 + LGC 0.13039 = 0.79343 standard; 0.06674 + 0.00000 + 0.13039 = 0.19713 CARE; 0.23736 + 0.02913 + 0.13039 = 0.39688 DRAH and FERA). The model carries only the 0.79343 total, while decomposing the energy UDC into its published sub-components on the same table. The billed amount is unaffected, so this costs no accuracy today; it costs the ability to answer what share of the fixed charge is distribution. Trivially expressible (three more PriceDefinitions and Prices on the existing BSC ledger). Recorded here because it is real, but it is a corpus-wide convention rather than a DR-SES decision: every SDG&E base plan with a BSC ledger carries a single PriceDefinition on it, so settle it once for SDG&E rather than plan by plan.
- General Special Condition 5 bars re-entry after leaving. Verbatim: "If a customer elects to discontinue service on Schedule DR-SES, the customer will not be permitted to return to Schedule DR-SES for a period of 12 consecutive months." EECC Special Condition 3, carried into this schedule from the retired Schedule EECC, adds that a customer receiving optional service "may elect to change to another applicable rate schedule, but only after receiving service on this Schedule for at least 12 consecutive months, except as specified in EECC Special Condition 4", and bars return for one year unless returning from a Direct Access Provider or a CCA. The RatePlanVersion carries urpx:minimumTenure P12M, which the v0.5.1 ontology defines as "The minimum duration a customer must remain enrolled in a rate plan, program, or service offering"; from this version EECC Special Condition 3 supports it. Neither re-entry bar is modeled. The sibling ev-tou filing records its own re-entry bar in its deferrals.
