| `drah` | ~ present-incomplete | `us/ca/sdge/electricity/drah/versions/2026-09-30` |
| `fera` | ~ present-incomplete | `us/ca/sdge/electricity/fera/versions/2026-06-01` |

#### `ev-tou` @ 2026-04-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Franchise Fee Differential of 5.78 percent, Sheet 2 (Cal. P.U.C. Sheet 64371-E): "A Franchise Fee Differential of 5.78% will be applied to the monthly billings calculated under this schedule for all customers within the corporate limits of the City of San Diego. Such Franchise Fee Differential shall be so indicated and added as a separate item to bills rendered to such customers." Not modeled. Expressible under URPX v0.5.1: a Price with multiplier 0.0578 and basedOnPrice naming a PriceSummary over this schedule's billed components, chargeType urpx:taxMunicipal, gated on urpx:hasMunicipality inside a ConditionExpression. Left unbuilt because the restriction to the City of San Diego would live only in that ConditionExpression and nowhere in the Price arithmetic: the Price would read as 5.78 percent on every EV-TOU bill rather than only on bills inside the corporate limits, turning a known omission into a confident wrong number for everyone else.
- Minimum Bill discount for CARE, Medical Baseline and FERA customers, Sheet 2 (64371-E): "The minimum bill to recover Distribution and TRAC costs is calculated as the minimum bill charge of $0.413 per day times the number of days in the billing cycle with a 50% discount applied for CARE, Medical Baseline, or Family Electric Rate Assistance Program (FERA) customers resulting in a minimum bill of $0.207 per day." The model carries only the $0.413 standard rate. The previous claim that the discount arrives through overlay composition is false, and was checked rather than assumed: sdge:care, sdge:fera and sdge:drah all list sdge:ev-tou in applicableToRatePlan, and each delivers a Base Services Charge adjustment (-0.59630, -0.39655 and -0.39655 per day), a charge Schedule EV-TOU does not have. Composing them onto this plan credits a nonexistent charge and never yields $0.207. Expressible under v0.5.1 as a Price with multiplier -0.5 and basedOnPrice naming this filing's Minimum Bill Price, whose identifier is still sdge:ev-tou-2026-04-01-price-base-services-charge from the EV-TOU-5 derivation.
- SDG&E Electric Rule 1 (DEFINITIONS), Sheet 6, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 61672-E, HOLIDAYS: "When a Holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday shall be defined as a Holiday. No change will be made for Holidays falling on Saturday." New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas Day in sdge:ev-tou-2026-04-01-holidays carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day or Christmas Day is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2029-11-12, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2035-11-12, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02 and 2040-11-12. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.
- Direct Access and Community Choice Aggregation bill path, Special Condition 7.b, Sheet 4 (37010-E): "The bills for a DA and CCA Customer will be calculated as if they were a UDC Bundled Service Customer, then crediting the bill by the amount of the EECC component, and DWR-BC component for continuous DA customers that are exempt from the DWR-BC charge, as determined for a UDC Bundled Customer, and including the appropriate Cost Responsibility Surcharge (CRS), if applicable." The credited-back amounts are this schedule's own EECC and DWR-BC prices and are not modeled; they are expressible under v0.5.1 as Prices with multiplier -1 and basedOnPrice naming each EECC Price, which the pinned consumer resolves. The Cost Responsibility Surcharge named in the same condition is priced in Schedules DA-CRS and CCA-CRS and is out of scope for this filing, as Sheet 1 Note (2) confirms. This replaces the previous "PCIA recovery for CCA-served customers (Special Condition 9)" entry, which misdescribed the source: SC 9 is a disclosure that bundled EECC rates already embed the PCIA, it states no rate, and it is about bundled customers rather than CCA customers.
- Sheet 1 (64665-E) Note (3): "DWR-BC and WF-NBC charges do not apply to CARE or Medical Baseline customers." The CARE half is carried by the sdge:care modifier, which prices a WF-NBC exemption of -0.00591 and a DWR-BC exemption of -0.00000. The Medical Baseline half is carried nowhere: this corpus holds no SDG&E Medical Baseline or Schedule E-MEDICAL filing, so a Medical Baseline customer on this schedule is billed the 0.00591 WF-NBC that Sheet 1 exempts, on top of the full rather than halved minimum bill. The blocker is a missing source filing, not missing vocabulary.
- Special Condition 6, Optional Billing - Utility Option, is a bill-computation rule on this schedule and the shipped deferral recording it should be kept rather than dropped as "utility-discretionary policy, not a rate component". It lets the utility bill "the lower of the bill under this schedule or the bill under their regularly applicable non-TOU schedule" for a stated run of months at the utility's sole option. The lower-of-two-bills arithmetic has no v0.5.1 form, so it is a blocked deferral, not unbuilt work.
- Special Condition 5's bar on returning to the schedule for one year after leaving is recorded in this filing's [extraction].notes and in no ConditionExpression or property of the model; urpx:minimumTenure covers only the stay, not the re-entry bar.
- Special Condition 7.b, the Direct Access and Community Choice Aggregation bill path (Sheet 4), operates on this filing's own EECC and DWR-BC Prices. Verbatim: "The bills for a DA and CCA Customer will be calculated as if they were a UDC Bundled Service Customer, then crediting the bill by the amount of the EECC component, and DWR-BC component for continuous DA customers". The crediting half is expressible now with multiplier -1 and basedOnPrice; the Cost Responsibility Surcharge half is another schedule's and SDG&E has no DA-CRS or CCA-CRS filing in this corpus.

#### `ev-tou-2` @ 2026-04-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- SDG&E Electric Rule 1 (DEFINITIONS), Sheet 6, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 61672-E, HOLIDAYS: "When a Holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday shall be defined as a Holiday. No change will be made for Holidays falling on Saturday." New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas Day in sdge:ev-tou-2-2026-04-01-holidays carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day or Christmas Day is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2029-11-12, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2035-11-12, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02 and 2040-11-12. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.
- Franchise Fee Differential is not modeled. Sheet 2 (64374-E): 'A Franchise Fee Differential of 5.78% will be applied to the monthly billings calculated under this schedule for all customers within the corporate limits of the City of San Diego.' Expressible under v0.5.1 as a Price with a multiplier and basedOnPrice over a PriceSummary, chargeType urpx:taxMunicipal, gated on hasMunicipality. Unbuilt for two reasons, not for want of vocabulary: the gate on hasMunicipality carries the City of San Diego limit declaratively, so a consumer that does not evaluate it applies the 5.78% to every customer on the schedule; and the fee covers the per-day Base Services Charge as well as the per-kWh charges, so it needs two Prices over two new unit-homogeneous summaries rather than the existing CARE and FERA subtotals, which both exclude the BSC.
- California Climate Credit is not modeled. Sheet 1 (64666-E) applicability: 'Customers on this schedule may also qualify for a semi-annual California Climate Credit $(49.36) per Schedule GHG-ARR.' The pattern ships in this corpus at sce:d-2026-03-20-pd-climate-credit: chargeType urpx:creditEnergy, an ApplicabilityRule with month --04 and --10, and a Price of unitPrice -36.00. What blocks copying it here is a source gap, not a vocabulary gap: this PDF says only 'semi-annual' and never names the months, and Schedule GHG-ARR is not in this corpus. urpx:intervalDuration P6M expresses the cadence without sourcing months.
- The Medical Baseline exemption is not modeled. Sheet 1 (64666-E) footnote (3): 'DWR-BC and WF-NBC charges do not apply to CARE or Medical Baseline customers.' The CARE half is carried by the CARE overlay (sdge:care-price-wf-nbc-exemption-2025-10-01 at -0.00591, sdge:care-price-dwr-bc-exemption-2025-10-01 at -0.00000); the Medical Baseline half is carried nowhere, and this corpus holds no SDG&E medical-baseline filing. Expressible the same way. Settle at corpus level first: sdge/ev-tou-5@2026-04-01 has the identical footnote, the identical omission, and complete = true.
- Special Condition 8.b, the Direct Access and Community Choice Aggregation bill path, is not modeled: the bill is computed as though bundled, then credited by the EECC and DWR-BC components, with the applicable Cost Responsibility Surcharge added. The crediting half is expressible now, as a multiplier -1 Price with basedOnPrice naming this filing's six EECC Prices. The CRS half belongs to another schedule: Sheet 1 footnote (2) sends the reader to Schedule DA-CRS and CCA-CRS, and this corpus holds neither for SDG&E. The corpus convention puts CRS in a separate overlay filing, as us/ca/sce/electricity/cca-crs and da-crs do, so the likely resolution is a new SDG&E CRS filing rather than work here.
- The model asserts eligibility this schedule does not grant. sdge:er-qualifying-vehicle-or-nbt-ev-tou-2 adds a Schedule NBT path ('customers enrolled on Schedule NBT for whom EV-TOU-5 is the otherwise-applicable schedule qualify regardless of vehicle ownership') as a live operand of an orOperator, but 'NBT' and 'Net Billing' appear nowhere in Sheets 1 to 5, and Sheet 5 Special Condition 9 names Schedules NEM, NEM-BIO and NEM-FC instead. That is a model-versus-source contradiction imported from the EV-TOU-5 derivation, and it has to be resolved before this filing can be called complete. Separately, and not a coverage question: the same derivation left ten 'EV-TOU-5' strings in names and descriptions, including both EligibilityRule names and the TRAC PriceDefinition name, and two PriceSummary names claiming 24 and 26 components against actual memberships of 19 and 21 (24 and 26 are EV-TOU-5's real counts; the memberships here are correct). Fix the strings as lint; they bear on nothing priced.
- Special Condition 7, Optional Billing - Utility Option, is a bill-computation rule on this schedule's own charges and the shipped deferral recording it should be kept. Verbatim, it lets the utility bill "the lower of the bill under this schedule or the bill under their regularly applicable non-TOU schedule for three consecutive months", and "This option is solely the utility's and shall in no way obligate the utility." The lower-of-two-bills arithmetic has no v0.5.1 form, so this is a blocked deferral rather than a dropped one.
- Special Condition 8.b, the Direct Access and Community Choice Aggregation bill path (Sheet 4), operates on this filing's own EECC and DWR-BC Prices: bills are "calculated as if they were a UDC Bundled Service Customer, then crediting the bill by the amount of the EECC component and DWR-BC component ... and including the appropriate Cost Responsibility Surcharge (CRS), if applicable." The crediting half is expressible now; the CRS half points at schedules this corpus does not hold.

#### `ev-tou-5` @ 2026-04-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Franchise Fee Differential 5.78% (Sheet 4, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 64379-E): applied to the monthly billings calculated under this schedule for all customers within the corporate limits of the City of San Diego. Expressible under URPX v0.5.1 and SHACL-validated against the v0.5.1 candidate: a Price with multiplier 0.0578, chargeType urpx:taxMunicipal and basedOnPrice naming a PriceSummary, gated by an ApplicabilityRule. It needs two unit-homogeneous summaries, one over the 26 per-kWh Prices and one over the per-day Base Services Charge, because the fee applies to the whole bill and a PriceSummary carries no unit conversion across the Prices it includes. Unbuilt rather than unbuildable: the restriction to the city limits lives in the ApplicabilityRule condition rather than in the price, so a consumer that does not evaluate that condition charges the fee to every customer on the schedule, not only those inside the city.
- Medical Baseline rates (Sheet 2, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 64668-E, block marked N as new in this filing): the schedule prints a Total Effective MB Rate for all six TOU cells, exactly 0.80 x the Total Rate in every cell, and note (3) exempts Medical Baseline customers from DWR-BC + WF-NBC, worth $0.00591/kWh. Neither the exemption nor the differential is modeled here or anywhere in the corpus; there is no sdge medical-baseline modifier filing and applicable_modifiers does not list one. The schedule states the discount is identified in Schedule E-MEDICAL, so the corpus pattern is the CARE and FERA one: host an MB-discountable PriceSummary in this filing and carry the percentage in an E-MEDICAL modifier. The summary validates under v0.5.1; sce/medical-baseline is the shape precedent.
- SDG&E Electric Rule 1 (DEFINITIONS), Sheet 6, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 61672-E, HOLIDAYS: "When a Holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday shall be defined as a Holiday. No change will be made for Holidays falling on Saturday." New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas Day in sdge:ev-tou-5-2026-04-01-holidays carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day or Christmas Day is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2029-11-12, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2035-11-12, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02 and 2040-11-12. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.
- Direct Access and Community Choice Aggregation billing (Special Condition 9.b, Sheet 6, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 63296-E): a DA or CCA customer's bill is the bundled bill credited by the EECC component, and by the DWR-BC component for continuous DA customers exempt from that charge, plus the applicable Cost Responsibility Surcharge. Nothing in this model expresses that, so a DA or CCA bill resolves at the bundled rate. The two credits are expressible as Prices with multiplier -1 and basedOnPrice naming the six EECC Prices and the DWR-BC Price. The CRS amounts are priced in Schedules DA-CRS and CCA-CRS (Sheet 2 note 2), neither of which is in this corpus, so that half is a source gap. Special Condition 11's PCIA is not a separate item: the sheet states it is already embedded in the bundled EECC rates this filing carries, so the PCIA deferral recorded on the sibling SDG&E filings misdescribes the source.
- California Climate Credit $(49.36) semi-annual (Sheet 1, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 64667-E): the schedule states the amount and attributes it to Schedule GHG-ARR, which is not in this corpus. The amount is expressible as a Price with intervalDuration P6M; the two months it lands in are a source gap, because this PDF never names them. Recorded the same way on the sibling sdge/ev-tou-2 filing.
- Optional Billing, Utility Option (Special Condition 8, Sheet 5, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 64380-E): the utility may at its option bill a new customer the lower of the bill under this schedule or the bill under the customer's regularly applicable non-TOU schedule for three consecutive months. Base deferrals:
- Franchise Fee Differential 5.78% (Sheet 4, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 64379-E): applied to the monthly billings calculated under this schedule for all customers within the corporate limits of the City of San Diego. Expressible under URPX v0.5.1 and SHACL-validated against the v0.5.1 candidate: a Price with multiplier 0.0578, chargeType urpx:taxMunicipal and basedOnPrice naming a PriceSummary, gated by an ApplicabilityRule. It needs two unit-homogeneous summaries, one over the 27 per-kWh Prices and one over the per-day Base Services Charge, because the fee applies to the whole bill and a PriceSummary carries no unit conversion across the Prices it includes. Unbuilt rather than unbuildable: the restriction to the city limits lives in the ApplicabilityRule condition rather than in the price, so a consumer that does not evaluate that condition charges the fee to every customer on the schedule, not only those inside the city.
- Medical Baseline rates (Sheet 2, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 64817-E): the schedule prints a Total Effective MB Rate for all six TOU cells, exactly 0.80 x the Total Rate in every cell, and note (3) exempts Medical Baseline customers from DWR-BC + WF-NBC, worth $0.00591/kWh. Neither the exemption nor the differential is modeled here or anywhere in the corpus; there is no sdge medical-baseline modifier filing and applicable_modifiers does not list one. The schedule states the discount is identified in Schedule E-MEDICAL, so the corpus pattern is the CARE and FERA one: host an MB-discountable PriceSummary in this filing and carry the percentage in an E-MEDICAL modifier. The summary validates under v0.5.1; sce/medical-baseline is the shape precedent.
- SDG&E Electric Rule 1 (DEFINITIONS), Sheet 6, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 61672-E, HOLIDAYS: "When a Holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday shall be defined as a Holiday. No change will be made for Holidays falling on Saturday." New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas Day in sdge:ev-tou-5-2026-06-01-holidays carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day or Christmas Day is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2029-11-12, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2035-11-12, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02 and 2040-11-12. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.

Sheet No. 63296-E): a DA or CCA customer's bill is the bundled bill credited by the EECC component, and by the DWR-BC component for continuous DA customers exempt from that charge, plus the applicable Cost Responsibility Surcharge. Nothing in this model expresses that, so a DA or CCA bill resolves at the bundled rate. The two credits are expressible as Prices with multiplier -1 and basedOnPrice naming the six EECC Prices and the DWR-BC Price. The CRS amounts are priced in Schedules DA-CRS and CCA-CRS (Sheet 2 note 2), neither of which is in this corpus, so that half is a source gap. Special Condition 11's PCIA is not a separate item: the sheet states it is already embedded in the bundled EECC rates this filing carries, so the PCIA deferral recorded on the sibling SDG&E filings misdescribes the source.
- California Climate Credit $(49.36) semi-annual (Sheet 1, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 64816-E): the schedule states the amount and attributes it to Schedule GHG-ARR, which is not in this corpus. The amount is expressible as a Price with intervalDuration P6M; the two months it lands in are a source gap, because this PDF never names them. Recorded the same way on the sibling sdge/ev-tou-2 filing.
- Optional Billing, Utility Option (Special Condition 8, Sheet 5, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 64380-E): the utility may at its option bill a new customer the lower of the bill under this schedule or the bill under the customer's regularly applicable non-TOU schedule for three consecutive months. #### `tou-dr` @ 2026-04-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Baseline Adjustment Credit bound: Sheets 1 and 3 price the credit at $(0.10892) per kWh "up to 130% of Baseline" in both seasons, and this filing carries it as a single Price with no bound, so the credit applies to every kWh and a high-usage bill computed from this data is too low. v0.5.1 expresses the bound (ServiceBracket with appliesInSeason, referencesProfileAlternative and lowerBound/upperBound plus matching operators; the pge:e-tou-c baseline-tier modifier ships 120 such brackets), so this is work rather than a blocker. TOU-DR's own sheets do not publish the climate-zone allowance quantities and do not cross-reference them: SDG&E's table (Coastal, Inland, Mountain, Desert, in Basic and All-Electric variants, kWh per day by season) is in Schedule DR Special Condition 3, inside the sdge:dr filing. Do not copy sdge:dr's existing tiers, which bound at 1.3 on a kWh-denominated metric and are the wrong shape.
- Franchise Fee Differential: Sheet 4 applies 5.78% to the monthly billings of customers within the corporate limits of the City of San Diego. v0.5.1 expresses this as a Price with a multiplier and basedOnPrice naming a PriceSummary, with chargeType urpx:taxMunicipal, the same shape this filing already uses for the CARE and FERA discounts, so it is work rather than a blocker. Two cautions before building it: the fee applies to the whole monthly bill, which mixes $/kWh with $/day, and a PriceSummary is a list of member Prices with no unit of its own, so it needs one Price per unit-homogeneous summary (the two summaries here are $/kWh only and both exclude the Base Services Charge); and the City of San Diego limit is a restriction on who pays rather than a rate difference, so it can only ride as a ConditionExpression under the Price's ApplicabilityRule, and the amounts alone do not distinguish a customer inside the city limits from one outside.
- SDG&E Electric Rule 1 (DEFINITIONS), Sheet 6, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 61672-E, HOLIDAYS: "When a Holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday shall be defined as a Holiday. No change will be made for Holidays falling on Saturday." New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas Day in sdge:tou-dr-2026-04-01-holidays carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day or Christmas Day is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2029-11-12, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2035-11-12, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02 and 2040-11-12. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.
- TOU-DR-MB Medical Baseline rates: Sheet 2 prices a Medical Baseline column running from $0.35668 per kWh summer super off-peak to $0.48202 winter on-peak, with DWR-BC and WF-NBC at zero per note (3) and no Base Services Charge discount per note (6), and Sheet 1 states the schedule is applicable to Medical Baseline customers. Nothing in this corpus models SDG&E Medical Baseline; the parallel CARE overlay does exist at us/ca/sdge/electricity/care and names sdge:tou-dr. Special Condition 16 sends the allowance detail to Schedule E-MEDICAL, which is not committed here, and the additive allowance itself is not expressible as a delta under v0.5.1 (urpx-org/urpx#32 open).
- Special Condition 2.b Direct Access and Community Choice Aggregation billing: a DA or CCA customer's bill is computed as a bundled bill, then credited the EECC component and charged the applicable Cost Responsibility Surcharge. The credit-back half is expressible now (a Price with multiplier -1 and basedOnPrice naming the EECC Prices, which the pinned consumer reads); the surcharge amounts are set by Schedules DA-CRS and CCA-CRS, which are not in this corpus, so that half is a source gap rather than a vocabulary gap. Special Condition 14 states the Power Charge Indifference Adjustment is already embedded in the bundled EECC rate this filing models, so no separate PCIA price is owed on this schedule.
- Special Condition 11's twelve-month tenure and one-year return bar are terms of service on this schedule and the verification set them aside as "enrollment terms" that are "out of scope under the brief's standard". The model sets no urpx:minimumTenure at all, and the return bar has no v0.5.1 form.
- Special Condition 10's Reconnection Charge provision re-applies this schedule's own charges for the lapsed period. It states a billing rule over already-modeled Prices and nothing in the model corresponds to it.
- Special Condition 2.b, the Direct Access and Community Choice Aggregation billing path (Sheet 5), computes a bill as if bundled and then credits it back by components this filing prices. The crediting half operates on this filing's own Prices; the Cost Responsibility Surcharge half points at SDG&E DA-CRS and CCA-CRS schedules that are in no filing in this corpus.

The crediting half operates on this filing's own Prices; the Cost Responsibility Surcharge half points at SDG&E DA-CRS and CCA-CRS schedules that are in no filing in this corpus.

#### `tou-dr` @ 2026-08-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Baseline Adjustment Credit bound: Sheets 1 and 3 price the credit at $(0.10702) per kWh "up to 130% of Baseline" in both seasons, and this filing carries it as a single Price with no bound, so the credit applies to every kWh and a high-usage bill computed from this data is too low. v0.5.1 expresses the bound (ServiceBracket with appliesInSeason, referencesProfileAlternative and lowerBound/upperBound plus matching operators; the pge:e-tou-c baseline-tier modifier ships 120 such brackets), so this is work rather than a blocker. TOU-DR's own sheets do not publish the climate-zone allowance quantities and do not cross-reference them: SDG&E's table (Coastal, Inland, Mountain, Desert, in Basic and All-Electric variants, kWh per day by season) is in Schedule DR Special Condition 3, inside the sdge:dr filing. Do not copy sdge:dr's existing tiers, which bound at 1.3 on a kWh-denominated metric and are the wrong shape.
- Franchise Fee Differential: Sheet 4 applies 5.78% to the monthly billings of customers within the corporate limits of the City of San Diego. v0.5.1 expresses this as a Price with a multiplier and basedOnPrice naming a PriceSummary, with chargeType urpx:taxMunicipal, the same shape this filing already uses for the CARE and FERA discounts, so it is work rather than a blocker. Two cautions before building it: the fee applies to the whole monthly bill, which mixes $/kWh with $/day, and a PriceSummary is a list of member Prices with no unit of its own, so it needs one Price per unit-homogeneous summary (the two summaries here are $/kWh only and both exclude the Base Services Charge); and the City of San Diego limit is a restriction on who pays rather than a rate difference, so it can only ride as a ConditionExpression under the Price's ApplicabilityRule, and the amounts alone do not distinguish a customer inside the city limits from one outside.
- SDG&E Electric Rule 1 (DEFINITIONS), Sheet 6, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 61672-E, HOLIDAYS: "When a Holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday shall be defined as a Holiday. No change will be made for Holidays falling on Saturday." New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas Day in sdge:tou-dr-2026-08-01-holidays carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day or Christmas Day is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2029-11-12, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2035-11-12, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02 and 2040-11-12. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.
- TOU-DR-MB Medical Baseline rates: Sheet 2 prices a Medical Baseline column running from $0.33789 per kWh winter super off-peak to $0.47688 winter on-peak, with DWR-BC and WF-NBC at zero per note (3) and no Base Services Charge discount per note (6), and Sheet 1 states the schedule is applicable to Medical Baseline customers. Nothing in this corpus models SDG&E Medical Baseline; the parallel CARE overlay does exist at us/ca/sdge/electricity/care and names sdge:tou-dr. Special Condition 16 sends the allowance detail to Schedule E-MEDICAL, which is not committed here, and the additive allowance itself is not expressible as a delta under v0.5.1 (urpx-org/urpx#32 open).
- Special Condition 2.b Direct Access and Community Choice Aggregation billing: a DA or CCA customer's bill is computed as a bundled bill, then credited the EECC component and charged the applicable Cost Responsibility Surcharge. The credit-back half is expressible now (a Price with multiplier -1 and basedOnPrice naming the EECC Prices, which the pinned consumer reads); the surcharge amounts are set by Schedules DA-CRS and CCA-CRS, which are not in this corpus, so that half is a source gap rather than a vocabulary gap. Special Condition 14 states the Power Charge Indifference Adjustment is already embedded in the bundled EECC rate this filing models, so no separate PCIA price is owed on this schedule.
- Special Condition 11's twelve-month tenure and one-year return bar are terms of service on this schedule and the verification set them aside as "enrollment terms" that are "out of scope under the brief's standard". The model sets no urpx:minimumTenure at all, and the return bar has no v0.5.1 form.
- Special Condition 10's Reconnection Charge provision re-applies this schedule's own charges for the lapsed period. It states a billing rule over already-modeled Prices and nothing in the model corresponds to it.
- Special Condition 2.b, the Direct Access and Community Choice Aggregation billing path (Sheet 5), computes a bill as if bundled and then credits it back by components this filing prices. The crediting half operates on this filing's own Prices; the Cost Responsibility Surcharge half points at SDG&E DA-CRS and CCA-CRS schedules that are in no filing in this corpus.

P.U.C. 65140-E) gives its bundled Total Energy Rates (summer 0.53692 / 0.49226 / 0.41710, winter 0.59073 / 0.51376 / 0.42828 per kWh on-peak / off-peak / super off-peak, with the same Baseline Adjustment Credit of (0.10702)), and Sheet 5 (Cal. P.U.C. 65142-E) its EECC-TOU-DR-P commodity rates (summer 0.20500 / 0.16034 / 0.08518, winter 0.25881 / 0.18184 / 0.09636) and an RYU Adder of $1.16 per kWh. EECC-TOU-DR-P Special Condition 15 bills the adder from 4:00 p.m. to 9:00 p.m. on an RYU Event Day only, EECC-TOU-DR-P Special Condition 14 allows at most eighteen events a calendar year, and EECC-TOU-DR-P Special Condition 6 has the Utility notify customers the day before. Sheets 11 to 14 (Cal. P.U.C. 65148-E to 65151-E) hold its applicability and Special Conditions: it is not available to Direct Access, Transitional Bundled Service or Community Choice Aggregation customers, and a customer taking it takes UDC service under this schedule. This filing prices only the EECC commodity rates, so a bill computed from it for an EECC-TOU-DR-P customer carries the EECC rates and no adder.

#### `tou-dr1` @ 2026-04-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Baseline Adjustment Credit is modeled as a single unbounded Price at $-0.10892/kWh, but Sheet 2 (Cal. P.U.C. 64669-E) prices it only "up to 130% of Baseline" and note (4) confirms the cap, so as modeled it applies to every kWh and a high-usage bill comes out too low (summer on-peak marginal 0.58680 against 0.69572, a 15.7 percent understatement). Not a source gap: Special Condition 15 on Sheet 7 publishes the allowance quantities (Basic summer 9.0 / 10.4 / 13.6 / 15.9 and winter 9.2 / 9.6 / 12.9 / 10.9 kWh per day for Coastal / Inland / Mountain / Desert; All Electric summer 6.0 / 8.7 / 15.2 / 17.0 and winter 8.8 / 12.2 / 22.1 / 17.1). Expressible at v0.5.1 either by precomputing 1.3x the daily allowance as ServiceBracket bounds over the 16 zone x allowance-type x season cells as a ProfileAlternatives selection (the pge:e-tou-c pattern, whose 120 brackets live in a separate modifier file at that filing's 2026-03-01 vintage), or by a ratio-denominated MetricSpecification bounded 0 to 1.3 and 1.3 up. A 16-cell precomputed candidate conforms against the v0.5.1 shapes; the tier vocabulary is materially unchanged since v0.2.1, so this is unbuilt work and the migration did not unblock it. Do not copy sdge:dr, which bounds at 1.3 against a kWh metric.
- Franchise Fee Differential of 5.78 percent is not modeled. Sheet 5 (Cal. P.U.C. 64386-E): "A Franchise Fee Differential of 5.78% will be applied to the monthly billings calculated under this Schedule for all customers within the corporate limits of the City of San Diego." The figure previously recorded here, 0.78 percent, was wrong. Expressible at v0.5.1, with urpx:taxMunicipal as the charge type, but it needs two Prices and not one. The tariff applies the percentage to the monthly billings under this Schedule, which include the $/day Base Services Charge as well as the $/kWh energy, so a percentage spanning both units needs one Price per unit-homogeneous base. urpx-shacl:PriceSummaryShape requires includesPrice at minCount 2, so the single BSC Price cannot get a summary of its own and its leg must use basedOnPrice pointing at sdge:tou-dr1-2026-04-01-price-base-services-charge directly, while the energy leg goes over the 17-member $/kWh summary. A two-leg candidate built that way conforms merged with the SDG&E base plans. A one-leg build omits 5.78 percent of the Base Services Charge, about $1.38 on a 30-day cycle. Leave it unbuilt on the base plan: inline, the City of San Diego limit would rest on a condition attached to a Price the plan charges to every TOU-DR1 customer. Build it as a separate modifier filing alongside care, fera and drah so it is opt-in.
- SDG&E Electric Rule 1 (DEFINITIONS), Sheet 6, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 61672-E, HOLIDAYS: "When a Holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday shall be defined as a Holiday. No change will be made for Holidays falling on Saturday." New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas Day in sdge:tou-dr1-2026-04-01-holidays carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day or Christmas Day is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2029-11-12, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2035-11-12, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02 and 2040-11-12. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.
- TOU-DR1-MB rates published on Sheet 3 (Cal. P.U.C. 64642-E) have no model anywhere in the corpus: Base Services Charge $0.79343/day, UDC $0.34061, DWR-BC plus WF-NBC $0.00000, EECC unchanged, Total Effective MB Rate $0.54054 / $0.36762 / $0.29919 summer and $0.48220 / $0.41817 / $0.34705 winter on-peak / off-peak / super off-peak, and a Baseline Adjustment Credit of $(0.10892) showing $(0.08535) effective. The CARE, FERA and DRAH columns of the same sheets are carried by us/ca/sdge/electricity/{care,fera,drah}; Medical Baseline is the one published variant with no counterpart, and applicable_modifiers omits it. The cells are more clearly buildable than a table of numbers would suggest: the Total Effective MB column is exactly 0.783603 times the Total Rate in all six season-and-period cells and on the baseline-credit line, and the sibling sdge:dr MB table carries the same constant, so the published MB cells reduce to a single multiplier over a unit-homogeneous summary, the shape care and fera already ship. What is not in this PDF is the mechanism behind the factor: Special Condition 14 attributes it to the extra allotment granted under Schedule E-MEDICAL, E-MEDICAL is not in this corpus, and an additive allowance extension has no URPX form today (urpx-org/urpx#32 open). Other SDG&E base schedules publish an MB table too, so this is a corpus-wide gap rather than one filing's. Add "medical-baseline" to applicable_modifiers once the modifier exists. Unsettled: Sheet 2 note (6) explicitly calls the CARE effective column illustrative and Sheet 3 carries no equivalent note, which decides whether an MB modifier should reproduce those numbers or derive them.
- Special Condition 12 Bill Protection is not modeled: a credit for the difference where a migrating customer's first twelve months on this schedule cost more than Schedule DR would have. The datum is recordable at v0.5.1, since urpx:creditPriceProtection is a ChargeTypeValue ("Credit from price protection program", chargeCategory discount); what cannot be computed is the value, which is a whole bill recomputed under a different schedule and differenced, with no cross-plan bill comparison and no greater-of arithmetic anywhere in the vocabulary. urpx:hasPriorRatePlan is eligibility-only (domain CustomerProfile, skos:note "Used for eligibility rules based on rate plan history"). Practically spent: the same condition states that a customer taking service on or after March 1, 2019 does not receive bill protection, so it is closed to every customer this 2026-04-01 filing can enroll. Recorded so a later reader does not rediscover it from the sheet.

- SDG&E Electric Rule 1 (DEFINITIONS), Sheet 6, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 61672-E, HOLIDAYS: "When a Holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday shall be defined as a Holiday. No change will be made for Holidays falling on Saturday." New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas Day in sdge:tou-dr1-2026-08-01-holidays carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day or Christmas Day is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2029-11-12, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2035-11-12, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02 and 2040-11-12. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.
- TOU-DR1-MB rates published on Sheet 3 (Cal. P.U.C. 64944-E) have no model anywhere in the corpus: Base Services Charge $0.79343/day, UDC $0.32601, DWR-BC plus WF-NBC $0.00000, EECC unchanged, Total Effective MB Rate $0.53711 / $0.35912 / $0.28869 summer and $0.47706 / $0.41115 / $0.33795 winter on-peak / off-peak / super off-peak, and a Baseline Adjustment Credit of $(0.10702) showing $(0.08386) effective. The CARE, FERA and DRAH columns of the same sheets are carried by us/ca/sdge/electricity/{care,fera,drah}; Medical Baseline is the one published variant with no counterpart, and applicable_modifiers omits it. The cells are more clearly buildable than a table of numbers would suggest: the Total Effective MB column is 0.7836 times the Total Rate, to the rounding of the published cells, in all six season-and-period cells and on the baseline-credit line, and the sibling sdge:dr MB table carries the same factor, so the published MB cells reduce to a single multiplier over a unit-homogeneous summary, the shape care and fera already ship. What is not in this PDF is the mechanism behind the factor: Special Condition 14 refers the Medical Baseline allowance to Schedule E-MEDICAL, E-MEDICAL is not in this corpus, and an additive allowance extension has no URPX form today (urpx-org/urpx#32 open). Other SDG&E base schedules publish an MB table too, so this is a corpus-wide gap rather than one filing's. Add "medical-baseline" to applicable_modifiers once the modifier exists. Unsettled: Sheet 2 note (6) explicitly calls the CARE effective column illustrative and Sheet 3 carries no equivalent note, which decides whether an MB modifier should reproduce those numbers or derive them.
- Special Condition 12 Bill Protection is not modeled: a credit for the difference where a migrating customer's first twelve months on this schedule cost more than Schedule DR would have. The datum is recordable at v0.5.1, since urpx:creditPriceProtection is a ChargeTypeValue ("Credit from price protection program", chargeCategory discount); what cannot be computed is the value, which is a whole bill recomputed under a different schedule and differenced, with no cross-plan bill comparison and no greater-of arithmetic anywhere in the vocabulary. urpx:hasPriorRatePlan is eligibility-only (domain CustomerProfile, skos:note "Used for eligibility rules based on rate plan history"). Practically spent: the same condition states that a customer taking service on or after March 1, 2019 does not receive bill protection, so it is closed to every customer this 2026-08-01 filing can enroll. Recorded so a later reader does not rediscover it from the sheet.

P.U.C. 65213-E) prices it only "up to 130% of Baseline", so as modeled it applies to every kWh and a high-usage bill comes out too low (summer on-peak marginal 0.58433 against 0.69135, a 15.5 percent understatement). Not a source gap: General Special Condition 15 on Sheet 9 publishes the allowance quantities (Basic summer 9.0 / 10.4 / 13.6 / 15.9 and winter 9.2 / 9.6 / 12.9 / 10.9 kWh per day for Coastal / Inland / Mountain / Desert; All Electric summer 6.0 / 8.7 / 15.2 / 17.0 and winter 8.8 / 12.2 / 22.1 / 17.1). A 16-cell precomputed candidate conforms against the v0.5.1 shapes; the tier vocabulary is materially unchanged since v0.2.1, so this is unbuilt work and the migration did not unblock it. The tariff applies the percentage to the monthly billings under this Schedule, which include the $/day Base Services Charge as well as the $/kWh energy, so a percentage spanning both units needs one Price per unit-homogeneous base. urpx-shacl:PriceSummaryShape requires includesPrice at minCount 2, so the single BSC Price cannot get a summary of its own and its leg must use basedOnPrice pointing at sdge:tou-dr1-2026-09-30-price-base-services-charge directly, while the energy leg goes over the 17-member $/kWh summary. A two-leg candidate built that way conforms merged with the SDG&E base plans. A one-leg build omits 5.78 percent of the Base Services Charge, about $1.38 on a 30-day cycle. Leave it unbuilt on the base plan: inline, the City of San Diego limit would rest on a condition attached to a Price the plan charges to every TOU-DR1 customer. - TOU-DR1-MB rates published on Sheet 2 (Cal. P.U.C. 65213-E) have no model anywhere in the corpus: Base Services Charge $0.79343/day, Total Energy Rates $0.68544 / $0.45830 / $0.36842 summer and $0.60880 / $0.52469 / $0.43128 winter on-peak / off-peak / super off-peak with no DWR-BC or WF-NBC (Sheet 4 marks both N/A for TOU-DR1-MB), Total Effective Energy Rates $0.53711 / $0.35912 / $0.28869 summer and $0.47706 / $0.41115 / $0.33795 winter, and a Baseline Adjustment Credit of $(0.10702) showing $(0.08386) effective. #### `tou-dr2` @ 2026-04-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Baseline Adjustment Credit is carried as a flat $-0.10892/kWh with no upper bound. Sheet 2 publishes it as 'Summer/Winter Baseline Adjustment Credit up to 130% of Baseline' and note (4) confirms the credit is limited to usage up to 130% of baseline; Special Condition 15 on Sheet 7 supplies the allowance quantities in kWh/day (Basic Allowance summer 9.0, 10.4, 13.6, 15.9 and winter 9.2, 9.6, 12.9, 10.9 for Coastal, Inland, Mountain, Desert; All Electric summer 6.0, 8.7, 15.2, 17.0 and winter 8.8, 12.2, 22.1, 17.1). As modeled the credit applies to every kWh, so the summer on-peak marginal rate resolves to 0.59129 where the schedule publishes 0.70021. Expressible under v0.5.1 and precedented in this corpus: pge:tiers-baseline on the PG&E E-TOU-C baseline-tier modifier carries absolute kWh/day ServiceBracket bounds per climate-zone ProfileAlternative and season. The work is 16 cells at 1.3 times the SC 15 allowance, hosted the same way. Not a vocabulary gap and not a source gap.
- Franchise Fee Differential of 5.78 percent is not modeled. Sheet 5: 'A Franchise Fee Differential of 5.78% will be applied to the monthly billings calculated under this Schedule for all customers within the corporate limits of the City of San Diego.' Expressible under v0.5.1 as a multiplier Price with basedOnPrice over a PriceSummary, chargeType urpx:taxMunicipal. It needs two such Prices over two separate unit-homogeneous summaries, because the fee applies to the whole monthly bill, which mixes the $/kWh components with the $/day Base Services Charge, and one summary spanning both would add unlike units; neither existing summary can be reused, since both deliberately exclude the BSC. The City of San Diego gate (urpx:hasMunicipality inside a ConditionExpression) validates and carries the restriction declaratively, so a build that left the gate out would charge the fee to every customer on the schedule.
- TOU-DR2-MB rate table on Sheet 3 is not carried, and no SDG&E Medical Baseline overlay exists anywhere in this corpus, so an MB customer cannot be billed from this data. The schedule publishes Total Effective MB Rates of 0.54405 and 0.33142 per kWh summer on-peak and off-peak, 0.48220 and 0.37486 winter, a Baseline Adjustment Credit at (0.08535), DWR-BC and WF-NBC at 0.00000, and per note (7) no Base Services Charge discount. Sheet 1 places these tables inside this schedule ('The rates for CARE customers and/or Medical Baseline are identified in the rate tables below'), on the same footing as the CARE column that the corpus does carry. Buildable now by per-cell subtraction against the Total MB Rate, and by the exemption-credit shape the sdge care modifier already ships; what this filing does not state is the rule that produces the effective rates (they are a uniform 0.78359 factor, which no provision here explains), so a modifier built this way is faithful to this filing but will not generalize. Schedule E-MEDICAL, which SC 14 names as the authority, is not committed to this repo.
- Semi-annual California Climate Credit of $(49.36) per Schedule GHG-ARR, named in the Sheet 1 applicability section, is not modeled. Expressible under v0.5.1 as a credit Price with urpx:intervalDuration P6M. The alternative form, an ApplicabilityRule enumerating the two credit months the way sce:d does, cannot be sourced from this filing: the schedule names neither month, and Schedule GHG-ARR is not in this corpus. That part is a source gap, not a vocabulary gap.
- Special Condition 9 (Sheet 6) bars a customer who leaves this schedule from returning for 12 months; the schedule sets no minimum stay. This is a term of service on this schedule, set aside by the assessment as "service and enrollment terms, not rates", and no re-entry construct exists at v0.5.1.

#### `tou-elec` @ 2026-04-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Franchise Fee Differential 5.78% (Sheet 4, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 64397-E): "applied to the monthly billings calculated under this schedule for all customers within the corporate limits of the City of San Diego" and added as a separate bill item. Not modeled. Expressible under URPX v0.5.1 as a modifier Price with multiplier 0.0578 and basedOnPrice naming a unit-homogeneous PriceSummary, chargeType urpx:taxMunicipal. Two cautions for whoever builds it: the City of San Diego gate needs a ConditionExpression, because a fee Price carrying no condition applies to every TOU-ELEC customer and would charge the 5.78% outside the corporate limits too; and a percentage spanning both $/kWh and $/day components needs one Price per unit-homogeneous summary, since PriceSummary members are summed without unit awareness. This is work, not a vocabulary blocker. No filing in the corpus models the franchise fee today.
- Medical Baseline rates (Sheet 2, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 64674-E, table "Description - TOU-ELEC MB Rates"). Note 3 exempts MB customers from DWR-BC and WF-NBC, so their DWR-BC + WF-NBC column reads 0.00000 against 0.00591 on the standard table, and the Total Effective MB Rate column is 80 percent of the Total MB Rate, rounded to the published five decimals, in all six season/period cells (0.57582, 0.31386, 0.27940, 0.40479, 0.30450, 0.27315). Note 6 confirms MB carries no BSC discount. Neither part is modeled: there is no SDG&E medical-baseline RatePlanModifier in this corpus (only us/ca/sce/electricity/medical-baseline/) and [modeling].applicable_modifiers omits it. The exemption half is expressible and precedented: sdge/care already carries sdge:care-price-wf-nbc-exemption-2025-10-01 at -0.00591 and a DWR-BC exemption at -0.00000 against the same base. The 80% factor is stated nowhere in this PDF; SC 5 sends the reader to Schedule E-MEDICAL, which this corpus does not hold, so the mechanism behind that column is a source gap even though the resulting per-cell rates are published here.
- SDG&E Electric Rule 1 (DEFINITIONS), Sheet 6, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 61672-E, HOLIDAYS: "When a Holiday listed above falls on Sunday, the following Monday shall be defined as a Holiday. No change will be made for Holidays falling on Saturday." New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day and Christmas Day in sdge:tou-elec-2026-04-01-holidays carry no urpx:observanceRule, so each resolves on its actual date and a Saturday one stays on Saturday. The Monday after a Sunday New Year's Day, Independence Day, Veterans Day or Christmas Day is carried as a dated urpx:Holiday with urpx:holidayDate: 2027-07-05, 2029-11-12, 2032-07-05, 2033-12-26, 2034-01-02, 2035-11-12, 2038-07-05, 2039-12-26, 2040-01-02 and 2040-11-12. After 2040 each such Sunday holiday needs a further dated entry, until urpx:mondayIfSundayObservance (proposed in urpx-org/urpx#77) can state the rule; the dated entries then come out.
- SC 7.b (Sheet 6, Cal. P.U.C. Sheet No. 63312-E) directs that a Direct Access or CCA customer's bill is computed as a bundled bill and then credited "by the amount of the EECC component and DWR-BC + WF-NBC component ... and including the appropriate Cost Responsibility Surcharge (CRS)" per Schedules DA-CRS and CCA-CRS. The modeled RatePlanVersion declares urpx:serviceRole urpx:bundledServiceRole, so the DA/CCA branch is outside its declared scope, and the CRS amounts live in Schedules DA-CRS and CCA-CRS, which this corpus does not hold for SDG&E. Recorded for parity with sdge/dr, sdge/tou-dr, sdge/tou-dr1, sdge/ev-tou and sdge/ev-tou-2. Note that those filings' equivalent deferrals attribute this to the PCIA Special Condition, which is wrong: on TOU-ELEC the PCIA condition is SC 9, it concerns bundled customers only, and it prices nothing extra because the PCIA is already inside the modeled bundled EECC.
- Special Condition 10's enrollment cap is a term of availability of this schedule, stated twice in this filing's own PDF, and the model records neither statement. Sheet 1: "There is a cap of 10,000 customers who may take service on this rate, as defined in SC 10." Special Condition 10 verbatim: "Enrollment Cap: There is a cap of 10,000 customers who may take service on this rate. A customer's ability to enroll will depend on availability. In the event that the cap is reached, SDG&E is not responsible for maintaining a queue of customers who want to take service on TOU-ELEC." The v0.5.1 ontology has no enrollment-cap property, so this is a blocked deferral. This filing currently ships complete = true with an empty deferrals array.
- Special Condition 4's re-entry bar is unmodeled while its twelve-month stay is modeled. Verbatim: "A customer receiving service under this schedule may elect to change to another applicable rate schedule after receiving service on this schedule for at least 12 consecutive months. If a customer elects to discontinue service on this schedule, the customer will not be permitted to return to this schedule for a period of 12 consecutive months." The model sets urpx:minimumTenure P12M for the first sentence. URPX v0.5.1 has no term for a return bar: minimumTenure is its only tenure term, and an ObjectOperandNode takes a single conditionProperty, so it cannot test a urpx:PriorRatePlan's referencesRatePlan and enrollmentEndDate together. The bar is expressible as an EligibilityRule negating a urpx:CustomOperandNode comparison of months since the customer left this schedule with 12, the route this corpus takes for predicates URPX does not name; that form conforms against the v0.5.1 shapes in the merged SDG&E graph.
- Special Condition 7.b, the Direct Access and Community Choice Aggregation bill path (Sheet 5), computes a bundled bill and then credits back "the EECC component and DWR-BC + WF-NBC component ... and including the appropriate Cost Responsibility Surcharge (CRS)". The crediting half operates on this filing's own Prices; the CRS half points at Schedules DA-CRS and CCA-CRS, which SDG&E has in no filing in this corpus.

### SMUD

#### `fixed` @ 2026-01-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Standby Service Charge, Sheet R-3 Sec IV.E: $8.855 per kW of Contract Capacity per month effective January 1, 2026, applying when on-site generation under 100 kW combined nameplate is connected to SMUD's system, and waived only for qualifying renewable generation under Schedules NEM1 and SSR. Priced in this PDF and absent from the model. Expressible under URPX v0.5.1 and therefore work, not a blocker: chargeType urpx:standbyCharge (an ontology member at v0.5.1), a contract-capacity MetricInput with measurementKind urpx:demand and unitOfMeasure urpx:kW (the shape cpau/e-7 already uses), and CalculationMethod intervalDuration P1M; a candidate of that shape conforms under SHACL validation. The waiver is the conditional part: it belongs on a ConditionExpression, and a Price that carries no condition applies to every customer with on-site generation, including the ones the schedule waives.
- Residential Three-Phase Service Option, Sheet R-3 Sec IV.J: Special Facilities fee $57.95 per month effective January 1, 2026, in addition to the System Infrastructure Fixed Charge. Priced in this PDF and absent from the model. Expressible under URPX v0.5.1 with the same shape as the SIFC this model already carries (flat monthly PriceDefinition, chargeType urpx:facilities, intervalDuration P1M); a candidate conforms under SHACL validation. Work, not a blocker. Note the schedule says the fee "may be charged", so it is contingent on SMUD assessing it rather than automatic.
- Section V proration, Sheet R-4: the System Infrastructure Fixed Charge and kWh are prorated on the relationship of the billing period to 30 days when the period is shorter than 27 days, kWh likewise when longer than 34 days, and kWh by the days falling in each season or pricing period when a period spans a season or a price change; Sheet R-1 Sec I defines a month as a billing period of 27 to 34 days. The model carries flat monthly and per-kWh amounts with no proration. URPX v0.5.1 offers only urpx:prorationMethod, a free-text xsd:string on BillingModel with maxCount 1, which no corpus filing uses. Recording the rule there documents it for a future consumer; free text does not compute it.
- Hydro Generation Adjustment, Sheet R-2 Sec III.A: a surcharge on all kWh that Schedule R refers to Rate Schedule HGA without stating an amount. Schedule HGA is not committed to this corpus, so every bill computed from this model is low by the HGA amount. This is a missing source document, not a URPX expressibility gap. The sibling filing us/ca/smud/electricity/tod-5-8pm/versions/2026-01-01/ records the same item.
- SMUD Renewable Energy Option (Sheet R-3 Sec IV.G) and Special Metering Charge (Sheet R-3 Sec IV.H): the schedule provides for an additional charge under each and publishes no amount, directing the reader to www.smud.org, with Special Metering billed through Rule and Regulation 2 Section IV Special Facilities. Missing source, not a URPX expressibility gap; nothing in the tariff is available to model.
- Master-Metered Multifamily Accommodation and Mobile Home Park Billing, rate category RSMM, Sheet R-1 Sec I.C and Sheet R-2 Sec II.B: closed to new customers but still served and fully priced in this PDF at $27.00 per unit per month, $0.1520/kWh non-summer and $0.1740/kWh summer, billed on master-meter kWh divided by the number of occupied single-family accommodations. No filing in the corpus carries it, and the model's CoverageClaim currently counts it against this filing. Nothing about it is blocked: these are plain Prices. Where it belongs is an open corpus-level scope ruling, not a fact about this filing: Sheet R-1 Sec I.A.7 bars RSMM customers from the Fixed Rate, and the convention that gave one R-TOD PDF three directories (tod-5-8pm, tod-low-use, cpp) argues for its own smud/rsmm filing, while it is priced in this schedule's own sheets. Recorded here until that is settled; the ruling belongs in MODELING-GAPS.md.
- Section I and Section I.A state this schedule's whole availability surface and the model carries it as EligibilityRule description prose with a single residential-kind ComparisonNode. The description recites the premises types, the analog and digital non-communicating meter requirement, the NEM1 grandfather at January 1, 2018, the SSR exclusion and the RSMM master-meter exclusion; the ConditionExpression tests only hasCustomerKind = residentialKind. Verbatim from Section I.A.5: "Customers who have an eligible renewable electrical generation facility under Rate Schedule SSR are not eligible to enroll in the Fixed Rate." Section I.A.4: "Customers who have an eligible renewable electrical generation facility under Rate Schedule NEM1 that was approved for installation on or after January 1, 2018 are not eligible to enroll in the Fixed Rate." Section I.A.2: "The Fixed Rate is required for customers serviced with analog meters and digital non-communicating meters." This is the same shape as the known casualty and none of it is in the deferrals array.

#### `fixed` @ 2027-01-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Standby Service Charge, Sheet R-3 Sec IV.E: $9.121 per kW of Contract Capacity per month effective January 1, 2027, applying when on-site generation under 100 kW combined nameplate is connected to SMUD's system, and waived only for qualifying renewable generation under Schedules NEM1 and SSR. Priced in this PDF and absent from the model. Expressible under URPX v0.5.1 and therefore work, not a blocker: chargeType urpx:standbyCharge (an ontology member at v0.5.1), a contract-capacity MetricInput with measurementKind urpx:demand and unitOfMeasure urpx:kW (the shape cpau/e-7 already uses), and CalculationMethod intervalDuration P1M; a candidate of that shape conforms under SHACL validation. The waiver is the conditional part: it belongs on a ConditionExpression, and a Price that carries no condition applies to every customer with on-site generation, including the ones the schedule waives.
- Residential Three-Phase Service Option, Sheet R-3 Sec IV.J: Special Facilities fee $59.70 per month effective January 1, 2027, in addition to the System Infrastructure Fixed Charge. Priced in this PDF and absent from the model. Expressible under URPX v0.5.1 with the same shape as the SIFC this model already carries (flat monthly PriceDefinition, chargeType urpx:facilities, intervalDuration P1M); a candidate conforms under SHACL validation. Work, not a blocker. Note the schedule says the fee "may be charged", so it is contingent on SMUD assessing it rather than automatic.
- Section V proration, Sheet R-4: the System Infrastructure Fixed Charge and kWh are prorated on the relationship of the billing period to 30 days when the period is shorter than 27 days, kWh likewise when longer than 34 days, and kWh by the days falling in each season or pricing period when a period spans a season or a price change; Sheet R-1 Sec I defines a month as a billing period of 27 to 34 days. The model carries flat monthly and per-kWh amounts with no proration. URPX v0.5.1 offers only urpx:prorationMethod, a free-text xsd:string on BillingModel with maxCount 1, which no corpus filing uses. Recording the rule there documents it for a future consumer; free text does not compute it.
- Hydro Generation Adjustment, Sheet R-2 Sec III.A: a surcharge on all kWh that Schedule R refers to Rate Schedule HGA without stating an amount. Schedule HGA is not committed to this corpus, so every bill computed from this model is low by the HGA amount. This is a missing source document, not a URPX expressibility gap. The sibling filing us/ca/smud/electricity/tod-5-8pm/versions/2026-01-01/ records the same item.
- SMUD Renewable Energy Option (Sheet R-3 Sec IV.G) and Special Metering Charge (Sheet R-3 Sec IV.H): the schedule provides for an additional charge under each and publishes no amount, directing the reader to www.smud.org, with Special Metering billed through Rule and Regulation 2 Section IV Special Facilities. Missing source, not a URPX expressibility gap; nothing in the tariff is available to model.
- Master-Metered Multifamily Accommodation and Mobile Home Park Billing, rate category RSMM, Sheet R-1 Sec I.C and Sheet R-2 Sec II.B: closed to new customers but still served and fully priced in this PDF at $27.80 per unit per month, $0.1566/kWh non-summer and $0.1792/kWh summer, billed on master-meter kWh divided by the number of occupied single-family accommodations. No filing in the corpus carries it, and the model's CoverageClaim currently counts it against this filing. Nothing about it is blocked: these are plain Prices. Where it belongs is an open corpus-level scope ruling, not a fact about this filing: Sheet R-1 Sec I.A.7 bars RSMM customers from the Fixed Rate, and the convention that gave one R-TOD PDF three directories (tod-5-8pm, tod-low-use, cpp) argues for its own smud/rsmm filing, while it is priced in this schedule's own sheets. Recorded here until that is settled; the ruling belongs in MODELING-GAPS.md.
- Section I and Section I.A state this schedule's whole availability surface and the model carries it as EligibilityRule description prose with a single residential-kind ComparisonNode. The description recites the premises types, the analog and digital non-communicating meter requirement, the NEM1 grandfather at January 1, 2018, the SSR exclusion and the RSMM master-meter exclusion; the ConditionExpression tests only hasCustomerKind = residentialKind. Verbatim from Section I.A.5: "Customers who have an eligible renewable electrical generation facility under Rate Schedule SSR are not eligible to enroll in the Fixed Rate." Section I.A.4: "Customers who have an eligible renewable electrical generation facility under Rate Schedule NEM1 that was approved for installation on or after January 1, 2018 are not eligible to enroll in the Fixed Rate." Section I.A.2: "The Fixed Rate is required for customers serviced with analog meters and digital non-communicating meters." This is the same shape as the known casualty and none of it is in the deferrals array.

#### `tod-5-8pm` @ 2026-01-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Hydro Generation Adjustment (Sheet R-TOD-3, Section III.A): R-TOD imposes the HGA as an electricity usage surcharge on every bill under this schedule, with no eligibility qualifier, but states only "Refer to Rate Schedule HGA" and publishes no amount. Schedule HGA is not in this corpus, so this is a source gap rather than a URPX expressibility gap; once fetched it is an ordinary per-kWh adjustment. Until then the resolved marginal $/kWh is understated for every customer on this schedule.
- Standby Service Charge (Sheet R-TOD-3, Section IV.D): $8.855 per kW of Contract Capacity per month effective January 1, 2026, for customers whose on-site generation has a combined nameplate rating under 100 kW and is connected to SMUD's system, waived only for qualifying NEM1 and SSR renewable generation. Not modeled anywhere in the corpus. Expressible under v0.5.1: urpx:standbyCharge is absent at v0.2.1 and present from v0.3.0, and pairs with unitOfMeasure urpx:kW, measurementKind urpx:demand and intervalDuration P1M. This is work, not a blocker; it belongs in a smud/standby RatePlanModifier filing.
- Residential Three-Phase Service Option (Sheet R-TOD-4, Section IV.H): Special Facilities fee of $57.95 per month effective January 1, 2026, billed in addition to the System Infrastructure Fixed Charge, where three-phase service is available. Not modeled. Structurally identical to the SIFC this filing already carries (chargeType urpx:facilities, intervalDuration P1M) and was equally expressible at v0.2.1, so this is work rather than a blocker; it belongs in a smud/three-phase RatePlanModifier filing because Section IV.H makes the fee conditional.
- Proration of the System Infrastructure Fixed Charge (Sheet R-TOD-5, Section VI.A): the $27.00 monthly charge is prorated on the relationship between the billing period length and 30 days whenever the period is shorter than 27 days, and is determined by the billing period end date; the electricity usage charge is never prorated. The model carries the charge flat at intervalDuration P1M with no proration. urpx:prorationMethod exists as a free-text xsd:string, maxCount 1, on BillingModel, and has been there since v0.2.1 with zero corpus uses, so the sentence can be recorded but the arithmetic cannot be computed.
- Section I states this schedule's premises-type applicability and a compulsory-enrollment rule, and the EligibilityRule tests only hasCustomerKind = residentialKind. Verbatim: "This Rate Schedule R-TOD applies to single- and three-phase service for the following types of residential premises: 1. Individual or dual metered residences with digital communicating meter installed, including single-family homes, duplexes, apartments, and condominiums; and 2. General farm service where the meter also serves the residence or additional meters on a farm where the electricity consumed is solely for domestic purposes. 3. Customers who have an eligible renewable electrical generation facility under Rate Schedules NEM1 or SSR that was approved for installation by SMUD on or after January 1, 2018, or who establish service at a premises that has an electrical generation facility that is fueled by a renewable fuel source on or after January 1, 2018 must be on this Rate Schedule R-TOD." And: "Master-metered service to a qualifying multifamily accommodation or mobile home parks are not eligible for Time-of-Day rates under rate schedule R-TOD." The assessment records these as "left in prose"; they are a part of the source document that is not modeled.

#### `tod-low-use` @ 2026-01-01  &nbsp; base: ~

Base deferrals:
- Standby Service Charge, $8.855 per kW of Contract Capacity per month effective January 1, 2026, priced in this filing's own PDF at Sheet R-TOD-3 Section IV.D. Applies to customers whose on-premises generation serves all or part of load with a combined nameplate rating under 100 kW, and is waived only for qualifying renewable generation under Rate Schedules NEM1 and SSR. Not a vocabulary gap and never was: urpx:kW, urpx:capacityCharge and urpx:demandDirect are all present at v0.2.1 and v0.5.1 alike, so the migration changed nothing here. It is unbuilt work awaiting a smud:standby modifier filing, which does not exist. It does not belong as an unconditional Price on the base plan, because a Price that carries no applicability condition applies to every customer on the rate. Note that the same table also prices $9.121 effective January 1, 2027, which no filing carries.
- Residential Three-Phase Service Option Special Facilities fee, $57.95 per month effective January 1, 2026, priced in this filing's own PDF at Sheet R-TOD-4 Section IV.H and stated there to be in addition to the System Infrastructure Fixed Charge. Section I brings three-phase service inside Rate Schedule R-TOD ("This Rate Schedule R-TOD applies to single- and three-phase service"). The fee itself is expressible at v0.5.1 and was at v0.2.1 before it, with chargeType urpx:facilities and intervalDuration P1M, both present at both versions; this is unbuilt work awaiting a smud:three-phase modifier filing rather than a blocker. Do not cite urpx:threePhaseCategory as the gating term, which was the reason recorded earlier: it was not a declared individual at v0.2.1 (the IRI appears there only as the object of skos:broader on PhaseCodeKind members, untyped), and it is the wrong term at v0.5.1 regardless, because urpx:phaseCategory carries rdfs:domain urpx:PhaseCodeKindValue and so classifies phase codes rather than customers or service points. The customer-side term is urpx:phaseCodeKind, whose domain is ServicePointProfile or MetricSpecification and whose range is PhaseCodeKindValue; there is no single member meaning "three-phase service", so a gate has to be an OR over the eight codes whose phaseCategory is urpx:threePhaseCategory (AB, ABC, ABCN, ABN, AC, ACN, BC, BCN), which BooleanOperationNodeShape permits since its operandNodes is unbounded. Two things that gate has no form for: the schedule's condition is availability ("customers located in areas where three-phase service is available"), not a measured phase code, and the fee is discretionary ("may be charged"). The same table also prices $59.70 effective January 1, 2027.
- Hydro Generation Adjustment. Sheet R-TOD-3 Section III.A applies an electricity usage surcharge to this rate by reference only, "Refer to Rate Schedule HGA", and states no amount, unit or period anywhere in the five sheets. A source gap, not a vocabulary gap: Rate Schedule HGA is not committed to this corpus, so there is no number to model. Fetch Rate Schedule HGA before treating this as modeling work; a per-kWh surcharge is straightforward to express once its value is in hand, and if SMUD's HGA is currently at zero the gap is nominal.
- Proration of the System Infrastructure Fixed Charge. Sheet R-TOD-5 Section VI.A: "The electricity usage charge will not be prorated, regardless of the number of days in the billing period or the spanning of multiple seasons. The monthly System Infrastructure Fixed Charge will be prorated when the bill period is shorter than 27 days", on the relationship between the billing period's length and 30 days; Sheet R-TOD-1 Section I defines a month as a billing period of 27 to 34 days. The model carries the charge as a flat unitPrice 17.00 with intervalDuration P1M, so a 20-day bill resolves at the full amount. urpx:prorationMethod (xsd:string, 0..1 on BillingModel, present since v0.2.1) can carry the rule declaratively, but no filing in this corpus uses it, and being a string it records the rule rather than expressing it as computable structure. Unsettled whether a billing-mechanics rule that changes the amount of an already-modeled charge is a coverage question under this corpus's convention; kept because it does change the bill.
- SMUD Renewable Energy Option, Sheet R-TOD-3 Section IV.F: "SMUD offers optional programs that allow customers to receive renewable energy for an additional charge, detailed on www.smud.org." The schedule states that an additional charge exists and prices it nowhere, pointing the reader off-document. A source gap: nothing in this corpus holds the amount. Kept rather than dropped because the sibling smud:tod-5-8pm, which shares this same five-sheet PDF, records it, and removing it here alone would desynchronize two filings with one source.
- Section I states this schedule's premises-type applicability and a compulsory-enrollment rule, and the EligibilityRule tests only hasCustomerKind = residentialKind. Verbatim: "This Rate Schedule R-TOD applies to single- and three-phase service for the following types of residential premises: 1. Individual or dual metered residences with digital communicating meter installed, including single-family homes, duplexes, apartments, and condominiums; and 2. General farm service where the meter also serves the residence or additional meters on a farm where the electricity consumed is solely for domestic purposes. 3. Customers who have an eligible renewable electrical generation facility under Rate Schedules NEM1 or SSR that was approved for installation by SMUD on or after January 1, 2018, or who establish service at a premises that has an electrical generation facility that is fueled by a renewable fuel source on or after January 1, 2018 must be on this Rate Schedule R-TOD." And: "Master-metered service to a qualifying multifamily accommodation or mobile home parks are not eligible for Time-of-Day rates under rate schedule R-TOD." The assessment records these as "left in prose"; they are a part of the source document that is not modeled.
- Section I.B.1 states this rate's own eligibility threshold and the shipped deferral misdescribes it. Verbatim: "The Time-of-Day (Low Use) Rate is an optional rate for residential customers that use a low amount energy. Customers must have a panel size less than or equal to 125 amps to be eligible for this rate. This optional rate will be available as of January 1, 2026." The shipped deferral reads "Specific kWh usage-cap eligibility threshold (modeled prose-only in EligibilityRule description)": the source states a panel-size limit in amps, not a kWh cap. Keep the deferral, correct its text.
- Section I.B.1 also contradicts the model's urpx:isDefault true. The ontology defines isDefault as "True if this version is the default rate plan version for new or unspecified enrollments"; Section I.A.1 says "The TOD (5-8 p.m.) Rate is the standard rate for SMUD's residential customers" and Section I.B.1 calls this rate optional. smud:tod-5-8pm also carries isDefault true, so the SMUD graph has two residential plans claiming the default.
